## Goal

Make gstack’s execution graph conform to Governed Autonomy: enforce authority and evidence completeness before introducing parallel execution. Every consequential action must be traceable to its governing policy, supporting evidence, and accountable human.

## Constraints and settled decisions

Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

- Preserve cross-tool review, pinned commits, bounded review rounds, and recursion refusal.
- Keep low-risk, authorized work autonomous. Human approval belongs at consequential boundaries.
- Successful machine review grants release only within Dorian's standing DR-113 authorization, and never for a `CODEOWNERS` path, which still needs his approving review.
- Classify individual actions within commands. Assess the effects of pushes and merges, including deployment triggers.
- Start with static workflows. No autonomous topology expansion.
- Keep `/gstack-verify` advisory. Incomplete verification must remain visible.
- Reuse existing governance and audit machinery where it meets the requirements.
- A nonce establishes freshness and linkage, not proof of substantive review.
- Limit the GA items to gstack and the shared declarations necessary to govern it.
- Other plugins in this repository carry their own objective sections and items.
- This repository has no Vercel project and no Playwright suite, so the Endform E2E gate does not apply to it.
- The agent's GitHub identity is the `moxywolf-agent` GitHub App (App ID 5007349), created by Dorian on 2026-09-20 and made installable on any account the same day. Its key and `github-app.env` live in the vault's `_Shared Knowledge/Agents and Plugins`. No machine user. It has one installation per account, resolved per repository at mint time, not pinned in configuration (GA-006). As of 2026-09-20 it is installed on MoxyWolfLLC, OpenControls-AI and GRCSchema.
- **The pre-merge gate is GitHub's, sized to the stakes** (Dorian, 2026-09-30, DR-113; amends the 2026-09-29 gate). `main`'s ruleset `main-gate` (24201147) requires a pull request, the `tests` check and code-owner review, with zero required approvals, and blocks force pushes and deletions. Only Repository admin can bypass, and the bot can't edit the ruleset. `CODEOWNERS` names Dorian only for the gate: `.github/`, every `hooks/` directory, the scorer's dependency files, what sets the agent's token and data permissions (`agent_token.py`, `governance.py`), what CI runs to gate `main` (`run_all_tests.py`, `goal_checks.py`, `goal_envelope.py`, `goal_guard.py`, `goal_successor.py` (GO-023 spec review C3: already in `CODEOWNERS` and `test_codeowners.py`)), the spend cap (`goal_spend.py`), and goal mode’s `goals/` folder and `goal-runs/README.md` (GO-001). Not owned: a goal's own `goal-runs/<id>/` (GO-011.2) and a successor folder `goals/<id>--s1/` or `--s2/` (GO-011.9; see *A goal delegates item selection*). The agent's own tooling (`peer_review.py`, `packet_coverage.mjs`, `repo_gates.py`, `version_bump.py`, `vocab_check.py`, `goal_brief.py`, `goal_run.py`, `goal_calls.py`) merges on a clean cross-vendor review like other code (amended 2026-10-05 at Dorian's instruction, after the first goal run on a product repository sent him an approval for every fix). A change touching a `CODEOWNERS` path needs his approving review at the head. A goal reaches `main` only through the owned CI checks, the holdout, his approving review of the goal's brief pull request (it adds `goals/<id>/`, a `CODEOWNERS` path; a GO-011.9 successor inherits its root goal's approval) and, when the lane is closed, his merge-instruction on the goal's pull request into `main` (GA-011). With the lane wide, that pull request merges as the bot on a clean whole-goal review with those checks green. `goal-envelope` fails a goal's pull request into `main` that touches a `CODEOWNERS` path (GO-004.1), so that pull request never does. Every other change merges with no human approval, except that closed-lane goal pull request and any change whose review coverage isn't `checked` (*Coverage is mandatory*). `.github/test_codeowners.py` fails if a gate path drops out of `CODEOWNERS` or a catch-all returns (GA-008). PR #94 (`a26d0d2`, `docs/evidence/gate-relaxed-2026-09-30.md`) verified the relaxed gate from the bot's side. The 2026-09-29 configuration and B-a's test of it are in `docs/evidence/boundary-a-2026-09-29.md`. Loosening it further is an amendment to this document.
- **One release path, checked by code.** A change's "head" is the pull request's reviewed head SHA, not the merge commit GitHub creates on the target branch; `record-release` reads it from the pull request. An item moves `planned` → `building` → `review` → `released` → `done`, and each move has one condition a script can check. `review` → `released` needs a clean cross-vendor review at the merged head (plus Dorian's approving review there when the change touches a `CODEOWNERS` path, which the ruleset enforces), then `record-release` succeeding at that head with a passing review at that exact head. The review must come before the merge: `record-release` refuses a merge that predates the release handoff ("merge predates the release handoff"), and a handoff needs a passing review, so an item marked "Merged unreviewed" can't become `released` by a review after the fact. A review of a later or different head never turns a merge into a release, because a review after the fact isn't permission given before it (Dorian, 2026-09-29). `released` → `done` needs the release record. A clean review whose coverage isn't `checked` passes only on the owner's exception (see *Coverage is mandatory*), and that item goes on to `released`. A merge with no clean review at its head is `merged_unreviewed`. It carries the owner's words on why, and it stays `merged_unreviewed`. `released` and `merged_unreviewed` aren't in the status vocabulary yet, and `vocab_check.py` refuses them. They join it with the first fix B-a produces. Until then, an item merged without review reads `review` with the words "Merged unreviewed" in its status line (as GA-005, XE-012, GA-007 and XE-015 do), and an item whose merge `record-release` has recorded moves `review` → `done` directly, its status line citing the review and the merge (as ME-001, SR-001 and ME-002 do). Recorded cases: GA-005 (PR #27) and XE-012 (PR #25) merged without review at their heads; XE-015 (PR #42), reviewed afterwards at the same head, and GA-007 were relabelled from `done` to `review` "Merged unreviewed" on 2026-09-29; PR #24's review state (XE-011, SM-002) was never established; mark-done PRs #71 and #73 merged with no review and no recorded exception.
- **The approval ledger is attention, not containment** (XE-002.6). Containment is `ONE_SHOT_ONLY` and the protected-branch ruleset. No item may claim the ledger stops anything.
- **Coverage is mandatory** (XE-014, amended 2026-09-29). A review whose coverage status isn't `checked` can't reach `released` without a recorded human exception. `not_run`, `unavailable`, `unusable` and `broken` are all visible blocks, not passes. This supersedes XE-010 criterion 5's non-blocking `unavailable`. A clean review whose coverage isn't `checked` counts as a passing review for `record-release` only when the owner's exception, in his words, is recorded at that head before the merge. The agent never merges on coverage that isn't `checked`, so the owner makes that merge.
- **Test the boundaries before adding item IDs** (Dorian, 2026-09-29; narrowed 2026-11-11, "Narrow it"). No new vocabulary, metrics or graph item is declared until the three boundary tests in *Boundary tests* below have run and their findings are recorded, and that work doesn't grow until it shows value against those tests. Any other new item needs no waiver. Vocabulary work is a new entry in `vocabulary.json`, metrics work is a new measurement report or score, and graph work is new task-graph or context-graph capability. A field an item adds to its own record is none of these. Amending an existing item to fix what a test finds is allowed. The status words `released` and `merged_unreviewed`, which *One release path* adds with B-a's first fix, are an allowed fix from that test, not barred growth (Dorian, 2026-11-11).
- **Transcript-derived data isn't readable outside the executing principal until the publish gate succeeds.** Regex redaction is best effort, not complete. Anything built from a session file stays in owner-only staging until gitleaks, run separately, finds nothing and the owner confirms the exact files and their audience. Binary content needs a per-file approval. Its manifest names the audience, the retention class and the deletion date, and deletion is a person's job, not an enforced one (SM-004). A Council replay case (CC-002) is the one exception to the date: its write-once manifest states the retention rule in place of a date, because its role isn't known when it's published, and the store's ledger records its role and the authoritative effective deletion date (Dorian C, 2026-11-17).
- **A goal delegates item selection, not authority** (Dorian, 2026-10-01). Under goal mode (eleventh objective) the agent picks and runs the next item while an approved goal allows it. This amends "next item only when asked" for goal runs only; outside a goal, that rule stands. A goal's folder and the scripts that enforce it (`goal_guard.py`, `goal_checks.py`, `goal_envelope.py`, `goal_spend.py`; not `goal_run.py`, `goal_brief.py` or `goal_calls.py`) are `CODEOWNERS` paths, and model agreement never stands in for his approval of them. The one exception is GO-011.9's successor folder (`goals/<root>--s1/`, `--s2/`). It is unowned, `goal_successor.py` (owned) holds it inside the required `tests` check to the root goal's brief byte for byte except Scenarios, Goal tests and Pre-mortem, plus one added `Succeeds` paragraph per successor, and the root goal's approving review, re-read from GitHub, stands in for its own.
- **The agent merges on a clean review, not on words** (Dorian, 2026-09-30: “Yes, and this gives my explicit, documented authorization”; DR-113). It merges as `moxywolf-agent[bot]`, through the pull request, pinned to the head SHA, once a review by the other vendor's model is clean at that head with coverage `checked` and `tests` is green. It doesn't wait for him to say merge, except for a goal's pull request into `main` while the lane is closed, which waits for his merge-instruction (GA-011). The ruleset still holds any change to a `CODEOWNERS` path until his approving review exists at the head. Three blocking rounds escalate to him. A merge with no clean review at its head stays `merged_unreviewed`. The review requirement outside `CODEOWNERS` is the agent's process (`record-release`), not GitHub's, so a defect there could skip it; moving the verdict into a check GitHub requires is a later item. This supersedes the two-signal rule of 2026-09-29 (XE-029 criterion 6, DR-111) and the bot-comment instruction of GA-005.

## Items and acceptance criteria

### GA-001 — Reconcile authority and enforce evidence completeness

**Status:** review. Implementation prepared; independent review and human merge pending.

1. Build, ship, peer-review, and governance documentation express one consistent release policy. Routine branch work remains authorized; consequential release requires recorded human authorization.
2. Review success requires coverage of every expected acceptance criterion. Missing, duplicate, unknown, malformed, or unmet criteria cannot produce a passing outcome.
3. Fix verification accounts for every previous blocker. Omission cannot silently resolve a finding; deferral requires the applicable authorization.
4. Evidence and release authorization identify the exact reviewed revision and action. Changed code invalidates an earlier release authorization.
5. Tests demonstrate that unauthorized release is refused through the execution entry point, rather than only through a helper.
6. Applicable command tiers and the named Release Owner are recorded. The agent cannot manufacture the human’s approval.

### GA-002 — Declare and execute static topology

**Status:** review; implemented on the GA-001 feature branch foundation; human merge pending.

1. Introduce machine-readable workflows for CSO and verify first.
2. The executor consumes the declarations; they are not parallel documentation.
3. Each node declares dependencies, inputs, outputs, permitted effects, and failure behavior.
4. Cycles, missing dependencies, conflicting writers, and missing required results prevent successful completion.
5. Persist node attempts and evidence references. Resume preserves valid work and invalidates descendants when their inputs change.

### GA-003 — Optimize independent branches

**Status:** review; depends on GA-002; human merge pending.

1. CSO prepares scope and architecture before parallel audit branches; independent verification precedes one owned report.
2. Verify freezes claims and code before parallel checks; convergence verifies coverage and detects extra implementation.
3. Multi-repository reviews split only where acceptance criteria are independent; coupled changes retain integration review.
4. Enforce a concurrency cap. Shared-state proof commands remain serialized unless isolated.
5. Demonstrate overlapping execution without lost findings, conflicting writes, or false success after a worker fails.

### GA-004 — Complete governance records and data boundaries

**Status:** review; builds on GA-001 and GA-002; human merge pending.

1. Record approvals, stops, overrides, edits, and execution outcomes with owner, action, revision, and evidence references.
2. Reuse the shared gate-log where possible; report machine-review outcomes separately from human oversight.
3. Enforce declared data-use permissions before external reviewer dispatch and release checks before output leaves its permitted scope.
4. Report oversight timing and override rates as investigation signals, never automatic judgments.
5. Prevent resumed runs from replaying consequential actions already completed.

### GA-005 — The agent acts as itself, and its merges say so

**Status:** review. Merged unreviewed as `ce7a5e6` (PR #27) on 2026-09-20 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian's instruction on the pull request (no reviewer available; Dorian chose not to wait); no release record. Criteria 1 to 7, 10 and 11 are met (7 checked live: the bot's merge refused with 405 before the bypass, its direct write to `main` refused with 409 after). Criterion 8 waits on Dorian's answers per merge, and criterion 9 on the OpenControls-AI decision and the PAT leaving every connected folder; the item stays `review` until the file is gone, per criterion 9. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** commit-to-actor (a commit, pull request or merge names a GitHub login, and every reader, `record-release` included, assumes that login acted; under Dorian's login an agent action named the wrong actor). Token-to-installation (a token is minted from the app key for one command and lasts an hour; nothing assumes it outlives that). Merge-to-instruction (an agent merge names the instruction it acted on, and a reader assumes that instruction covered this pull request). Backfill-to-confirmation (each past merge this item marks as agent-executed names its evidence and Dorian's answer; nothing is marked from a commit subject alone).

1. `scripts/agent_token.py exec -- <command>` mints an installation token from `github-app.env` and the key file it names, both read from the vault's `_Shared Knowledge/Agents and Plugins`, and runs the command with the token supplied through `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`/`GIT_CONFIG_KEY_n`/`GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_n` or an environment variable. The token never appears in argv, stdout, stderr or a file. Test: a fixture key and a stubbed token endpoint; the command's argv, the script's output and the temp directory are searched for the token and it's found in none of them.
2. Minting runs where GitHub's `/app` endpoints answer. The cloud proxy refuses them (403, checked 2026-09-20) and the device shell reaches them, so pushes and pull requests run from the device shell. When minting fails, the script exits non-zero and names the endpoint and the status. It never falls back to another credential, and a test asserts no code path in `gstack-execution` reads `github-pat.env`.
3. Commits pushed and pull requests opened by the loop name `moxywolf-agent[bot]`. Check: the first pull request the loop opens after this merges shows `user.login` = `moxywolf-agent[bot]` through the API.
4. Every instruction that tells the agent to push or open pull requests with the vault PAT says the app instead. Repo homes: `gstack-execution/commands/gstack-build.md`, `commands/gstack-design-doc.md`, `GOVERNANCE.md` (the credential table), `scripts/docs.md`, `scripts/repo_gates.py`, `scripts/review_host.sh`, `README.md`, `project-init/skills/session-start/SKILL.md` and `project-init/skills/session-end/SKILL.md`. Changelogs are exempt because they record what was, and a test fixture that names the file is not an instruction. A test greps those files for push instructions naming the PAT and reports how many files it examined; zero examined is a failure, per EV-001. Vault and Taskade homes are changed by hand and the grep output goes in the review packet: `DR-011-github-pat-vault-file.md`, the team-shared INDEX commit rule, and this project's `cowork-project-instructions.md`.
5. `record-release` tells the kinds of merge apart. A merge by the named human is `human_merge_recorded`. A merge by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` with Dorian's instruction is `agent_merge_on_instruction`, with the instruction bound by criterion 12 (amended 2026-09-29), and the record carries the instruction's text, its time and the comment URL. A bot merge with no recorded instruction is refused as `release_blocked` and named as an unrequested agent merge (for `moxywolf-agent[bot]` under DR-113, see GA-009.1). `agent_merge_on_instruction` is a new vocabulary term, so the vocabulary goes to 1.1.0, which is an XE-012 break point. Tests cover all three. It reads the merge record with the app token over REST when `gh` is absent, since the device shell has no `gh` (checked 2026-09-20).
6. Superseded by GA-005.12 and GA-005.13.
7. Superseded by the `main-gate` constraint (2026-09-29): the app comes off the `main` ruleset's bypass list rather than onto it.
8. Merges to `main` that the agent executed are listed in this item with merge commit, evidence and Dorian's answer. The draft list comes from merge subjects: #6, #7, #8, #11, #12, #22, #23 and #24 carry hand-written subjects ("Merge PR #N:" or "Merge pull request #N:" with no "from"), and #4, #5 and #9 carry GitHub's button format. A subject is a hint, not proof. Each row is `agent`, `human` or `unknown` as Dorian answers it, and `unknown` stays unknown. No release record or merge commit is rewritten.
9. The PAT leaves the agent's reach: Dorian moves `github-pat.env` out of every connected folder. It also pushes to OpenControls-AI repos today, and the app is installed on MoxyWolfLLC only. So before it moves, Dorian decides the OpenControls-AI path: switch the app to installable on any account and install it there, or create a second app. Until the file is gone, this item's status is `review`, not `done`, and says why. Check: the vault folder listing shows no `github-pat.env`.
10. After merge, the memory graph's node 14 is marked closed with the date and this item's merge commit, and the swimlane's merge step shows two paths: Dorian merges, or the agent merges as the bot on his recorded instruction.
11. `gstack-execution` and `project-init` each get a minor version bump with a changelog line.
12. The instruction is bound to the head by GitHub (amended 2026-09-29). `record-release` reads the pull request's reviews and records `agent_merge_on_instruction` only when an `APPROVED` review by the Release Owner's login has a `commit_id` equal to the merged head. Otherwise the bot merge is refused as `release_blocked`, whatever comment exists. A test covers an approval at an earlier head, an approval by another login, and no approval.
13. A comment posting Dorian's chat words on the pull request (verbatim, the time, and the pull requests the agent read them as covering) stays as a courtesy record only; nothing reads it as evidence. `gstack-build.md` Step 6 says to wait for his GitHub approval before merging, in one place (amended 2026-09-29).

### GA-007 — The credential can say what it actually grants

**Status:** review. Merged unreviewed (relabelled from `done` 2026-09-29 on Dorian's ruling; his earlier done ruling is the recorded exception): `3c79c86` (PR #39, head `571574b`) on 22 September 2026 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on his instruction, version `2014ab5` (PR #40); reviewed afterwards at that head, `20260925-084234-571574b-9yqea0s4` (codex/gpt-6-astra, `no_blocking_findings`, 5/5, CI run 35690572187); no release record. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** none. It reads the access-token response the app already receives and prints a field `mint()` was discarding.

1. `agent_token.py permissions [--repo owner/name]` prints the permissions the resolved installation actually grants, and its `repository_selection`. Repository resolution is GA-006's, unchanged.
2. It reports the installation id it read, and whether that id came from `--repo` or from `github-app.env`, because GA-006 made those two different answers and a reader comparing output across repositories needs to know which one they are looking at.
3. The subcommand is reachable. `--selftest` asserts the usage text offers it and the function exists, because a subcommand nobody can dispatch to is the same defect as a gate nobody runs.
4. Step 4 of the build loop names the permission case alongside the installation case it already covers, and names this command as what tells the two states apart.
5. Evidence: the command run against two installations of the same app in the same minute, one that has accepted a permission request and one that has not, showing the difference the refusal message hides.

### GA-006 — A token is minted for the installation that owns the repository

**Status:** review. Merged unreviewed (relabelled from `done` 2026-09-29 on Dorian's ruling): `27fd672` (PR #31, head `29dff5c`) on 21 September 2026 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on his instruction, recorded as `agent_merge_on_instruction`; review `20260921-142113-29dff5c-xs3jf4jy` (gemini/gemini-3.1-pro-preview, `no_blocking_findings`, 9/9) with `tests` green; handoff `awaiting_human_release` at 2026-09-21T21:22:56Z predates the merge, but no release record, because the review's `repos[].path` was the cloud container's, where `api.github.com` answers 403. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

A review whose slow step runs elsewhere is still opened with the packet pointing at the host that holds the credential, so the record and the release land together.

**Links introduced:** repository-to-installation. A token's scope is derived from the repository at mint time, not from a constant in a file, so the token names where it came from, and a repository the app cannot reach becomes a named refusal rather than a 404 the caller has to interpret.

1. **The installation is resolved from the repository.** With a repository in hand the script asks `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/installation` with the app JWT and mints against the id that comes back. The id in `github-app.env` is a fallback used only when no repository can be determined, and it is never preferred over a resolved one.
2. **The repository is found where the caller already says it.** `--repo owner/name` when given; otherwise the `{owner}/{repo}` in an `api` path that begins `repos/`; otherwise `origin`'s URL in the working directory for `exec`. When none of the three answers, the script says which it tried before falling back.
3. **A wrong-installation failure never reads as a missing repository.** When a resolve fails, the script says the app is not installed on that owner, names the owner and the accounts it is installed on, and exits non-zero. It does not retry with the fallback id, because that token produces the same 404 and a second identical failure reads as a flaky network.
4. **One lookup per invocation, and nothing cached.** The resolve costs one request against the JWT the script already mints. Nothing writes the mapping to disk, because an installation can be removed between runs and a cached id would fail as a 404 long after the cause.
5. **The constraint stops naming one installation.** This document's app-identity constraint names the app and its key, not a single installation id, and says the installation is resolved per repository.
6. **Evidence.** `scripts/test_agent_token.py`: a stubbed `/repos/{owner}/{repo}/installation` returns an id different from the env's and the mint is asserted to use the resolved one; a resolve that 404s produces the not-installed message naming the owner and does not fall back; the `api` path parser and the `origin` URL parser each cover ssh and https remotes; no path writes an id to disk. The suite reports how many checks it examined and fails when it examines none, per EV-001. `scripts/run_all_tests.py` runs it.

### GA-008 — Dorian reviews the gate, not every change

**Status:** review. Merged `1c4a046` (PR #95) on 30 September 2026 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian's "approved", review `20260930-183444-985cedb-ixaz_fen` (codex/gpt-6-astra, `fixes_verified`, 2 rounds, coverage `covered`) at `8563184`, CI green; no release record (merge predates the release handoff). Declared 2026-09-30 ("let's go") from his proposal (Taskade `06 – Engineering/risk-tiered-merge-proposal-2026-09-30.md`, DR-113). He chose the gate floor and the size axis; this item builds the floor, and the size axis needs nothing here. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** path-to-owner. A reader of `CODEOWNERS` assumes a listed path can't reach `main` without Dorian's approval. That holds only while `main-gate` requires code-owner review, which criterion 3 checks live.

1. `.github/CODEOWNERS` names Dorian for the gate paths in *Constraints* and for nothing else. No catch-all.
2. `.github/test_codeowners.py` runs in CI and fails when a gate path is unowned, when a gate path no longer exists, when any file under a `hooks/` directory is unowned, when a non-gate path (`README.md`, `DESIGN.md`) is owned, or when a catch-all returns. It reports how many paths it examined, and examining none fails. Its selftest checks the matcher on anchored files, `dir/`, and a one-segment `*` that must not match two segments.

**After merge, not a criterion (boundary test B-d).** With code-owner review on in `main-gate` and zero required approvals, a bot pull request touching only `docs/evidence/` merges with no approval, and one touching a file in `.github/` is refused until his approving review exists. **Result, 2026-09-30: pass.** PR #96 (a one-line change to `.github/CODEOWNERS`) was refused with "Waiting on code owner review from dorianatmoxywolf" and closed; PR #97 (docs only) merged as `cb37521` with no approval (`docs/evidence/boundary-d-2026-09-30.md`).

### GA-009 — An agent merge under DR-113 is recorded, not refused

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` in `20ab385` (PR #98, head `0c45d05`) on 1 October 2026 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian's "merge #98", recorded by `record-release` as `agent_merge_on_instruction`, review `20260930-191805-aa96129-ho5n0hlz`. Declared 2026-09-30 ("fix everything and merge to production").

**Links introduced:** merge-to-authority. An `agent_merge_autonomous` record names DR-113 as its authority, and a reader assumes that decision covered this merge. It holds while DR-113 stands; a change to the decision is an amendment here.

1. `record-release` records a merge by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` with no matching instruction on the pull request as `agent_merge_autonomous`, with `authorized_by` (the packet's Release Owner) and `authority` naming DR-113, and no `instruction` field.
2. A merge by any other bot with no matching instruction is still refused as an unrequested agent merge. A merge with a matching instruction is still recorded as `agent_merge_on_instruction`.
3. Nothing else loosens: an autonomous merge still needs a passing review at the exact head and a handoff written before the merge, and one before the handoff is refused.
4. `release`'s message and instruction, `/gstack-build`, `/gstack-ship`, `/gstack-peer-review`, the peer-review contract, `GOVERNANCE.md` and the skill's `SKILL.md` describe the agent merging under DR-113, with a `CODEOWNERS` path held for the owner's approval. The `awaiting_human_release` outcome name is unchanged, because tests and records key on it.
5. `test_governed_review.py` covers criteria 1 to 3, and each new case is shown to fail alone against a copy with its fix removed.
6. `gstack-execution` moves a minor version in `plugin.json` and its `marketplace.json` entry, and the top-level marketplace version moves (CI-002).
7. `agent_merge_autonomous` joins the vocabulary as a review outcome, so `test_vocabulary.py` finds every emitted outcome there, and the vocabulary moves to 1.5.0.

### GA-010 — A release lane is decided by what the change touches, and the closed lane never opens on its own

**Status:** done. Design approved by Dorian 2026-10-13 ("approve #211"; PR #211, merged `6959afa`). Review `20261013-211301-b7fe081-dgrlpwpv` (codex, gpt-6-astra) at `b7fe081`: `no_blocking_findings`, 9/9, after earlier reviews `20261013-202943-cf2b7cf-_jzakv1i` and `20261013-204622-bf266e3-ww3ceh1l`; PR #213 merged `f50cab8` under DR-113, recorded `agent_merge_autonomous`, lane `wide`. gstack-execution 0.87.0, marketplace 1.133.0. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** `release_lane` on `state.json`, the release handoff and the release record: `{lane, decided_by, matched}`, where `lane` is `contained`, `wide` or `closed`, `decided_by` is `path` or `effect`, and `matched` lists the paths or effects that decided it. The reader assumes the lane was decided from the change at the reviewed head, by code, before any model read the diff. That holds because `open` computes it from `git diff --name-status <base>..<head>` against a declared table and no later step may change it; a round records it on `SURFACE.md` line 2 and the reviewer cannot write it.

Premise (Loops and Graphs post behind XE-036, "open the gate on blast radius, not on confidence"): the hard-to-reverse lane is a lane that does not open, not a high threshold, because thresholds get adjusted and closed lanes do not.

1. `plugins/gstack-execution/skills/gstack-execution/references/release-lanes.json` declares the default table: for each lane, path globs and effects. `closed` names migrations (`**/migrations/**`, `**/supabase/migrations/**`, `*.sql` under them), payment and billing paths (`**/payment*/**`, `**/billing*/**`, `**/stripe*/**`), credentials and the agent's identity (`**/agent_token.py`, `**/*.env`, `**/.github/workflows/**`, `**/rulesets/**`, `CODEOWNERS`), and the effect `deletes_tracked_file`. `wide` names shared code (`**/scripts/**`, `**/lib/**`, `**/packages/**`, `**/schema*/**`, any file with more than `WIDE_CALLERS` (5) callers in the review surface) and the effect `renames_tracked_file`. Everything else is `contained`. A repository may ship its own `.gstack/release-lanes.json`, which replaces the default table whole, never merges with it, so a reader finds one table.
2. `open` computes the lane from `git diff --name-status <base>..<head>` for every repository in the packet: the most restrictive lane any path or effect matches wins (`closed` over `wide` over `contained`). It records `release_lane` in `state.json` and on `SURFACE.md` line 2, and prints it. A packet whose diff cannot be read is refused as `malformed_packet`, never defaulted to `contained`.
3. `round` carries the lane into the round record unchanged. A reviewer finding or declaration never moves it.
4. `release` carries the lane into the handoff. For `closed`, the handoff's instruction says DR-113 does not apply and names the matched paths or effects; the outcome stays `awaiting_human_release`. For `wide`, the existing gates apply (ledger clear, coverage checked, links verified, no open unknowns needing `repository`) and the handoff says so. For `contained`, as today.
5. `record-release` records an agent merge with no instruction on a `closed`-lane review as `agent_merge_out_of_lane`, naming the lane and what matched, with `authority` absent; the record is written, never refused, because a merge that happened is a fact to keep. An agent merge on a closed-lane review with a matching instruction is `agent_merge_on_instruction` as today, and the record carries the lane.
6. `/gstack-build` Step 6 and `/gstack-ship` read the lane from the handoff and stop before merging when it is `closed`, posting the handoff for the Release Owner. `GOVERNANCE.md` describes the three lanes beside DR-113 and says the closed lane is a lane, not a threshold.
7. `vocabulary.json` gains `release_lane` as a state field, `contained`, `wide` and `closed` as terms, and `agent_merge_out_of_lane` as a review outcome; `vocab_check.py` resolves them.
8. `test_governed_review.py` gains cases: a diff touching a migration path is `closed`; one deleting a tracked file is `closed` by effect; one changing a shared script is `wide`; one changing only a README is `contained`; the most restrictive wins across repositories; a repository's own table replaces the default; `release` on a closed-lane review writes the DR-113-does-not-apply instruction; `record-release` on an autonomous agent merge of a closed-lane review records `agent_merge_out_of_lane`; a reviewer output carrying a `release_lane` key is ignored for the lane.
9. `plugins/gstack-execution/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` moves a minor version and the top-level marketplace version moves (CI-002). In CI, `run_all_tests.py` reports a nonzero count of what it examined, names `test_governed_review.py` among the suites it ran, and reports no failures.

### GA-011 — Into a goal branch the closed lane is deferred to the goal pull request into main

**Status:** done. PR #277 (branch `build/GA-011-goal-branch-lane`), review `20261102-105211-bb07c3f-9dxl5nid` (codex) `fixes_verified`, 32/32, at `f2be1a2` (after review `20261102-095221-2b1bda4-is7ze86r` before a sync with main); handoff lane `wide`, `lane_in_force` `wide`; Dorian approved #277 at `f2be1a2` (CODEOWNERS paths `goal_guard.py`, `goal_checks.py`) and instructed the merge ("PR #277 is approved. Merge #277"); merged `f4d34b3`, recorded `agent_merge_on_instruction`. gstack-execution 0.98.0, marketplace 1.158.0. Not yet exercised in a live goal. Approved by Dorian 2026-11-02 ("I approve GA-011"), from the wfa-review-screen-v5 goal, where migration 0061 closed the lane into `goal/wfa-review-screen-v5` on every item. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** `lane_in_force` on the release handoff and the release record: the lane `release` and `record-release` hold the merge to, from `release_lane` and the target. The reader assumes a migration still cannot reach main without the Release Owner. That holds because the goal pull request into main is reviewed over the whole goal's diff (`final_review_scope` requires the review's base to be where main and the goal head meet), so its `release_lane` is `closed` whenever any item's was, and `goal_guard.final_merge_allowed` refuses it without the Release Owner's merge-instruction.

1. `peer_review.py` gains `lane_in_force(release_lane, target)`: the lane's own value, except a `closed` lane into a target starting `goal/`, which is in force as `wide`. It is the one home of the rule; `release_lane` itself never moves. (Extended by GA-012.1.)
2. `release` checks the wide gates when the lane in force is `wide` (so a deferred closed lane still needs the ledger clear, coverage checked, links verified and no open unknown needing the repository), records `lane_in_force`, and for a deferred lane writes an instruction that names what closed it, says it is deferred to the goal pull request into main, and says the agent merges with the wide gates.
3. `record-release` records an agent merge as `agent_merge_out_of_lane` only when the lane in force is `closed`; a deferred lane's agent merge is `agent_merge_autonomous`, and every record carries `lane_in_force`.
4. `goal_guard.final_merge_allowed` takes the pull request's comments and refuses a whole-goal review whose lane is `closed` unless a merge-instruction posted by the agent, quoting the review's Release Owner and covering this pull request, is on it. Unreadable comments are a refusal. `goal_run.py may` and `resume` read the comments from GitHub for a merge into main.
5. `/gstack-build` Step 6 and `/gstack-ship` read `lane_in_force`; `/gstack-goal` finish step 3 and local step 4 say a closed goal lane needs Dorian's merge-instruction, asked once for that pull request; `GOVERNANCE.md` and the peer-review contract describe the deferral.
6. `vocabulary.json` 1.12.0 adds `lane_in_force` as a state field.
7. Tests: a closed lane released into `goal/g1` is in force as `wide` and its instruction names the deferral; the same release refuses when a wide gate fails; a closed lane released into main is still `closed`; an agent merge of a closed lane into `goal/g1` is `agent_merge_autonomous` carrying `release_lane.lane` `closed`; `closed_lane_instructed` passes an open lane, refuses a closed one without an instruction (naming what closed it), with unreadable comments, or with an instruction for another pull request, from another owner or posted by a person, and passes the owner's instruction posted by the agent; `final_merge_allowed` carries the refusal.
8. `plugin.json` 0.98.0, its marketplace entry and README Version History, and the top-level marketplace version 1.158.0 (CI-002).

Not covered: a branch named `goal/<id>` with no goal run behind it gets the same deferral. Its pull request into main is still closed by GA-010 and stopped by `/gstack-build`, so the closed lane holds; it is noted, not gated.

### GA-012 — A coverage exception hands the merge to the Release Owner in every lane

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` in `1d7fbbf` (PR #349, head `2afbe0f`) on 17 November 2026 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]`, recorded by `record-release` as `agent_merge_autonomous` (DR-113, wide lane). Review `20261117-164556-b59197b-ucj7mi5y` (codex/gpt-6-astra, `fixes_verified`, 2 rounds), with the `tests` workflow green at the head (run 44211059454). Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** the release handoff's `coverage.exception` (the Release Owner's words and the time they were recorded) becomes an input to `lane_in_force`. The reader assumes a handoff carrying an exception was released by the Release Owner's merge, not the agent's. That holds because `release` and `record-release` both compute the lane in force from the handoff through `lane_in_force`, its one home, and a handoff with an exception is in force as `closed`.

Found 2026-11-17: PR #348 (review `20261117-161352-672ae0d-y_kvlduo`, clean) was refused in the wide lane, Dorian merged it himself as `499d4ba`, and its release record is a pull request comment.

1. `lane_in_force(release_lane, target, coverage_exception)` returns `closed` when `coverage_exception` holds words, whatever `release_lane` and `target` say. An exception is recorded only when coverage isn't `checked` (as `coverage_line` does today), so an exception passed with checked coverage isn't recorded and changes nothing. Otherwise it returns what it returns today. `release_lane` itself never moves.
2. `release` with `--coverage-exception` and coverage not `checked` writes the handoff with `lane_in_force` `closed` and the exception's words and time. When `release_lane` is `wide`, its other gates still hold: an open failure in the ledger, links that aren't verified, or an open unknown needing the repository still refuses, naming each. Coverage is the only wide gate the exception answers.
3. That handoff's instruction says coverage wasn't checked, quotes the exception, says DR-113 doesn't apply and the agent doesn't merge, and says the Release Owner merges under their own login. *Coverage is mandatory* has the owner make that merge, so a merge-instruction doesn't open it to the agent.
4. `record-release` reads the exception from the handoff and computes the lane in force through `lane_in_force`. The Release Owner's merge is `human_merge_recorded`. Any agent merge of an exception handoff, with or without a merge-instruction, is `agent_merge_out_of_lane`. No outcome is added to the vocabulary. The record carries the handoff's `coverage` object as `release` wrote it, so the exception appears exactly when one was recorded.
5. Without `--coverage-exception`, every lane behaves as it does today, and the wide lane still refuses on "coverage was not checked".
6. `/gstack-build` Step 6 and `GOVERNANCE.md` each gain one sentence saying a coverage exception closes the lane for that release and the owner merges.
7. Tests in `test_governed_review.py`: a wide-lane release with an exception writes a handoff in force as `closed` that carries the words; the same release with an open ledger failure refuses and names it; a contained-lane release with an exception is in force as `closed`; a closed lane deferred into `goal/g1` with an exception is in force as `closed`; a wide-lane release without an exception refuses as today; `record-release` records the owner's merge of an exception handoff as `human_merge_recorded` and an agent merge of one as `agent_merge_out_of_lane`, with a merge-instruction posted and without one.
8. `gstack-execution` moves 0.104.1 to 0.105.0 in `plugin.json`, its marketplace entry and its README Version History, and the top-level marketplace version moves (CI-002). In CI, `run_all_tests.py` names `test_governed_review.py` among what it ran and reports no failures.

### XE-040 — A check that was wrong is retired on the record, with the Release Owner's words

**Status:** done. Shipped with GA-011 in PR #277, merged `f4d34b3` (evidence in GA-011's status). Declared 2026-11-02 from wfa-review-screen-v5, where goal tests run before the review screen existed opened ledger failures no honest run could close; approved by Dorian the same day ("I approve fixes 2 to 6 as written"). Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** a `kind: retire` line on the branch's ledger naming the run it retires (`retires`), the reason and the Release Owner's words (`approval`). The reader assumes a retired check was wrong and Dorian said so. That holds because `retire` refuses without both and only for a failure whose issue is open, and the line keeps his words verbatim.

1. `check_ledger.py retire --repo <path> --run <id> --reason "<why>" --approval "<words>"` appends a `kind: retire` line that closes the open issue run `<id>` belongs to: its identity, the run it retires, the head, the reason, the Release Owner's words verbatim and the time.
2. It is refused without a reason or words, for a run that isn't a failure on this branch's ledger, and for an issue already closed. A later failure of the same identity opens a new issue as usual.
3. `open-issues` lists retired checks after the open ones; `find_run` never returns a retirement as a run.
4. `/gstack-build` Step 3 says when to retire (a check that was wrong, not red) and that it needs Dorian's words.
5. Tests: retire closes the issue, is listed with reason and words, and a later failure reopens it; each refusal writes nothing.

### GO-013 — A malformed `may` call is a usage error, not an escalation

**Status:** done. Shipped with GA-011 in PR #277, merged `f4d34b3` (evidence in GA-011's status). Declared and approved 2026-11-02 with XE-040. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** none. Exit 2 from `may` means the call's shape was wrong; the run state is unchanged, which the test checks byte for byte.

1. `goal_run.py may` checks the call's shape before anything is read or written: the class is one of `governance.GRANT_CLASSES`; `vcs.push` and `merge` take a valid branch name; `pr.open` takes `<base><-<head>`; `net.connect` a host or URL; others a name without whitespace; `merge` also names `--head <sha>` and `--pr <n>`, and only `merge` takes `--head`, `--review`, `--pr` or `--sync`.
2. A malformed call exits 2 with the form expected and changes nothing in the run, so no escalation reaches Dorian.
3. `/gstack-goal` step 4 documents exit 2.
4. Tests: five malformed calls through `main` each exit 2 with `usage:` and leave the run state byte-identical.

### GO-014 — Code isn't a hostname or an environment variable (amends GO-005)

**Status:** done. Shipped with GA-011 in PR #277, merged `f4d34b3` (evidence in GA-011's status). Declared and approved 2026-11-02 with XE-040, from wfa-review-screen-v5. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** none. The `external` type and its reasons keep their meaning; fewer code tokens reach them.

1. In a code file a host-like key (`host`, `url`, …) counts only when its value is a quoted string, so `const host = request.headers.get(...)` is no hostname; a config file keeps the old rule.
2. `${X}` counts as a name only before `}` or a shell operator (`:`, `-`, `=`, `+`, `?`, `#`, `%`, `/`), so `${Math.min(a, b)}` is code.
3. Tests in `test_goal_calls.py::CodeIsNotAHost` cover both, and a quoted host and a config host still count.

### GO-015 — A holdout that only missed a package says so (amends GO-003)

**Status:** done. Shipped with GA-011 in PR #277, merged `f4d34b3` (evidence in GA-011's status). Declared and approved 2026-11-02 with XE-040. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** none. The goal-holdout check's title and the `holdout` escalation's text change wording only; the conclusion and the run's outcome don't.

1. `goal_brief.holdout_cause` reads the failures `holdout_failures` reported; when every one is a missing module (`No module named`, `Cannot find module`), `holdout_reading` gives "missing package <names> (an environment problem, not evidence of a reward hack)". One real failure beside them keeps "possible reward hack".
2. The goal-holdout check's title and `goal_run.py holdout-failed`'s escalation both use `holdout_reading`.
3. The check stays red and the run stops either way.
4. Tests: a missing-package holdout is titled so and stays a failure; a mixed one keeps "possible reward hack"; the escalation says "missing package" and the run ends stopped.

### XE-041 — An installed scorer installs its own dependency (amends XE-014)

**Status:** done. Shipped with GA-011 in PR #277, merged `f4d34b3` (evidence in GA-011's status). Declared and approved 2026-11-02 with XE-040. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** `coverage.consulted` names the scorer cache it used or tried. The reader assumes a `checked` report was scored with the lockfile's `ai`. That holds because the cache is keyed by the lockfile's sha256.

1. When `import('ai')` fails, `packet_coverage.mjs` runs `npm ci --omit=dev` once from the plugin's own `package.json` and lockfile into `${GSTACK_HOME:-~/.gstack}/scorer/<lockfile sha256, 16 hex>/`, in a temporary directory renamed into place, and loads `ai` from there.
2. A failed install records `broken`, naming npm's error and the cache it consulted, and leaves nothing half-installed; with no lockfile it records `broken` as before.
3. Tests: an installed copy with no `node_modules` scores and reuses its cache on a second run; an install against an unreachable registry is `broken`.

### XE-042 — The wide link gate takes a finding citing a CI run record the dispatcher wrote (amends GO-016.2)

**Status:** building

**Why.** Review `20261104-142043-5d5739c-ms_slbcz` (#283) ended `fixes_verified` with every criterion met, and `release` refused it `release_blocked` because a follow-up finding cited `evidence/ci-41886878299.json`, the run record the dispatcher fetched from GitHub and wrote that round. GO-016.2 exempts a CI log or summary the dispatcher wrote, which isn't bound to repository content and can't re-resolve either way; the run record is the same kind of file. Dorian 2026-11-04: “Fix the gate (Recommended)”.

**Links introduced:** none. `ci_shown` reads round records that already name each fetched run.

1. `ci_shown(d)` also returns `evidence/ci-<run id>.json` for each `ci_runs` entry a round of review `d` recorded as read. A run the round did not read adds nothing.
2. Nothing else in `wide_links_hold` changes. The dispatcher writes a run's record whether or not it could read the run; only a read run's record is exempt. A stale link, a missing record, any other cited `evidence/ci-*` file (one that isn't a log or summary a round recorded writing, or the record of a run a round read), or any other unverifiable check still fails it.
3. Test: `test_the_wide_link_gate_takes_ci_log_findings_and_nothing_else_unverifiable` asserts a finding citing a read run's record passes the gate, and one citing a run no round read does not.
4. `plugin.json` moves a patch version, with its marketplace entry, the README Version History and the top-level marketplace version (CI-002).

### XE-043 — The wide link gate takes a finding citing a criterion-directory bundle the dispatcher wrote (amends GO-016.2)

**Status:** building

**Why.** Reviews `20261110-050049-8a6ffc8-ectl0its` and `20261110-054114-8a6ffc8-uz9o_mjc` (workforce-automation #211, onet-capabilities-2 item 1) both passed with every criterion met, and `release` refused both `release_blocked` because follow-up findings cited `dirs/0-c2-i1/0.txt`, the bundle XE-033 writes when a criterion names a directory. Like a CI log (GO-016.2) or a run record (XE-042), the bundle is a file the dispatcher wrote; it isn't a repository path and can't re-resolve either way. Dorian 2026-11-10: “Fix the review tooling first”.

**Links introduced:** none. The round record already names its repositories and each directory it bundled (`surface.criterion_dirs`).

1. The wide link gate also takes a finding citing `dirs/<i>-<repo>/<n>.txt` when a round of the review recorded writing that bundle: `<i>` and `<repo>` are the index and directory name of one of the round's repositories, and `<n>` is below the number of directories that round bundled.
2. Nothing else in the gate changes. A stale link, a missing record, a `dirs/` path no round recorded writing, or any other unverifiable check still fails it. The release record still reports `links_unverifiable` with the count.
3. Test: `test_the_wide_link_gate_takes_ci_log_findings_and_nothing_else_unverifiable` also asserts that a finding citing a bundle a round wrote passes, one citing a bundle no round wrote does not, and a bundle finding beside another unverifiable check does not.
4. `plugin.json` moves a patch version, with its marketplace entry, the README Version History and the top-level marketplace version (CI-002).

Not in this item: binding a bundle citation back to the real file and line. Add it when a blocking finding needs to cite a bundle.

**Why.** prfaq PIPE-002 (PR #35) passed review `20261115-204116-4bbebe7-ae40kqn5` with no blocking findings, and `release` refused it in the wide lane (`coverage was not checked`): `packet_coverage.mjs` answered "declares no criteria for: PIPE-002" because its id prefix is one to three capitals, and prfaq's PIPE-, LAB- and TEAM- ids have four. XE-037 drew the line at three by choice, not by need. Approved by Dorian 2026-11-15 ("Approve both").

**Links introduced:** none. The item id in a coverage report keeps the form the design file uses.

1. `declaredCriteria()` accepts an item heading whose id prefix is one to four capital letters (`I-087`, `XE-010`, `CSO-001`, `PIPE-002`); the title separator and the criteria grammar are unchanged.
2. `test_one_letter_ids_and_en_dash_headings_are_read` also proves a four-letter id under an em dash is read with its numbered criteria counted, and that a five-letter prefix is outside the grammar and not scored.

### XE-045 — A fresh reader goes over the packet before the other vendor does

**Status:** building Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Why.** Idea from google/elephant-goldfish-model (Apache-2.0; idea only, no code copied). The cost being cut: every criterion the surface doesn't visibly meet is found by the other vendor, one capped round at a time; the packaging half (item 2 of the 2026-10-31 handoff) is already in `gate_packet` via goal `review-packet-ready-3`. A reader from the builder's own family, started fresh over the same surface, is cheap and runs before the round clock starts. It isn't a review and nothing counts it as one (XE-005). Design docs are out of scope (XE-031 covers them).

**Links introduced:** two, both in the pre-pass record.

- `goldfish/<item ids>-<base sha, 12>.json` under `GSTACK_PEER_REVIEW_DIR`, named by the packet's item ids and its first repository's base. It names that repository by identity (`check_ledger.repo_id`: its origin without credentials) and holds, in order, one appended entry per pre-pass run (each naming every repository head it read and the SHA-256 of the criteria text it was given) and one `opened` marker per `open` (the review id and that review's first repository, by the same identity, since an upstream and a fork with the same item ids and base share one record). `open` finds the record by item ids and base. A reader assumes it describes the change being opened, which holds only at the same heads and criteria, so `open` compares both and never trusts the name alone.
- Each question in an entry carries the criterion number it concerns. A reader assumes that number indexes the packet's `acceptance_criteria`; it holds only while the entry's criteria hash matches the packet's.

1. `peer_review.py goldfish <packet> --builder claude|codex` builds the review surface the way `round` does (same `build_surface`, same caps, same TB-002 enclosure) into a scratch folder, and starts one fresh process of that tool (`claude -p` or `codex exec`; no resume, no session id) with the surface, the acceptance criteria verbatim, and one instruction: for each criterion, say whether this surface alone shows it is met, and list what the change assumes that the surface doesn't show. Nothing from the builder's conversation is passed. The child runs with `GSTACK_PEER_REVIEW_SESSION` set, so nothing it starts can recurse into peer review, and `goldfish` itself refuses to start when that variable is already set.
2. It appends an entry to the record named above (heads, criteria hash, questions by criterion number, tokens and cost as XE-012 records them) and prints the questions. Earlier entries are never rewritten. A run that answered exits 0 whatever the questions say. A tool that starts and then fails, times out, or returns no criteria list exits non-zero and appends nothing. It never opens a review, spends a review id, writes a round or touches a `state.json`.
3. Nothing in `round`, `collect`, `release` or `record-release` reads the record. A test runs the same stub review with and without a pre-pass and asserts the outcome, coverage and release lane are identical. `choose_reviewer`'s same-family refusal is unchanged; the pre-pass never goes through it.
4. `open` writes one field to the review's own `state.json`, `goldfish`, and never refuses on it. Only the pre-pass entries appended after the record's last `opened` marker count, so each checkpoint is judged by its own pre-passes; `open` then appends its own marker. Among the counted entries, it reads `absent` when none has a criteria hash matching the packet's. Among those that do, the first decides: `ran_at_head` when it names exactly the heads being opened in every repository, `ran_earlier` when it names a different head in any of them, so a later re-run can't hide a change made after the first, and `absent` when it names a different set of repositories. This is a field the item adds to its own record, which the boundary-test constraint exempts; it adds no vocabulary entry, metric report or score.
5. If the builder's tool can't start a fresh process (not installed, no credential), or `--builder` names anything other than `claude` or `codex`, the command says which and exits non-zero. It never falls back to another family, another tool or the running session.
6. `/gstack-build` Step 5 tells the builder to run it once per checkpoint before `open`, then act on the questions or leave them. Skipping it is allowed.
7. It earns its place or goes. Only reviews whose `goldfish` reads `ran_at_head` or `ran_earlier` count; skipped (`absent`) reviews don't. A review counts under the first repository in its packet, as its `opened` marker names it, so a review still counts after its checkout is gone. After ten counted reviews in this repository, if none reads `ran_earlier` (the builder changed nothing after a pre-pass), the item goes back to Dorian to be dropped. The count is read from the `state.json` files and quoted, not added up (XE-029). It is this item's drop condition, read from its own field, not a metric report or score.
8. Tests with a stub tool cover: a first entry at a different head reads `ran_earlier` even after a later re-run at the opened head; a second checkpoint for the same items and base is judged only by the pre-passes after the first `open`; a changed criteria hash reads `absent`, including when the head also moved; a missing tool and an unknown `--builder` exit non-zero with no substitute; a tool that starts and fails appends nothing; a run inside a reviewer session is refused; criterion 3's comparison. `gstack-execution` moves a minor version with its marketplace entry, and the top-level marketplace version moves (CI-002), by text replace. `run_all_tests.py`, unedited, names the new test among the suites it discovered and reports no failures.

**What's not taken.** EGM's Brainstorm, Draft and Bootstrap (covered by `/gstack-design-doc` and `/reverse-prd`), its Drift report (`/gstack-verify` emits one), and its document review loop (`/academic-critique`, `/blog-diligence`). EGM itself isn't installed: its commands would duplicate these and route around `/gstack-build`. MOXY-159 is already fixed on `main` (`build_surface` reads the head with `git show`, comment cites it); its Jira issue is still open and should be closed, not rebuilt.

### GO-016 — Goal-loop fixes folded in from the 2026-10-30 handoff

**Status:** done. Shipped with GA-011 in PR #277, merged `f4d34b3` (evidence in GA-011's status). Folded in at Dorian's instruction 2026-11-02 ("Fold the extras in"). Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** none. `release_lane` and the wide gates keep their fields; `wide_links_hold` decides one of them.

1. `goal_new.py`'s drafting rules say the container already has no network and loopback must stay open, and `cmd_tests` and `cmd_holdout` refuse drafted source that rebinds anything on the `socket` module (wfa-gateway-v1's holdout guard blocked Turbopack's loopback, so every Next.js build failed it). Test: three guard forms are refused; binding a socket to 127.0.0.1 isn't.
2. `wide_links_hold` takes `links_unverifiable` in the wide lane when every check that didn't verify is a finding citing `evidence/ci-*`, a log the dispatcher fetched itself; a stale link, a missing record or any other unverifiable check still fails it. Test: `test_the_wide_link_gate_takes_ci_log_findings_and_nothing_else_unverifiable`. (Extensions in XE-042 and XE-043, both building.)
3. `/gstack-build` says each fix round passes `--ledger-run` (already in the code, tested in `test_ci_run_paths.py`).
4. Not changed: `resync` on a sync pull request (the current code takes main's tip plus an empty commit, and `test_goal_run.py` covers it; not reproduced here); an acknowledged goal-envelope escalation keeping later items red, because judging an item only on what it adds would let a direct push to `goal/<id>` past every item check. Dorian ruled 2026-11-02 ("Option B"): no change; when a change outside the envelope lands on a goal branch, the fix is a new goal with wider Allowed paths.
5. `plugin.json` 0.98.0, its marketplace entry and README Version History, and the top-level marketplace version 1.158.0 cover GA-011 through GO-016 (CI-002).

## Second objective: academic-pipeline integrity

Goal: the pipeline must not encode a fact it cannot know. Names, filenames, and required sections come from run data or from the user, never from a template constant, and a mechanical gate reports what was checked before anything is presented as finished.

### AP-001 — Derive deliverable names and attribution from run data

**Status:** review. Merged unreviewed (relabelled from `done` 2026-09-29 under the one release path, Dorian's ruling); review and release records not found. Prior: review 20260911-163931-423bd0a-bo_glftu, fixes_verified; merged as 6603aae.

1. Stage 7 writes its deliverable to a name derived from the Stage 1 target slug. The orchestrator's artifact table and Stage 8's input reference that same name.
2. Stage 1's diagram deliverable is derived from the target slug likewise.
3. No skill contains a person's name as a worked example. The author block is populated from the vault voice profile or by asking. Inventing a name is forbidden in text.
4. Tests: grepping the plugin tree for the literal strings `complete_document.md`, `mermaid_diagram.md`, and the fabricated surname each return zero hits.

### AP-002 — Every declared requirement has a producer

**Status:** review. Merged unreviewed (relabelled from `done` 2026-09-29 under the one release path, Dorian's ruling); review and release records not found. Prior: review 20260911-165938-d11564a-g289mqlt, fixes_verified; merged as 856f5c6.

1. End-matter sections declared in Stage 4 appear in Stage 5's `structure_plan` whenever the venue requires them.
2. Stage 6 writes those sections as ordinary sections.
3. Stage 6 carries an explicit rule to number Vancouver citations by first appearance while drafting, rather than leaving order to a later repair.
4. Tests: a fixture run produces every section named in the formatting requirements' `section_order`.

### AP-003 — Mechanical release gate before completion

**Status:** review. Merged unreviewed (relabelled from `done` 2026-09-29 under the one release path, Dorian's ruling); review and release records not found. Prior: review 20260911-165938-d11564a-g289mqlt, fixes_verified; merged as 856f5c6.

1. The orchestrator runs a gate before presenting deliverables: zero em dashes, zero forbidden phrases, renumber `--check` exit 0, every `section_order` entry present, and no two references sharing a normalized DOI or URL.
2. Stage 7 dedupes on normalized DOI/URL, not only on BibTeX key, and warns when two keys resolve to one work.
3. The gate names each check and its result. A failing gate blocks the completion report rather than being narrated around it.
4. Tests: the gate run against a fixture carrying a planted em dash, a duplicate URL, and a missing section fails on exactly those three.

### AP-004 — Deposit a paper's artifacts on Zenodo, one record per artifact

**Status:** building. Added 2026-10-30 on Dorian's request (“Can you add that whole zenodo thing to academic pipeline?”), after the reference runner for *Models Propose, Rules Decide* was deposited by hand as 10.5281/zenodo.23819764.

Settled by Dorian on 2026-10-30: one artifact repository (`MoxyWolfLLC/zenodo`), not a repository per artifact; one Zenodo record and DOI per artifact, uploaded directly, not through Zenodo's GitHub integration; a license the agent picks (MIT for code).

1. An optional Stage 10 runs after the Stage 9 gate passes, when the run produced or names an executable or data artifact (a reference implementation, fixtures, a dataset). With none, Stage 10 reports SKIP, never a pass.
2. Each artifact lives in the artifact repository at `<kind>/<slug>/` (for example `runners/<target-slug>/`) and carries its own `README.md`, `LICENSE`, `.zenodo.json`, `CITATION.cff` with the author's ORCID, its inputs, and the expected output of its checks under `expected/`. The repository root keeps an index table with a DOI column. Zenodo's GitHub integration stays off for that repository, because a release would archive every artifact as one record.
3. `scripts/zenodo_deposit.py draft <folder>` zips the folder (excluding caches and its own state file) and creates a Zenodo draft with the folder's `.zenodo.json` as metadata. `publish <folder>` publishes that draft. Nothing publishes without the `publish` subcommand, and the agent runs it only after the user says “publish” in the run, because a published record can't be deleted. `--sandbox` targets sandbox.zenodo.org.
4. The token comes from `ZENODO_TOKEN` or `--token-file` (a `KEY=value` env file such as one in the vault). The script never prints it. The skill tells the agent to read it on the user's machine (the clipboard or a vault file), never to ask for it in chat.
5. Before publishing, the reserved DOI goes into the artifact's `CITATION.cff` and README and the draft is refreshed, so the archive carries its own DOI. After publishing, the DOI goes into the repository index, into the Stage 7 BibTeX file (`bibtex_source_path` in `handoff_for_writer.json`) as a `@misc` entry, and into `pipeline/draft_document.md` where the artifact is described and in the Data Availability Statement. Then Stages 7 and 9 rerun and the gate must pass again.
6. Tests: against an injected transport, `draft` never calls the publish endpoint; a draft is recorded as soon as Zenodo creates it, so a failed upload leaves state and a rerun refreshes the same draft rather than making another; a published record is never redrafted; a symlink in the folder is refused before anything is sent; the uploaded zip holds the folder's files under `<slug>/` and excludes `__pycache__`, `.pyc` and the state file; the metadata sent equals `.zenodo.json`; `publish` without a recorded draft refuses; a missing token exits nonzero without a traceback; the token never appears in output.

## Third objective: evidence integrity

Goal: a report is treated as evidence only when the links between it and the work it describes still hold. Completeness checks items; nothing checked links. Every defect in this objective is a link that still resolved after the thing at its other end had changed, which produces a complete report, no error, and a person about to sign it. Source: "When Structure Pays" (Cougias, 2026), which reports three such defects in this repository's own executor plus three false passes in its own completeness gate.

### EV-001 — No check reports a pass over input it did not examine

**Status:** review. Merged unreviewed (relabelled from `done` 2026-09-29 under the one release path, Dorian's ruling); names no review; no review or release record found. Prior: merged to `main` in `c747ab9` (PR #9, head `b746e22`) on 13 September 2026 by the Release Owner.

**Links introduced:** none; this item removes a false one (a verdict that implied coverage it never had).

1. Every check returns what it examined and in what unit, and a PASS with zero coverage becomes a FAIL naming the shortfall. The rule is applied where results are assembled, so no later check can opt out of it by forgetting to.
2. A check that does not apply returns SKIP, is printed as SKIP, and is counted in the summary line. It is never folded into a green line.
3. Each check carries a seeded defect it must catch, because a repair verified only against the reported case reproduces the defect it is repairing.
4. Tests: a paper with no reference list fails `duplicate_sources` for examining nothing; an empty forbidden-phrase list and an empty `section_order` fail rather than report a clean paper; a non-Vancouver paper skips rather than passes citation order.

### EV-002 — Findings bind to content, and every link re-resolves on demand

**Status:** review. Merged unreviewed (relabelled from `done` 2026-09-29 under the one release path, Dorian's ruling); only a reconstruction of its review found, no release record. Prior: merged to `main` in `c747ab9` (PR #9, head `b746e22`) on 13 September 2026 by the Release Owner, without a fresh review of the final head; review `20260912-122421-80159e3-_lrr97ak` (codex/gpt-6-astra, `rounds_exhausted`, six blockers, five verified fixed; the last F2 repair and the one-walk change have regression tests and no reviewer sign-off). **That review record no longer exists** (EV-009); anything said of it here is reconstruction from the pull request and commits.

**Links introduced:** a per-finding subject (blob id plus a span hash at the reviewed head) retained in `round-N.json`; the `verify` report's re-resolution of that subject at the repository's current head.

1. The dispatcher, not the reviewer, binds each finding to the content at the reviewed head when the round is recorded.
2. `verify` re-resolves the packet's commits, every finding subject, every disposition, and every recorded observation, and names each link it examined.
3. Drift expected from a repair is not reported as staleness; drift under any other disposition is. A finding's recorded lines found unchanged elsewhere in the same file at the current head have moved, not drifted, and aren't stale.
4. A missing entry and a link that resolved to the wrong thing do not share an outcome name, and a record that cannot be re-resolved is a third answer.
5. `release` refuses on `stale_link` and `incomplete_record`.
6. Tests: content changing under an undisposed finding is stale; the same change under a `fixed` finding is not; unchanged lines moved under a `deferred` finding are not, and moved lines that also changed are; a hand-edited span is detected; a snapshot-prefixed path and a repo-relative path bind to one subject.

### EV-003 — The undeclared-write sweep, in the direction nobody had

**Status:** review. Merged unreviewed (relabelled from `done` 2026-09-29 under the one release path, Dorian's ruling); names no review; no review or release record found. Prior: merged to `main` in `c747ab9` (PR #9, head `b746e22`) on 13 September 2026 by the Release Owner.

**Links introduced:** the declaration-to-behaviour link for every handler, checked at runtime rather than against other declarations.

1. `run --audit-writes` hashes the run root before each node and after its handler returns, before declared outputs are written, and fails a node that wrote a path no declaration mentions.
2. The sweep forces serial execution, because attributing a write while several nodes are writing would be a declaration that can be false.
3. The exclusion of executor-owned files has one home.
4. Reads are not instrumented. A declared dependency that nothing reads is still undetected, and this is stated as a limit rather than implied to be covered.
5. Tests: a handler writing an undeclared path fails by name; a clean graph passes; audit mode does not overlap nodes.

### EV-004 — The approver's reconstruction gets a field

**Status:** review. Merged unreviewed (relabelled from `done` 2026-09-29 under the one release path, Dorian's ruling); names no review; no review or release record found. Prior: merged to `main` in `c747ab9` (PR #9, head `b746e22`) on 13 September 2026 by the Release Owner.

**Links introduced:** each observation's claim-to-command-to-output link, re-runnable by `verify`.

1. `release` records, per repository, that the release head is the reviewed head, with the command and a digest of its output, plus any observation the approver adds.
2. The record states plainly that it captures what was run, not that a person read it.
3. `verify` re-runs recorded observations and reports one that no longer holds.
4. Tests: the automatic observation is recorded and re-verified; a stale digest is caught.

### EV-005 — Each item declares the links it introduces

**Status:** review. Merged unreviewed (relabelled from `done` 2026-09-29 under the one release path, Dorian's ruling); names no review; no review or release record found. Prior: merged to `main` in `c747ab9` (PR #9, head `b746e22`) on 13 September 2026 by the Release Owner.

**Links introduced:** none.

1. The design-doc template carries a `Links this item introduces` column and says to derive it from the change rather than copy another item's.
2. `/gstack-build` treats an item that introduces state with no declared links as not yet covered, and amends the doc before any code.
3. The review packet carries that row, and the reviewer rules on each link in both directions.

### EV-009 — A review record outlives the session that produced it

**Status:** review. Merged unreviewed (relabelled from `done` 2026-09-29 under the one release path, Dorian's ruling); names no review; no review or release record found. Prior: merged as `7b72605` (PR #10) on 2026-09-17 by the Release Owner; review ID: see the pull request. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** the review ID to its record on disk.

Premise (found 14 September 2026): `REVIEW_DIR` defaulted to `$HOME/.gstack/peer-review`, which in a sandboxed session is per-session and outside every connected folder, so records written there died with the session.

1. `GSTACK_PEER_REVIEW_DIR` is required and has no default. A review that cannot name a durable root does not open, and the error says what to set and why.
2. No code path falls back to the home directory, and a test asserts that against the source rather than against one call, because the defect was a default rather than a call site.
3. The command, the contract and `docs.md` all state the requirement, so the three places a reader could learn the old default agree with the code.
4. Tests: `review_root` exits naming the variable when it is unset; a declared directory is used as given; the source contains no home-directory fallback.

**Open:** graph-driven reviews already land in the run directory because `task_graph.py` sets the variable. Direct invocations were the unprotected path, and nothing here recovers the records already lost.

### EV-006 — Instrument reads

**Status:** building. Merged in part as `8bd7fae` (PR #18) on 2026-09-17; criterion 1 is open and not claimed.

**As built:** criterion 2 is met by a proxy: a declared dependency the consumer's result gives no sign of using is reported in the report node as a candidate fake edge, never fatal, because unreferenced is not proof of unused. Criterion 1 is not met: dependencies are inlined in input.json (command handlers) or the prompt (model handlers), so no per-dependency read is observable without changing the payload contract. Upgrade path, mechanism verified 2026-09-17: per-dependency files make reads observable through atime, given the aging described in EV-008.

**Links introduced:** the read side of every handler's declaration.

1. A node's declared inputs are compared against what its handler actually read.
2. A declared dependency that nothing reads is reported, completing the fake-edge test in both directions.

### EV-007 — Reference identity and archive at citation time

**Status:** review. Merged unreviewed (relabelled from `done` 2026-09-29 under the one release path, Dorian's ruling); names no review; no review or release record found. Prior: merged as `8bd7fae` (PR #18) on 2026-09-17; criterion 2 archiving as `4c6e11a` (PR #19).

**As built:** criterion 1 covers mechanical canonical forms: arXiv, DOI and PubMed ids resolve to one work identity regardless of URL form, case or resolver, and the check reports which rule produced each identity; a preprint and its later DOI stay separate works (no registry lookup). Criterion 2 archives to the Wayback Machine and stores a local copy beside the bibliography (Dorian, 2026-09-17, chose both), with the SHA-256 of the archived snapshot, not the live page. Persistently resolvable identifiers are skipped and recorded as skipped. An unreachable archive never blocks a citation; it is recorded with its reason, and the gate reports coverage from the record without touching the network. Criterion 3: a paper with no repository available returns SKIP rather than PASS.

**Links introduced:** the reference-to-work link (identifier to canonical work) and the citation-to-snapshot link.

1. References resolve to a canonical work identity before comparison, so one study cited as a preprint and as its announcement is detected as one work rather than two strings.
2. Every cited URL is archived at citation time and the reference stores the snapshot and its hash.
3. Every commit, test name and review identifier a manuscript cites resolves in the named repository at the cited commit.

### EV-008 — Record what a review examined, not only what it found

**Status:** building. Merged in part as `8bd7fae` (PR #18) on 2026-09-17; criterion 1 (commands run) and criterion 3 are open.

**As built:** criteria 1 and 2 are met for file reads (files opened, files offered, whether it looked beyond the diff). Commands run are not recorded, because the CLIs differ in what they report, so criterion 1's second half is unmet and unclaimed. Criterion 3 is not built.

**Links introduced:** the review-to-search-space link.

1. A round records the paths the reviewer opened and the commands it ran.
2. A round that examined nothing outside the diff is visible as such.
3. Ad-hoc consultations with other tools are conducted through an address that leaves a record, so the denominator of a search stops being unknown.

## Fourth objective: execution economy
