2. `/session-end` gains a step before it composes the handoff that runs the session review as `/session-review` steps 1 to 4 describe (capture, review prompt, draft, finalize), with no prompt from the person. It never runs `publish-prepare` or `publish`.
3. The handoff links the review by its folder path, and the final report names its validation status and lists its proposals. A review that can't run is reported in one line with its reason and never blocks the handoff.
4. Tests: a `project-init:session-end` expansion writes the hook record and a capture from it ends at that boundary; `hooks.json` wires exactly the commands the script accepts; an unrelated command still doesn't fire.
5. `plugins/project-init/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` moves from 0.33.0, and the top-level marketplace version moves with it (CI-002).
6. In CI, `scripts/run_all_tests.py` reports a nonzero count of what it examined, with no failures.

### SM-007 — Team rules live in the Projects repo, and only a Taskade project mounts Taskade

**Status:** done. Approved by Dorian on 2026-10-24; review `20261024-135830-1660de2-5rezzu36`; merged as `e136d48` (PR #236) by `moxywolf-agent[bot]`, `agent_merge_autonomous` under DR-113; follow-up F1 fixed in SM-008.

**Links introduced:** the team-rules link. Every skill, command and project instruction that reads or writes team rules names `GitHub/Projects/_shared-memory/`, and assumes the local `GitHub/Projects` clone is current with `MoxyWolfLLC/Projects` main.

The team rules moved from `Taskade/_Shared Files/_shared-memory/` to `GitHub/Projects/_shared-memory/` on 2026-10-24 (Projects `e3f178c`; hand edits in Projects `45a09c5`; the Taskade INDEX is now a redirect). This item moves the plugin homes, so an org-shared project no longer needs the Taskade folder.

1. No skill, command, README or reference in this repo names `Taskade/_Shared Files/_shared-memory` as a place to read or write team rules; each names `GitHub/Projects/_shared-memory/`. Homes: `project-init/skills/session-start/SKILL.md`, `project-init/skills/session-end/SKILL.md`, `project-init/skills/project-init/SKILL.md`, `project-init/commands/` (every file that names the path), `gstack-execution/commands/gstack-build.md`, `gstack-execution/skills/gstack-execution/SKILL.md`, `obsidian-update/skills/personal-os/SKILL.md` and the root `README.md`. `plugin.json` and `marketplace.json` changelogs are exempt.
2. `/session-start` mounts the Taskade root only when the resolved workspace is a Taskade folder. An org-shared or vault-only project mounts the MoxyWolf Vault and GitHub, and the briefing reports Taskade as "not needed". The most-recently-active fallback scans `Taskade/` only when it is already mounted.
3. `/session-start` fast-forwards `GitHub/Projects` before it reads the team-rules INDEX for every project type. A clone with local work, local commits or no upstream is reported and read as it is, never stashed, reset or forced.
4. `/session-end` writes a new or changed team rule into `GitHub/Projects/_shared-memory/` and its INDEX, commits only that folder, pushes as `moxywolf-agent`, verifies `ls-remote` and pulls back. A rule whose push did not land is reported in the handoff as local only, never as shared.
5. `/init-project` asks for the Taskade root only for a personal (Taskade) project, and no longer says the Taskade root stays mounted for every type.
6. A repo test asserts that none of the homes in criterion 1 names the old path, and reports how many files it examined; zero examined is a failure (EV-001).
7. `project-init` gets a minor version bump, `gstack-execution` and `obsidian-update` patch bumps, each with a changelog line, and the top-level marketplace version moves with them (CI-002).
8. In CI, `scripts/run_all_tests.py` reports a nonzero count of what it examined, with no failures.

Not covered: the gate log at `Taskade/_Shared Files/_gate-log/`, and team rules whose content says deliverables or design mirrors land in Taskade. Each is its own decision.

### SM-008 — The gate log and every deliverable home follow the project's workspace

**Status:** done. Approved by Dorian on 2026-10-24; merged as `6358b51` (PR #238, head `209d3ae`) by `moxywolf-agent[bot]`, `record-release` `agent_merge_autonomous` under DR-113, after review `20261024-162820-1800419-3nnw71qr` (codex/gpt-6-astra, `fixes_verified`, 2 rounds); earlier reviews `20261024-152459-b6851af-skagaovm` (`rounds_exhausted`, see XE-039), `20261024-155646-e8ecd41-psyej_hf` (`fixes_verified`), `20261024-161453-6fdc1ed-lrcmr66k` (`no_blocking_findings`); criterion 13's mirror is Projects `6d83fd6`. The trade-off to approve: in Taskade (a Drive folder) a row reached everyone at once; in a repo a row is shared only when committed and pushed, so criterion 3 makes that step explicit, as SM-007 criterion 4 did for team rules.

The editor's internal `taskadePath` key keeps its name.

7. `project-init/skills/session-start/SKILL.md`'s closing write-policy note says Step 3b fast-forwards `GitHub/Projects` for every project type, and an org-shared project's listed repos as well. The matching edge case says "a clone", not "an org-shared clone".
8. `/refresh-project-instructions` and `/session-end` write `cowork-project-instructions.md`, `project-surfaces.json` and `cowork-session-handoff.md` from the project's Hub, plus `GitHub/Projects/_shared-memory/INDEX.md`, into the attached claude.ai Project with `project_write`, at the workspace-relative paths the loader stub names. With no Project attached, each reports "not mirrored: no Project attached". Neither ever writes a Project copy back to disk.
9. A test greps every tracked text file outside criterion 2's exemptions for both old paths, `Taskade/_Shared Files/_shared-memory` and `Taskade/_Shared Files/_gate-log`, and the gstack homes for `Taskade/<project>/06`, then prints how many files it examined and how many hits it found. Zero examined is a failure (EV-001), and a positive control proves the check catches each string.

11. In CI, `run_all_tests.py` reports a nonzero count of what it examined, with no failures.
12. SM-007 is marked `done` in this document, citing review `20261024-135830-1660de2-5rezzu36` and merge `e136d48` (PR #236, `agent_merge_autonomous`).

### SM-009 — Session end keeps a cloud review without asking, and its roster names Dorian C

Dorian, on keeping a cloud session's review (2026-10-24): it "need never be asked again -- *always* do that."

Dorian's standing operating rule, verify before recommending commitment, has one home: the verification checks file the gstack-execution hook already injects verbatim at session start and on the first prompt. The startup commands read it explicitly and say when they couldn't.

### ME-001 — Session start reports the model economy

**Status:** done. PR #278 (opened as `moxywolf-agent`), merged as `cb3b10d` (head `b7a849a`) by `moxywolf-agent[bot]`, `record-release` `agent_merge_autonomous` under DR-113, after review `20261102-121400-b7a849a-3zbguceo` (codex/gpt-6-astra, `no_blocking_findings`, coverage covered 15/15); earlier reviews kept as evidence: `20261102-111417-84eef5b-_vmr3e30` (`examined_nothing`), `20261102-112253-c64f196-ko5n394u` (`malformed_output`), `20261102-112922-c64f196-vvvom69v` (`fixes_verified`), `20261102-115547-c64f196-h46bzk9t` (`malformed_output`), `20261102-120119-c64f196-ivhtuykd` (clean; marketplace to 1.159.0), `20261102-120835-b7a849a-j_25w51e` (blocking); approved by Dorian on 2026-11-02 ("I'm ready"); `session-start.patch` applied as step 8b's text and committed at `docs/evidence/ME-001-session-start.patch`.

**Links introduced:** a settings link. The session-start skill names `~/.claude/settings.json`, the project's `.claude/settings.json`, the keys `model` and `advisorModel`, and the environment variables `DISABLE_TELEMETRY` and `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ADVISOR_TOOL`. Claude Code owns those names; after a rename there, the step reports NOT SET rather than failing.

Dorian's standing rule: a cheaper main model with a stronger advisor, never the strongest model running throughout. A session can't switch its own model, so `/session-start` reports whether the settings follow the rule and applies the gstack-build economy inside the session either way.

1. `commands/session-start.md` carries step 8b exactly as `session-start.patch` gives it, between steps 8 and 9.
2. The session-start skill gets a matching step h. It reads `model` and `advisorModel` from `~/.claude/settings.json`, then from the project's `.claude/settings.json` if present; the project's value wins. Set means `model` is `sonnet` or a Sonnet model ID and `advisorModel` is `fable` or `opus`.
3. The briefing's Shared services block has a `Model economy:` line: `sonnet main + <advisor> advisor` when set; `NOT SET (main=<x>, advisor=<y>)` otherwise, with the remedy on its own line as step 8b words it. If `DISABLE_TELEMETRY` or `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ADVISOR_TOOL` is set, the line says the advisor is off regardless of the setting.
4. The step never gates the session and never edits a settings file.
5. Whatever the setting says, the session applies the gstack-build economy: reading and mapping go to the `explore` and `docs` agents on Haiku; the advisor is consulted before committing to an approach and before declaring an item done.
6. A test in `plugins/project-init/tests/` checks that the command carries 8b with its remedy line, and that the skill carries step h, both statuses, the two environment variables, the no-gate and no-edit rule, and the Shared services line.
7. project-init gets a minor bump with a README Version History line, and its marketplace entry and the marketplace's top-level version move (CI-002).

### ME-002 – spec_review caches its prompt and records what the cache did

**Status:** done. Merged in `fd5aab6` (PR #296, head `72d0ac0`) on 11 November 2026 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]`, `record-release` `agent_merge_autonomous` under DR-113, after review `20261111-085918-b2b3309-r0meyyw8` (codex/gpt-6-astra, 2 rounds, `fixes_verified`, coverage covered); `run_all_tests.py` 111 of 111 through ledger run `9342b48f13b3` on Dorian's Mac; CI `tests` run 43067578276 success; live runs `docs/evidence/ME-002-ME-003-live.md`. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** the record's `cache` field (`read`, `write`), read by anyone judging whether caching pays. It measures this run because it is summed from the API's own `usage` on every response in the run, never estimated.

`spec_review.py` resends its system prompt and the item's scope on every `pause_turn` resume and on the advisor reminder; caching removes that cost. ME-002, ME-003 and ME-004 are exempt from *Test the boundaries before adding item IDs*, by Dorian's ruling on 2026-11-11 ("Waive for all three", covering ME-002, ME-003 and ME-004 only).

1. Every request carries top-level `"cache_control": {"type": "ephemeral"}` (5-minute TTL). No explicit breakpoints. Tools and thinking settings stay fixed for the whole conversation, so no resend invalidates the prefix.
2. The advisor tool's own `caching` field stays unset (break-even is about three advisor calls per conversation; `max_uses` is 3). Revisit only when the existing `advisor_calls` field (SR-001.3) averages 3 or more across the last 10 spec-review records.
3. The default advisor is `claude-fable-5-2` (was `claude-fable-5-1`; amends SR-001 criterion 2). `GSTACK_SPEC_ADVISOR` still overrides it.
4. The record gains `cache: {read, write}`, summed from each response's `cache_read_input_tokens` and `cache_creation_input_tokens`. If any response in the run lacks those fields, `cache` is `null` for the whole run with the reason, never zero or a partial sum (EV-001).
5. `--selftest` with the stub transport shows the field on every request body, including a resume and a reminder, and the record's sums across three stubbed responses.
6. One live run on a real item that includes at least one resume or reminder quotes `cache.read` and `cache.write` from its record. A read of 0, or `null`, is the finding, reported as such; the criterion is the quote, not a number.

### ME-003 – peer_review's Claude reviewer runs with an advisor

**Status:** done. Merged in `fd5aab6` with ME-002 (PR #296, head `72d0ac0`, 11 November 2026, `agent_merge_autonomous` under DR-113, review `20261111-085918-b2b3309-r0meyyw8` `fixes_verified`; tests and live runs as ME-002). Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** `GSTACK_CLAUDE_ADVISOR` names the advisor, and the round record's `advisor` block (`requested`, `seen`, `disabled_by_env`) is read by anyone checking whether a review actually had one. `seen` means the advisor ran only when the CLI's `modelUsage` lists the advisor model; otherwise it's `null` with the reason.

Exempt from *Test the boundaries before adding item IDs* (Dorian's 2026-11-11 ruling, see ME-002).

1. The `claude -p` reviewer command gains `--advisor <model>`, from `GSTACK_CLAUDE_ADVISOR`, default `fable` (the reviewer is `claude-opus-5`, which accepts Fable or Opus 5 and later). `GSTACK_CLAUDE_ADVISOR=off` drops the flag.
2. If the CLI exits at launch over the advisor (pairing, allowlist, Fable usage-credits consent), the round reports `review_unavailable` naming the advisor. It never quietly retries without one.
3. If `DISABLE_TELEMETRY` or `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ADVISOR_TOOL` is set in the reviewer's environment while an advisor is requested, the round reports `review_unavailable` and `disabled_by_env` names the variable. With `GSTACK_CLAUDE_ADVISOR=off`, `off` wins: `requested` is `off` and the variables are not consulted.
4. Tests with a fake `claude` binary: the flag is present by default, absent on `off`; a launch failure maps to `review_unavailable`; a disabling variable with an advisor requested maps to `review_unavailable` with `disabled_by_env` recorded; `off` with a disabling variable set runs with `requested: off`.
5. One live round on the review host records `seen` from `modelUsage`, or `null` with the reason; either satisfies the criterion, and `null` is reported as unverified in the review's handoff without blocking it. A launch failure is `review_unavailable` whatever its cause; the record names the advisor when the CLI's stderr does.

### ME-004 – Council's Anthropic calls ask for caching and log what it did

**Status:** building. Unparked 2026-11-11 on Dorian's approval ("I approve the one-sentence fix to ME-004") after SR-002's sweep; branch `build/ME-004-council-cache`; parked code kept as `MW Plugins/06 – Engineering/ME-004-council-cache-parked-2026-11-11.patch` in the Projects repo.

**Links introduced:** each result file and `_summary.json` gain `cache: {read, write}` per job, read by anyone judging whether council caching pays. The numbers come from OpenRouter's `usage.prompt_tokens_details` on that response, never estimated.

Kept on Dorian's ruling against evidence that it may not pay: every council call is one-shot, Stage 2 uses a different system prompt than Stage 1, `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5` caches nothing under 1,024 tokens, and a cache write that's never read costs 1.25x the input price. This item makes the cost visible; the decision stays Dorian's. Exempt from *Test the boundaries before adding item IDs* (Dorian's 2026-11-11 ruling, see ME-002).

1. `openrouter_dispatch.py` adds top-level `"cache_control": {"type": "ephemeral"}` to a job whose `model` starts with `anthropic/`, unless the job sets `"cache": false`. Other providers' payloads are unchanged.
2. Each successful result records `cache: {read, write}` from `usage.prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` and `cache_write_tokens`; each absent field is `null`, and `cache.reason` says which (EV-001). `cache.requested` says whether the job sent `cache_control`. A non-Anthropic or `"cache": false` job records the same fields from whatever OpenRouter returns. `_summary.json` carries each job's `cache`.
3. A test with a stubbed `urlopen` shows the field on an Anthropic job, its absence on a non-Anthropic job and on `"cache": false`, and the recorded numbers from a stubbed response.
4. One live deliberation's `_summary.json` is quoted with its cache numbers and reported to Dorian. For a job that sent `cache_control`: reads of 0 with writes above 0 are reported as a cost with no saving; reads and writes both 0 as below the cache minimum; reads above 0 as the saving; `null` as unverified. A job that didn't send it is reported as caching not requested, whatever its numbers. Both fields sit under `usage.prompt_tokens_details`, per OpenRouter's documentation. Nothing switches caching off automatically: whether to set `"cache": false` in the deliberation engine's jobs is Dorian's call.
5. council gets a minor bump with its README Version History line, and its marketplace entry and the top-level marketplace version move (CI-002).

**Shared by ME-002 and ME-003:** gstack-execution gets a minor bump (council's is ME-004.5, separately), its marketplace entry, the top-level marketplace version and README Version History move (CI-002). `run_all_tests.py` names every changed selftest and reports no failures. **Not taken:** an advisor in skill-creator's `run_eval.py`; it measures whether a skill triggers on a short single query, and an advisor would change what's measured.

### SS-001 — Session end sweeps the loop's scratch

**Status:** done. Shipped with ME-001 (PR #278, merged as `cb3b10d`, head `b7a849a`, `agent_merge_autonomous` under DR-113, review `20261102-121400-b7a849a-3zbguceo` `no_blocking_findings` 15/15; earlier reviews and approval as ME-001); `session-end.patch` applied as step 8b's text and committed at `docs/evidence/SS-001-session-end.patch`; follow-up: the Cowork Linux VM's own `/sessions` volume isn't measured or swept. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** a scratch-layout link. The session-end skill names where gstack leaves scratch (`~/review-host` and `review_host.sh`'s reinstall, `/tmp/gstack-ship-*`, `/tmp/qa-*`, `/tmp/<suite>.log` and its `run-record.json`) and what it must never touch (`GSTACK_PEER_REVIEW_DIR`, `tests/container/results/`). If gstack-execution moves any of those, Step 7c measures or protects the old place; criterion 7's test checks the names are present, not that gstack still uses them.

`/session-end` measures the loop's worktrees, captures, logs and installs on the Mac first, then removes only the loop's own disposables, and only on the person's word.

1. `commands/session-end.md` carries step 8b exactly as `session-end.patch` gives it, between steps 8a and 9.
2. The session-end skill gets a matching Step 7c after the Project mirror. It measures from the macOS shell (Desktop Commander, not `device_bash`) with the patch's `du` and `docker system df` commands and prints the sizes before it calls anything clean.
3. In a local session it asks with AskUserQuestion, listing each candidate and its size, and removes only: `/tmp/gstack-ship-*` worktrees no `git worktree list` still names (then `git worktree prune` in that repo), `/tmp/qa-*` captures a report already quotes, `/tmp/<suite>.log` files whose `run-record.json` exists, and `~/review-host/node_modules`. In a cloud session it removes nothing and reports.
4. Docker images and the Playwright cache are offered on a separate line and never swept by default.
5. It never touches `GSTACK_PEER_REVIEW_DIR`, `tests/container/results/`, any repository clone or the Cowork VM bundle.
6. The Step 8 report carries one line: `Scratch: freed <n> GB (<what>); left <m> GB (<what>, why)`, or `Scratch: not measured (<reason>)` when the sweep couldn't run, never clean.
7. A test in `plugins/project-init/tests/` checks 8b's place and text, Step 7c's measure-first order, the protected paths, the cloud rule and the report line.
8. Released as part of project-init 0.45.0 with ME-001; the README line names both.

### SM-011 — Session start never drops a briefing section, and reads without waiting on a mount

**Status:** done. Approved by Dorian on 2026-11-10 ("Yes"); criteria 1 to 8 merged as `bac058d` (PR #289) by Dorian, no cross-tool review ran; criterion 9 ships in project-init 0.46.1 on Dorian's instruction ("Ship the Step 3b fix as project-init 0.46.1").

**Links introduced:** tool-name links. The reader order names tools (Desktop Commander, `get_file_contents` on `MoxyWolfLLC/Projects`, `project_read`), so a renamed tool leaves the order pointing at nothing; criterion 6's test checks only the text, not the tool.

On 2026-11-10 two sessions skipped the Jira, decision-record and open-issue sections because a folder-access prompt was refused or timed out, and the skill allowed skipping sections that read from a missing root. Cowork offers no supported way to attach a project's folders from code, so reads stop depending on the mount.

1. `/session-start` reads project files in a fixed order and moves to the next source when one fails: Desktop Commander on the user's computer, at the file's absolute path (no prompt); the mounted root, when this session already has it; for files in an org-shared workspace or `_shared-memory/`, the GitHub connector's `get_file_contents` on `MoxyWolfLLC/Projects` at the workspace-relative path; for the instructions, `project-surfaces.json`, the handoff and the team `INDEX.md`, the claude.ai Project copy via `project_read`; for vault files, the Google Drive connector.
2. The folder-access request still goes out at session start, but nothing waits on its answer. A refused or unanswered request changes which source reads a file, never whether the section runs.
3. No briefing section is skipped to save time or to stay on a task. Every section appears with its findings or `unavailable: <reason>` naming each source tried. Jira and the GitHub pull requests and issues never depend on a mount.
4. A task passed as the argument is not a project name and doesn't replace the briefing. The project resolves as usual, the full briefing runs, and the task becomes Step 6's first focus option.
5. The phrase that allowed the skip, skip the briefing sections that read from a missing root, appears nowhere in the skill or its command file.
6. `plugins/project-init/tests/test_briefing_never_skipped.py` checks criteria 1, 3, 4 and 5 in the skill text, with a positive control that the old phrase is caught.
7. project-init gets a minor bump with a README Version History line, and its marketplace entry and the marketplace's top-level version move (CI-002).
8. After the merge, this document is mirrored to `GitHub/Projects/MW Plugins/06 – Engineering/DESIGN-moxywolf-plugins.md`.
9. Git runs on the Mac itself, through Desktop Commander's `start_process`, in `/session-start` Step 3b, `/session-end`'s handoff push and `/init-project`'s scaffold push. `device_bash` is a Linux VM with the folders mounted and no GitHub login, so git there fails to authenticate and strands `.git/index.lock`; it's used for git only when Desktop Commander isn't present, and an authentication failure there is reported as such. The criterion 6 test checks all three skills say so.

## Sixth objective: trust boundaries

Opened 2026-09-20. Premise: **a record the loop reads as settled is a record nobody re-derives, and that is the property worth attacking.** EV-001 made a check say what it examined; this objective makes a record say where it came from. gstack's own writes come from repository artifacts, land through a pull request and live in git. The wider exposure is elsewhere in the marketplace: `obsidian-update`'s memory extraction and `vault-skills`' capture write notes later sessions read as established, `research-pipeline` ingests web sources with no inclusion rubric, `synergy-engine` scores text scraped from LinkedIn, and `document-analysis` converts documents other people wrote. Scope is the write path, not the model. These items don't claim to make a prompt injection-proof.

### TB-001 — Every record says where it came from

**Status:** building. Criteria 1 to 5 are built in this change; criterion 6 follows when the weekly report scores P5 from the origin field.

**Links introduced:** the record-to-origin link: the `origin` field makes checkable the reader's assumption that a record was produced by the system that names it. The origin-to-reviewer link: a record whose origin is external text names the gate or human that examined it, and a record naming neither is treated as unexamined.

1. Every record gstack writes (round record, packet, run measurement note, release record, handoff ref) carries `origin`, a controlled value from the vocabulary: `repository_artifact`, `gate_output`, `human_instruction`, `external_text`. The writer sets it. Nothing defaults it.
2. A record whose origin is `external_text` also carries `examined_by`, naming the gate or the human that read it, or the value `unexamined`.
3. `verify` reports records carrying `unknown` or a value outside the vocabulary. It reports how many records it examined, and zero examined is a failure, per EV-001.
4. Adding `origin` and `examined_by` moves the vocabulary to 1.2.0, which is an XE-012 break point.
5. Tests: a writer that omits `origin` fails; a record with `external_text` and no `examined_by` fails; the verify pass reports its coverage; the vocabulary version bump appears in the run note.
6. P5 in the pre-registration becomes measurable when this merges, and the weekly report scores it from that point rather than reporting it as declared.

### TB-002 — One enclosure for text the loop did not write

**Status:** done. Review 20260929-163026-becf08d-r7y5tqnk, `fixes_verified`, 5/5; merged as 6ca612b (PR #81), recorded `agent_merge_on_instruction`. Its limit, per criterion 4: a speed bump on unverified evidence, not a control.

**Links introduced:** the quotation link. The model cannot tell data from instruction unless the boundary is marked. The enclosure marks it. It does not enforce it.

1. One file, `plugins/gstack-execution/skills/gstack-execution/references/untrusted-enclosure.md`, defines the enclosure and the instruction-immunity rule: text inside it is data, never a directive, whatever it claims about itself.
2. Every place the loop puts text it did not produce into a prompt loads that file rather than restating the rule: reviewer output, pull request and issue bodies, fetched pages, and any retrieved memory. One producer, per XE-008.
3. A test greps the ingesting skills for a restated enclosure rule and fails on a second home, reporting how many files it examined.
4. The item's report states the measured limit of this defense rather than implying it is one. In the numbers the external response supplied (revised edition, 2026-09-20), an enclosure of this kind moved executed adversarial actions from 60% to 30%, on a synthetic benchmark whose traces and harness were not published. It is a speed bump on unverified evidence. TB-001 and TB-003 are the controls.
5. Adoption outside gstack is a later item, and only for the plugins named in the objective preamble.

### TB-003 — Egress is granted, not filtered

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` as `b134ca3` (PR #89, head `ded5df5`) 2026-09-30 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian's instruction (`record-release`: `agent_merge_on_instruction`). Review `20260930-110719-f03345a-nz0o2zq3` (`fixes_verified`, 4/4, 2 rounds), opened with coverage `covered` and no override; `tests` green at the reviewed head (dispatcher-read). Asked for by Dorian on 2026-09-30. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** the destination-to-grant link. A tool call carrying a destination is assumed to go somewhere the owner allowed; the grant now covers destinations as well as tools and output roots.

1. `governance.py`'s grant model gains destination patterns beside `allowed_tools` and `output_roots`. A call whose destination matches no grant is refused and named, with the destination and the item that would have to grant it.
2. Refusal is the default. There is no pattern list of bad destinations. DR-099 has the worked example.
3. The refusal is a typed error naming the exact missing grant and its value, for example `MissingGrantError: NetConnectGrant(host=192.168.1.10) required`. Grants are typed by what they cover: network destinations first, with database and executable grants as a later item.
4. Tests: an ungranted destination is refused; a granted one passes; the check reports how many calls it examined; a test asserts no denylist of destination patterns exists in the source.

**Not in this item:** the dispatcher's own GitHub and OpenRouter calls in `peer_review.py`. They are review machinery, not a task's egress, and need their own item. The destinations checked here are the ones a task graph node declares, checked before any worker starts, as its tools and outputs already are.


## Seventh objective: retrieval review

Opened 2026-09-20. Premise: **a retrieval stage that does nothing returns exactly what a working one returns, an ordered list.** A pass-through reranker, a top-k that never cuts, a grounding check that never fires: each reports work it did not do. `code-review-pro` and `analyze-repo` cannot tell. This objective asks somebody else's retrieval pipeline to prove its stages worked, as the third objective asked of our own checks.

### RR-001 — A retrieval stage proves it did its work

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` as `6c9912c` (PR #32, head `991dd34`) 2026-09-21 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian's instruction, recorded as `agent_merge_on_instruction`. Review `20260921-185258-991dd34-a61iuonn` (gemini/gemini-3.1-pro-preview, `no_blocking_findings`, 13/13 acceptance).

**Links introduced:** findings carry chunk and citation identifiers of the *analyzed* repository. They are names, not handles: they re-resolve only against that repository at the commit examined, and each finding records that commit.

1. The reviewer identifies the reranking stage and reports whether the post-rerank top result differs from the raw-similarity top result over a sample of queries. Where it cannot execute the pipeline, it reports the static finding (stage absent, output unused, score discarded) and says it did not execute, rather than a verdict it did not earn.
2. The reviewer names the line where top-k is cut before the model call, or reports that no cut exists.
3. The reviewer reports the absence of an explicit insufficient-grounding path as a finding, not a note.
4. Where the pipeline emits citations, each is checked to resolve to a chunk that was in the context window, not merely in the corpus. Where the reviewer cannot execute the pipeline, it reports that it did not execute and returns a counted SKIP. RR-002 provides the execution path.
5. Every check reports what it examined, per EV-001. A repository with no reranking stage returns SKIP for criterion 1, counted in the summary, never folded into a green line.
6. Tests: a fixture whose reranker returns input order unchanged is caught by 1; a fixture with no grounding fallback is caught by 3; a repository with no retrieval pipeline SKIPs every criterion and reports zero coverage rather than a pass.

### RR-002 — The probe runs the pipeline, or says it did not

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` as `6c9912c` (PR #32, head `991dd34`) 2026-09-21 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian's instruction, recorded as `agent_merge_on_instruction`. Review `20260921-185258-991dd34-a61iuonn` (gemini/gemini-3.1-pro-preview, `no_blocking_findings`, 13/13 acceptance).

**Links introduced:** for one run, the probe holds the chunk identifiers captured at the model-call boundary and the citation identifiers captured at the answer boundary, both belonging to the executed repository and that run only. They are compared and discarded; nothing is retained across runs.

1. Execution is opt-in and explicit. Absent a named entry point supplied by the operator, nothing is executed, and RR-001 criterion 4 stays a counted SKIP. The reviewer never decides on its own to run someone's code.
2. The probe captures the context window at the model-call boundary and the citations at the answer boundary, then asserts that every emitted citation identifies a chunk present in that captured context. A citation naming a corpus chunk that never entered the context is the finding.
3. It patches only those two boundaries.
4. It runs in a subprocess with no network granted by default. Egress is granted per TB-003, never filtered.
5. A run that captures zero context, or zero citations, is a FAIL naming the shortfall, never a pass. EV-001 governs the probe as it governs the static checks.
6. Tests: a fixture citing a chunk that was never in context is caught by 2; a fixture whose model boundary is never reached reports zero capture and fails by 5; a repository with no declared entry point executes nothing and returns SKIP.

## Eighth objective: the catalog imports

Opened 2026-09-21. Premise: **every check in this repository tests the scripts, and nothing tests the catalog they ship inside.** A skill whose frontmatter will not parse, or whose description the loader truncates, is broken when somebody installs the plugin. Ten of the 150 packages were not loadable while the suites were green; an outside compatibility assessment found them, not the gate.

### CI-001 — A shipped package is one a loader can read

**Status:** review. Merged unreviewed: relabelled from `done` on 2026-09-29 under the one release path, on Dorian's ruling; review folder found (20260921-134157), no release record, and none was fabricated (one prepared now would postdate the merge). Previously: merged to `main` in `d619c06` (PR #33, head `69ab8e4`) 2026-09-21 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian's instruction, recorded as `agent_merge_on_instruction`; review `20260921-134157-69ab8e4-oolg8b8m` (gemini/gemini-3.1-pro-preview, `no_blocking_findings`, 9/9 acceptance), `tests` green on the same head. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** none. The check reads files already in the tree and names them by repository path.

1. Every `SKILL.md` in the repository is examined: the frontmatter block parses, `description` is present, it is not empty, and it is at most 1024 characters.
2. The check asserts what the compatibility assessment asserted, and nothing it did not. No judgement of wording, tone or usefulness.
3. It reports what it examined, per EV-001, and a run that finds no packages FAILS rather than reporting a clean catalog it never located.
4. It is stdlib-only, as the rest of this repository's checks are, because the CI runner is a bare `setup-python` with no install step. The frontmatter reader covers the subset this catalog uses and reports anything outside it as unreadable. Its agreement with PyYAML was measured over all 150 real packages, text and length, before and after the fixes: 150 of 150, including the two files PyYAML rejects.
5. The ten failures are fixed in the same change. Where a description was shortened to fit the limit, its original text is preserved in the body under `## When this skill applies`, so no trigger wording is lost. Two of the ten were only malformed, never too long, and their text is unchanged.
6. Tests: a description over the limit, an empty one, an absent one, an unquoted colon in a plain scalar, and an unterminated block are each caught by a fixture; a description of exactly 1024 characters passes; and an empty tree FAILS rather than passing. The gate also runs the check over this repository's own 150 packages, not only over fixtures.

### CI-002 — A change ships to installs only if its version moves

**Status:** review. Merged unreviewed: relabelled from `done` on 2026-09-29 under the one release path, on Dorian's ruling; review folder found (20260922-105812), no release record (`peer_review.py release` was never run, and its packet names the cloud container's checkout); none fabricated, see `Taskade/Team Plugins/00 – Project Hub/note-2026-09-22-pr38-no-release-record.md`. Previously: merged to `main` in `e28909b` (PR #38, head `008099a`) 2026-09-22 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian's instruction; review `20260922-105812-008099a-vs3gs2t4` (openrouter-gemini/google/gemini-3.1-pro-preview, `no_blocking_findings`, 20/20 acceptance across XE-013 and CI-002, run as a recorded fallback with no cross-family corroboration). Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** none. The check resolves a diff range at run time, names plugins by their marketplace roster name and repository path, and retains nothing.

The version has three homes: a plugin's own `plugin.json`, its entry in `marketplace.json`, and the **top-level** `marketplace.json` version. The plugin manager gates "is there an update?" on the top-level one (DR-013, 2026-06-14), so bumping only the first two ships nothing. `test_manifest_consistency.py` checks only that the first two agree. DR-100.

1. For a given diff range, every plugin whose tracked files changed has a `version` in its `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` differing from its version at the base of that range. A plugin that did not change is not examined and is not required to move.
2. When any plugin changed in the range, the **top-level** `marketplace.json` version differs from its value at the base as well.
3. The roster is `marketplace.json`'s own entries. A plugin directory absent from the roster is reported by name rather than silently skipped.
4. A plugin new within the range has no base version and is not required to bump.
5. It reports what it examined per EV-001: the range it resolved, the changed-file count, and every plugin it checked by name. A range it cannot resolve FAILS rather than reporting a clean tree it never diffed.
6. Stdlib only, per DR-101. git is invoked as a subprocess; the CI runner has it from `actions/checkout`.
7. The workflow gives the check the history it needs. `actions/checkout` at its default depth of 1 has no base commit, so this is a workflow change as well as a script.
8. The catalog is clean when the check lands, per DR-102. The top-level version is bumped in this same change, which is what finally ships 0.12.0.
9. Tests: a changed plugin at an unmoved version FAILS; the same plugin with a moved version passes; a changed plugin whose own version moved while the top-level did not FAILS; a new plugin passes; a range touching no plugin passes while reporting zero examined; an unresolvable base FAILS; and the check runs over this repository's real history: across `6c9912c`, where it must catch `gstack-execution` at an unmoved 0.27.0, and across `d619c06..2293ff6`, where six consecutive merges sit at an unmoved top-level version and it must fail the three of them that changed a plugin while passing the three that changed none.
10. The check runs where a version can ship: on every pull request, against its base, and on every push to `main`. A push to any other branch is checked on its pull request, against the base it merges into, and the push run says it examined nothing and why (a goal item or `goal-finalize` branch measured from `main` would read red for the top-level line GO-004.1 forbids it to move). The workflow's token is read-only (amended 2026-10-04).

### XE-018 — A reviewer sees what the change names, and CI results the dispatcher fetched itself

**Status:** review. Merged unreviewed: relabelled from `done` on 2026-09-29 under the one release path, on Dorian's ruling; its review record and any release record were not found. Previously: merged to `main` in `b213899` (PR #50, head `53cb396`) 2026-09-24 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian's instruction (`record-release`: `agent_merge_on_instruction`); review `20260924-145914-09e6ee3-gtge526o` (codex/gpt-6-astra, round 2 `fixes_verified`, 10/10), `tests` green at the reviewed head (dispatcher-read). Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** `tests.ci_runs` in the packet names GitHub Actions runs by ID. The round record carries what the dispatcher fetched for each, including the run's `head_sha`; a run is evidence only for the head it ran at. A run ID that later points at nothing is reported as not read, never dropped.

1. The surface gains `dependencies/`: tracked files, not changed and not already callers, that a changed file names by repository-relative path or imports by a relative specifier (`./x`, `../x`, resolved with the extensions `.ts`, `.tsx`, `.js`, `.mjs`, `.py`, `.json` and `/index.ts`), and files an acceptance criterion names by path. A criterion that names a directory brings that directory's `package.json` or `pyproject.toml`. The set is bounded by the same cap as callers and the withheld count is reported.
2. `SURFACE.md` lists the dependencies and says why each is there, and the round record's `surface` block counts them.
3. The packet may carry `tests.ci_runs`, a list of `{repo, run_id}` where `repo` is a packet repository path or directory name. For each, the dispatcher reads the GitHub Actions run and its jobs through `github_get` and writes `evidence/ci-<run_id>.json` into the surface with `origin` `gate_output`: the run URL, `head_sha`, the reviewed head, `head_matches`, the conclusion, and every job and step with its conclusion. The builder never writes this file.
4. A run whose `head_sha` is not the reviewed head is still written, with `head_matches: false`, and `SURFACE.md` says it is not evidence for this head. A run that cannot be read is written with `read: false` and the error, and the round continues. Neither is silently dropped.
5. The prompt tells the reviewer that `evidence/` was fetched by the dispatcher from the CI provider, not written by the builder, and that a step with conclusion `success` in a run whose `head_matches` is true is evidence that step ran and passed at the reviewed head.
6. The round record carries an `evidence` block: runs requested, runs read, and runs whose head matched.
7. Tests in `test_review_evidence.py` drive `build_surface` over a temporary repository: a changed file naming another by path, a relative import, and a criterion naming a file and a directory each bring the named file into `dependencies/`; the cap reports what it withheld; a CI run is written with `head_matches` true and false as the fake GitHub answers; and a run GitHub refuses is written with `read: false` without raising.
8. The peer-review contract's packet table and scope section, and `/gstack-build`'s Step 5, name `tests.ci_runs` and the two new surface folders, each in one place.
9. `plugins/gstack-execution/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` moves from 0.34.0, and the top-level marketplace version moves, per CI-002.
10. `peer_review.py --selftest` passes, and `scripts/run_all_tests.py` reports what it examined with a nonzero count.

### XE-026 — jev_route's selftest examines something on every machine

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` as `87763e4` (PR #65, head `89e8d83`) 2026-09-25 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian's instruction (`record-release`: `agent_merge_on_instruction`). Review `20260925-135313-8b7cca5-ae00x9cr` (codex/gpt-6-astra, `fixes_verified`, 2 rounds; F1 fixed in `89e8d83` with an in-process test that makes the key loader and the real `_ask` raise), `tests` green at the reviewed head (dispatcher-read). **Links introduced:** the `--selftest --live` flag, which `smart-router/SKILL.md` and anyone running the live check now has to name; and exit code 3 for "skipped, nothing examined," which means the same thing it does for `tests/container/run.sh`.

1. `--selftest` makes no network call and loads no key. It swaps `_ask` for a stub and checks three things. First, the request `route()` builds: the model, a state carrying the query and the budget, and the four questions with their types and criteria. Second, parsing: canned gateway answers for each of the three `CHECKS` produce the expected decision and category, with each confidence landing in its band. Third, an answer missing `deliberate` or `category` raises `JevUnavailable`. It prints how many cases it examined, and it exits nonzero on any failure or on zero cases.
2. A new `test_jev_route.py` runs `--selftest` in a subprocess with a bogus `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY` set, `JEV_ENDPOINT` pointed at `http://127.0.0.1:9`, and `curl` off `PATH`, and it passes. CI runs this, so the proof doesn't depend on any one machine.
3. `--selftest --live` is the old three-call check. With no key it prints what it searched and exits 3, not 0. If the gateway fails, it prints the reason and exits 2 rather than a traceback. `run_all_tests.py` never passes `--live`.
4. `plugins/council/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` goes from 0.8.0 to 0.9.0, and the marketplace's council entry and top-level version move with it, per CI-002.
5. In CI, `scripts/run_all_tests.py` reports a nonzero count of what it examined, with no failures.

**Not in this item:** the live check's discrimination quality. It keeps its three cases.

### XE-025 — A criterion about a place the reviewer can't see needs evidence it can

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` as `b80464d` (PR #67, head `133a5c9`) 2026-09-26 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian's instruction (`record-release`: `agent_merge_on_instruction`). Review `20260926-135446-4639568-vnkf1k0p` (codex/gpt-6-astra, `fixes_verified`, 2 rounds; F1 fixed in `133a5c9`), opened with `--accept-off-repo-criterion` because criterion 1 names the pattern's own words; `tests` green at the reviewed head (dispatcher-read). Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** a link from a criterion to its evidence file. Each off-repo criterion is tied to a repo path at the reviewed head, and the review assumes that file is the evidence for that claim. The review state gets new fields recording that link and any override.

M-012 repeated M-007: a criterion about the Release Owner's Mac whose only evidence was the builder's `tests.results`, which the reviewer can't verify. The team-memory rule after M-007 didn't stop it, so this makes the rule a check at the point of action (DR-108).

1. `cmd_open` scans every acceptance criterion against one declared pattern, `OFF_REPO`. It matches words naming a place outside the repo: `Mac`, `macOS`, the Release Owner's or the builder's machine, `locally`, the vault. If a criterion matches and names no repo-relative file that exists at a packet repo's pinned head, the open is refused as `unevidenced_off_repo_criterion`. The refusal names each such criterion and the word it matched.
2. If a matching criterion names such a file, the review opens normally. Existence is checked with `git cat-file -e <head>:<path>`. The review state records each off-repo criterion and its evidence path.
3. `--accept-off-repo-criterion` opens the review anyway and records the override in the state, following the shape of XE-010's `--accept-narrow-packet`.
4. A new `test_off_repo_criterion.py` covers five cases: XE-022 criterion 3's exact text, with no evidence path, is refused; the same text naming `docs/evidence/XE-022-macos-run.md`, at a head where that file exists, opens; a named path that's missing at the head is refused; a criterion with no off-repo word opens; and the override opens and records itself.
5. `plugins/gstack-execution/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` moves from 0.39.2, and the top-level marketplace version moves with it, per CI-002.
6. In CI, `scripts/run_all_tests.py` reports a nonzero count of what it examined, with no failures.

**Not in this item:** whether the evidence file actually supports the claim; that stays the reviewer's call, and this check only guarantees there's something to read. It's a keyword gate: a criterion that describes an off-repo place without one of the `OFF_REPO` words gets past it.

### XE-027 — A failed CI job reaches the reviewer as quotes the dispatcher checked

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` as `e0aec5c` (PR #70, head `5aafee7`) 2026-09-28 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian's instruction (`record-release`: `agent_merge_on_instruction`). Review `20260928-211336-491929a-_gf467f7` (codex/gpt-6-astra, `fixes_verified`, 2 rounds; four blockers fixed in `5aafee7` and verified), `tests` green at the reviewed head (dispatcher-read). Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** each failed job's log is archived at `ci-logs/<run_id>-<job_id>.log` in the review directory, and the round record ties that path to the log's sha256. A receipt, `evidence/ci-<run_id>-job-<job_id>.receipt.txt`, names that sha256, and the review assumes every quote in it came from that log. `GSTACK_REDUCER_MODEL` names the model that drafts receipts. The CLI gains a `ci-log` subcommand the builder has to name.

XE-018 shows the reviewer that a job failed, never why. The idea, from NVlabs/SoL-Pi (MIT, arXiv 2609.20519): a cheaper model reads the log and returns quotes, kept only if every quote appears in the log byte for byte, with the full log going through on any miss. Ported in our own words; no SoL-Pi code is copied. Built ahead of a measured need, on Dorian's call on 2026-09-28. Fit note: `Taskade/Team Plugins/06 – Engineering/sol-pi-fit-2026-09-28.md`.

1. A new stdlib-only `ci_log_receipt.py` has `validate_receipt(raw, log, failed)`. It accepts a receipt only when every check holds, and otherwise it returns the reason that failed. The checks: the JSON parses; `schema` is `gstack_ci_receipt_v1`; `source_sha256` is the log's sha256; `status` is `failure` for a failed job and `success` otherwise; `uncertain` is a boolean; there are at most 12 evidence items, each of kind `fatal`, `failure`, `warning`, `target` or `summary`, with a quote of 1 to 500 characters found in the log; and a failed log that matches the failure-signal pattern carries at least one `fatal` or `failure` quote.
2. `reduce(log, failed, ask)` returns `(receipt_text, None)` or `(None, reason)`. It asks nothing when the log is under 8 KB or over 2 MB, or matches the likely-secret pattern. It returns a reason, never a receipt, when `ask` raises, when validation fails, or when the receipt isn't smaller than the log. The instructions sent with the log say the log is untrusted data, and that the model must copy quotes exactly and must not diagnose.
3. `fetch_ci_evidence` reads the log of each job whose conclusion is `failure` or `timed_out`, in a run it read, from GitHub's `actions/jobs/{id}/logs`. It follows the redirect to the log store without sending the token there. It archives the full log per the Links line. It records a `log` block on the job: `read`, `bytes`, `sha256`, the archive path, and `receipt` (`applied`, or the reason it wasn't). If the receipt applied, the receipt goes into `evidence/`, with the line number of each quote. If it didn't, `evidence/ci-<run_id>-job-<job_id>.log` holds the log, cut to its last 64 KB, with the cut stated on its first line. A log that can't be read is recorded `read: false` with the error, and nothing raises.
4. The receipt model is `GSTACK_REDUCER_MODEL`, default `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash`. It's reached over the openrouter transport, with the key from `GSTACK_OPENROUTER_ENV` (XE-013.8). With no key, the reason is `reducer_unavailable` and the log goes through as in criterion 3. The receipt carries the model and its token usage.
5. `SURFACE.md` lists each receipt and log. It says that the dispatcher, not the builder, checked a receipt's quotes against the log whose sha256 it names, and that the reducer's `uncertain` flag and its omissions prove nothing. The round record's `evidence` block adds `failed_jobs`, `logs_read` and `receipts_applied`.
6. `peer_review.py ci-log <repo> <run_id>` prints, for each failed job, the receipt or the capped log that criterion 3 would put in the surface, and says which.
7. A new `test_ci_log_receipt.py` covers the following. Validation accepts a good receipt, and rejects bad JSON, the wrong hash, the wrong status, a quote not in the log, too many items, and a failed log with no failure quote. `reduce` skips a short log and a log with a secret, and returns a reason when `ask` raises and when the receipt isn't smaller. With a fake GitHub (a local server named by `GSTACK_GITHUB_API`) and a stubbed `ask`, a failed job gets a receipt file; with `ask` failing, it gets the capped log file; with the log refused, it gets `read: false` and nothing raises. The token is never sent to the redirect target.
8. The peer-review contract's scope section and `/gstack-build`'s Step 3 each name the receipt and `ci-log`, each in one place.
9. `plugins/gstack-execution/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` moves from 0.40.0, and the top-level marketplace version moves with it, per CI-002.
10. In CI, `scripts/run_all_tests.py` reports a nonzero count of what it examined, with no failures.

**Not in this item:** harness-level mechanisms, which live in Claude Code and Codex, not in a plugin (SoL-Pi's edit-then-run fusion, observation handles and compaction economics), and reducing logs of jobs that passed.

### XE-028 — A router pick that nearly tied is not a pick

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` as `799fbc1` (PR #72, head `3479b9a`) 2026-09-29 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian's instruction (`record-release`: `agent_merge_on_instruction`). Review `20260928-220019-fc33ac5-ywfrcyp5` (codex/gpt-6-astra, `fixes_verified`, 2 rounds; F1 fixed in `3479b9a`), `tests` green at the reviewed head (dispatcher-read).

**Links introduced:** a `needs_review` list in `jev_route.py`'s output, naming each choice field the router returned as null because Jev's pick didn't clear the bar. Step 3 of `smart-router/SKILL.md` has to read it and fill those fields from Step 1's heuristic extraction.

`jev_route.py` took Jev's `category` and `protocol` picks at face value, so a 0.51 against 0.49 near-tie routed like a 1.0. The idea comes from wuyoscar/jev-skill (MIT), ported in our own words with no code copied; fit note `Taskade/Team Plugins/06 – Engineering/jev-skill-fit-2026-09-28.md`. Its calibration test (`evals/CALIBRATION_RESULTS.md`, 160 BIG-Bench Hard items, 2026-09-20): Jev's `[.9,1]` bin stated 0.98 on average and was right 89% of the time; its `[.8,.9)` bin was right 71% of the time.

1. For each choice field (`category`, `protocol`), `route()` accepts Jev's pick only when the pick is the highest-probability label, its probability is at least `CHOICE_MIN_PROBABILITY` (0.8), and it beats the next label by at least `CHOICE_MIN_MARGIN` (0.15). Otherwise the field is returned as `null` and named in `needs_review`. Probabilities that are missing, or don't name the pick, count as not clearing the bar.
2. A field missing from the answer entirely still raises `JevUnavailable`. A null from criterion 1 is not unavailability: `decision`, `confidence` and the other fields are returned as usual.
3. `needs_review` is always present, empty when every pick cleared the bar.
4. The offline selftest's canned answers carry choice probabilities. It adds three cases: a clear pick is used; a near-tie (0.52 against 0.48) returns `category: null` with `category` in `needs_review` while `decision` is unchanged; and a pick with no probabilities is treated as a near-tie.
5. The docstring's claim that a 0.9 is "right about nine times in ten", and the matching sentence in `smart-router/SKILL.md` Step 3, are replaced by jev-skill's measured numbers with a link to their source. Step 3 says to fill a `needs_review` field from Step 1's heuristic extraction and to record that field's source as heuristic.
6. council's README credits wuyoscar/jev-skill (MIT) for the idea and the measurement.
7. `plugins/council/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` moves from 0.9.0, and the marketplace's council entry and top-level version move with it, per CI-002.
8. In CI, `scripts/run_all_tests.py` reports a nonzero count of what it examined, with no failures.

**Not in this item:** the `deliberate` and `compound` booleans, which already have bands; `packet_coverage.mjs`; recalibrating with our own data.

### XE-029 — A fix round sees what round one saw, and an unmet criterion is not a malformed reply

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` as `c816ed0` (PR #85, head `fbde1b2`) 2026-09-30 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian's instruction (`record-release`: `agent_merge_on_instruction`). Review `20260930-100453-fbde1b2-1xfzuint` (`no_blocking_findings`, 14/14 across SM-005 and XE-029), `tests` green at the reviewed head (dispatcher-read). From proposals P2 to P7 of session review `20260930-095045-8bb8d311-109fbe`, approved by Dorian on 2026-09-30. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** `review_base` in a review's stored packet: each repository's base as it was at `open`, kept when a fix round moves `base` to the previous head.

1. `open` stores each repository's base as `review_base`. A fix round's surface is built from `review_base` to the new head: `CHANGE.diff`, `changed/`, callers and dependencies cover the whole change under review. The round's own delta, from the previous head, is written as `ROUND.diff`, and `SURFACE.md` says what each diff is. A packet stored before this item has no `review_base` and keeps the earlier behaviour.
2. A reply whose verdict is `blocking_findings`, with no blocking finding and at least one unmet acceptance row, is valid. The round's outcome is `blocking_findings`, or `rounds_exhausted` on the last round, and the round record names the unmet criteria. A clean verdict with a blocking finding, and a blocking verdict with no blocking finding and every criterion met, are still `malformed_output`.
3. A round is clean only when there is no blocking finding, no regression, and every acceptance row is met.
4. `dispatch` refuses, before starting anything, when the packet or `--ci-run` names a CI run and neither `GITHUB_TOKEN` is set nor `gh auth status` succeeds. The refusal names `agent_token.py exec` as the way to run it.
5. `merge-instruction` says on stderr that it printed the comment and did not post it, and gives the `agent_token.py api POST` command that posts it.
6. `/gstack-build` says that test counts in commits, packets and reports are quoted from the run's own output, never computed. Its release step says that only words telling the agent to merge count as an instruction; a general permission ("move forward", "full permission") does not.
7. Tests: a fix round's surface carries a file changed only before round 2; a blocking verdict with an unmet criterion and only a `separate` finding passes validation with outcome `blocking_findings`; the two real contradictions are still `malformed_output`; `dispatch` refuses with a named CI run and no token and no `gh`; `merge-instruction` warns on stderr.
8. `plugins/gstack-execution/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` moves from 0.44.0, and the top-level marketplace version moves, per CI-002.
9. `peer_review.py --selftest` passes, and `scripts/run_all_tests.py` reports what it examined with a nonzero count and no failures.

### XE-030 — A criterion the packet carries word for word is covered without asking a model

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` as `44fa797` (PR #87, head `847b639`) 2026-09-30 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian's instruction (`record-release`: `agent_merge_on_instruction`). Review `20260930-103118-847b639-hs_67rl5` (`no_blocking_findings`, 7/7), opened with coverage `covered` and no override, `tests` green at the reviewed head (dispatcher-read). Asked for by Dorian on 2026-09-30 ("Build the coverage scorer"). Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** none. Each scored criterion gains a `match` field, `verbatim` or `model`, inside the packet's existing `coverage` block.

1. Before any model call, each declared criterion is compared with the packet's acceptance criteria after whitespace is collapsed on both sides. A declared criterion whose whole text appears inside one packet criterion is scored `probability: 1` with `match: "verbatim"`, and it is not sent to the model. Every other criterion is scored by the model as before, with `match: "model"`.
2. When every declared criterion matches verbatim, the scorer makes no model call and needs no credential: the report is `checked`, with `input_tokens: 0` and `credential_source: null`.
3. When any criterion needs the model, a missing `ai` package, a missing credential and a refused call are still `broken`, `unavailable` and `unusable`, as XE-014 defines them.
4. The report counts how many criteria matched verbatim and how many the model scored, and the printed list marks each verbatim match.
5. Tests in `test_packet_coverage_run.py` run the script as a subprocess: a packet carrying every declared criterion is `checked` with no credential and no request reaching the stub gateway; in a mixed packet the stub is asked only about the criterion that isn't verbatim; a declared criterion with extra words after it in the packet criterion is verbatim; and a paraphrase is not.
6. `plugins/gstack-execution/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` moves from 0.45.0, and the top-level marketplace version moves, per CI-002.
7. In CI, `scripts/run_all_tests.py` reports a nonzero count of what it examined, with no failures.

**Not in this item:** changing the question the model is asked about criteria that aren't verbatim.

### XE-031 — A design doc is argued by two reviewers in turn before a human reads it

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` as `ba69f52` (PR #93) 2026-09-30 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian's instruction (`record-release`: `agent_merge_on_instruction`), review `20260930-164615-b32e10a-447eb6am`. Asked for by Dorian on 2026-09-30: one model writes the design doc, reviewer 1 reviews and hands to reviewer 2, reviewer 2 builds on that and hands back to the writer, who revises and records what changed, round after round, until all three agree or 20 rounds pass and the doc is written with the remaining dissent. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** each round record names the SHA-256 of the exact text reviewed, so an approval binds to one revision. Each finding gets an ID when first raised (`R<round>-<reviewer>-<n>`) and keeps it for life; later rounds, reviewer 2's stance and the writer's disposition cite that ID, and the loop's finding state is keyed by it. The `## Dissent` block names finding IDs that must exist in the log. The design-doc editor names the log's path.

This extends `/gstack-plan-review`'s existing bounded writer-and-critic loop rather than adding a second one; `/gstack-design-doc` had no review before the human saw the draft.

1. `/gstack-plan-review` takes `critics=2` and `target=design`. With `target=design` it reviews the sections a `/gstack-design-doc` draft adds or changes, plus Goal and Constraints for context, not the whole file. Constraints stay usable as review criteria: a finding that the draft violates a constraint, or can't satisfy one, is an ordinary finding. A finding that proposes changing a constraint is logged as a `policy proposal`, isn't argued in the loop, and is listed for Dorian in the final report. A violation the writer can't resolve goes to `## Dissent`.
2. Each round runs in this order: reviewer 1 reviews revision *n*; reviewer 2 reviews revision *n* **and** reviewer 1's critique, marking each of reviewer 1's new findings `agree`, `disagree` or `extend` with a reason, then adding its own; the writer revises and logs a disposition per finding ID (`accepted`, `rejected` with a reason, `deferred` with a reason, or `duplicate of <ID>`). That gives revision *n+1*.
3. Every reviewer reply accounts for every open finding in the loop's state, its own and the other reviewer's, as `resolved` or `still open`, citing the finding's original ID. A reply that omits an open finding is malformed. A material finding closes only when the reviewer who raised it marks it `resolved`; the writer's `rejected` or `deferred` leaves it open. A minor finding closes on the writer's disposition. `duplicate of <ID>` merges a reissued finding into the original, which stays open, so a reissue can't close anything or reset a stall.
4. Writer, reviewer 1 and reviewer 2 come from three different model families, chosen from the existing `REVIEWERS` table. If fewer than two non-writer families are reachable, the loop doesn't start: it says which were tried and why each failed. A same-family stand-in is never used (XE-005).
5. The loop has converged only when both reviewers return `VERDICT: APPROVED` on the same revision hash and the finding state holds no open material finding. An approval of an older revision doesn't count.
6. The cap for `target=design` defaults to 20 rounds. At the cap, the draft is published with a `## Dissent` section listing each open finding: its ID, who holds it, the round it was raised, and the writer's counter-position in one line.
7. A round where the revision hash and the finding state (every ID and whether it's open) are unchanged from the previous round is a stall. Two stalls in a row end the loop early with the same `## Dissent` section as criterion 6.
8. A reviewer that fails after the loop has started (timeout, quota, transport error, or a malformed reply that stays malformed after one retry of the same reviewer and model) ends the loop as `incomplete`. The latest draft, the finding state and the failure reason are kept and shown. `incomplete` never counts as approval, and no other reviewer or family is substituted for the one that failed.
9. The log is `DESIGN-REVIEW-LOG-<date>.md` beside the Taskade mirror, round by round: revision hash, each reviewer's findings verbatim inside the TB-002 enclosure, reviewer 2's stance on each reviewer 1 finding, the writer's dispositions, and the finding state after the round. Every reviewer call records its tokens and cost to the XE-012 run record.
10. `/gstack-design-doc` runs the loop between drafting and publishing the editor, and the editor shows the outcome (`converged at round N`, `cap reached`, `stalled` or `incomplete`), any policy proposals, and the log path above the markdown. The loop informs; it never approves. **Mark approved** stays Dorian's.
11. Tests with stub reviewers cover: convergence at round 2; reviewer 2 disagreeing with a reviewer 1 finding and the writer rejecting it, which leaves it open; an approval of a stale revision not counted; a reply that omits an open finding treated as malformed; a reissued finding merged as a duplicate, with the stall still detected; the cap writing `## Dissent` with valid finding IDs; two stalls ending the loop; reviewer 2 failing after reviewer 1 responds, ending `incomplete` with the draft and findings kept and no substitute run; a same-family reviewer refused before it runs; a constraint-violation finding kept while a policy proposal is set aside.
12. `gstack-execution` moves a minor version in `plugin.json` and its `marketplace.json` entry, and the top-level marketplace version moves (CI-002), by surgical text replace.
13. In CI, `run_all_tests.py` reports a nonzero count of what it examined, names the new test among the suites it ran, and reports no failures.

### XE-032 — A plugin name that contains a place word is not the place

**Status:** done. Merged with XE-031 in `ba69f52` (PR #93), same review and release record. Declared on Dorian's instruction of 2026-09-30 ("do all three in the order you choose"), after DG-001's review open was refused on its own version criterion. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** none. The pattern is a constant.

1. `OFF_REPO` matches a place word only when it stands alone: not joined to other word characters by a hyphen, slash, underscore or a dot followed by more word characters. `excalidraw-vault`, `vault/x`, `mac_os_x` and `mac.py` don't match. `the vault`, `the vault.`, `the vault's`, `locally,` and `on the Release Owner's Mac` still do.
2. `test_off_repo_criterion.py` gains cases for every example in criterion 1, including DG-001 criterion 9's exact text opening without the override, and XE-025's existing five cases still pass.
3. `plugins/gstack-execution/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` moves a version with XE-031's change, and the top-level marketplace version moves (CI-002).
4. In CI, `run_all_tests.py` reports a nonzero count of what it examined and no failures.

### XE-033 — A criterion about a whole directory is judged against the whole directory

**Status:** building. Declared 2026-10-05 during goal no-em-dashes-2 on moxywolf-website, under Dorian's standing instruction to build goal mode until it is done. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** none. The directory files live in the round's surface and are torn down with it; the round record keeps what was carried.

1. When an acceptance criterion names a directory tracked at the reviewed head, the dispatcher writes every tracked text file under it, read from git at that head and not from the working tree, into one surface file per directory under `dirs/`, each file headed `=== path ===`; binary files are skipped and counted, and a byte budget of 512,000 per review withholds and counts what doesn't fit.
2. `SURFACE.md` lists each directory file with its text, binary and withheld counts, says the dispatcher wrote it from git at the reviewed head, and says a withheld file is not evidence; the round record's surface stats carry the same counts as `criterion_dirs`.
3. A criterion that names only files, or no paths, carries no `dirs/`, and the existing surface tests still pass.
4. `test_review_surface.py` covers a named directory carried whole from the head with a binary skipped and an uncommitted edit absent, and a criterion with no directory carrying none.
