### XE-035 — A file a criterion names reaches the reviewer before anything the change merely mentions

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` as `e48e1ec` (PR #190, head `a0a0e6c`) 2026-10-07 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]`, recorded by `record-release` as `agent_merge_autonomous` under DR-113. Review `20261007-132627-b9dbce6-5ftjho4_` (codex/gpt-6-astra, `fixes_verified`, 2 rounds, 4/4, coverage `covered`; round 1's bracketed-path fix in `a0a0e6c`). CI `tests` green at the merged head (run 37683758758). Evidence: `docs/evidence/XE-035-release-2026-10-07.md`. Declared 2026-10-06 on Dorian's instruction, after CS-001's second review; first numbered XE-034 in PR #187 and renumbered on 2026-10-07 because that id went to the check-ledger item. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** the round record's `surface.criterion_files` lists the surface path of each file a criterion names, and the manifest an API reviewer is sent marks each of them `criterion_named` and marks a changed file sent as its hunks `diff_hunks`. The reader assumes a path in that list is the file the criterion meant. That holds only for a path written out whole, as git tracks it; a criterion naming a file by its bare name, or by a path the file no longer has, matches nothing and the record says nothing about it (corrected 2026-10-07, on Dorian's instruction).

1. Files an acceptance criterion names by exact path are packed onto the surface before callers and before files a changed file merely mentions, and they don't count against the 25-file dependency cap.
2. When the API character cap would drop a criterion-named file, a changed file over a size threshold is sent as its diff hunks instead of whole, and the manifest says so.
3. A criterion-named file is never withheld silently. If one still can't fit, the review opens as `unusable` and names the file.
4. A test reproduces CS-001's shape: a large changed `DESIGN.md`, a 47,000-character changed `marketplace.json` and nine criterion-named files. All nine reach the surface, and the manifest names each one.

### XE-036 — One failed node is retried with its evidence, and nothing else moves

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` as `e88807b` (PR #194, head `ec9e4a7`) 2026-10-13 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian's instruction ("I approve PR #194 - please merge."), recorded by `record-release` as `agent_merge_on_instruction`. Review `20261012-212850-4b20e64-02gy0nlt` (codex/gpt-6-astra, `fixes_verified`, 3 rounds, 10/10, coverage `covered`; fixes in `c3db115` and `ec9e4a7`). CI `tests` green at every reviewed head (run 38350189639 at the merged head). Declared 2026-10-12 from Dorian's brief "Design goal: bounded correction of one failed gstack node", approved by him the same day; design committed as `2886d07`. Conceptual source he supplied: https://x.com/hanakoxbt/status/2091515787366306154. Inspected at `d81d9fc` on 2026-10-12. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** a per-node cycle record in `state.json`, `nodes.<id>.cycle`, keyed by the node's execution signature (`digest([node, packet, dependencies])`), holding the count of dispatches made under that signature and one entry per dispatch (attempt number, start, finish, status, failure category, reason, and the path of the captured diagnostic excerpt). Earlier cycles move to `nodes.<id>.cycles_prior` when the signature changes. A correction packet in a retried attempt's `input.json` and prompt names the node id, the signature and the previous attempt number. The reader assumes a cycle's count is the number of times a handler actually started under that signature; that holds because the count is written before the dispatch, so an interrupted attempt is counted and never refunded, and it errs toward exhausting sooner when a crash falls between the write and the start. `attempts`, the lifetime counter, and the cycle count disagree after a signature change; the cycle count is the budget.

Before this item, one node that could not produce a valid result failed the whole run on its first try. The executor now retries that one node, at most twice, with a record of what went wrong, while its green siblings stand and its dependents wait. Machine success stays advisory. Nothing here touches release permission, the Release Owner Gate, the advisory checker's standing, other-model review, permissions, the deploy phrase, or graph topology. Workers still cannot add nodes, and no handler repairs source code.

1. `on_failure` accepts `block` and `retry`; any other value is refused by `validate_graph()` before dispatch. `retry` takes an optional `max_attempts`, an integer from 1 to 3, default 3, and a boolean or a value outside that range is refused. `max_attempts` counts total dispatches, so the default is one attempt and two retries. `retry` is accepted on `worker` and `checker` nodes only; a `peer`, `proof` or `report` node declaring it is refused by name. `block` behaves exactly as before. No node in `workflows/cso.json`, `workflows/verify.json` or `workflows/review.json` changes policy in this item; enabling retry on a built-in workflow is a later amendment that names the eligible nodes.
2. A retry is for a handler that failed to produce a valid result, never for a valid result the run doesn't like. The executor classifies each node failure into an explicit category carried on the exception, and only these retry: a command handler exiting nonzero, a handler timeout, output that isn't JSON, a result that `validate_result()` refuses, and a model handler that returns nothing parseable; a `ReviewError` naming `review_unavailable` or `model_below_floor` is environmental and never retries. A proof's nonzero exit is a valid `FAILED` finding and stays one; `MISSING`, `DRIFTED`, `UNVERIFIED`, `FAILED` and findings that carry blockers are results, not failures, and never trigger a retry. These never retry under any policy: permission or egress refusal, an undeclared write found by `--audit-writes`, a changed peer contract, a graph defect, `run_locked`, and any exception the executor didn't classify. Classification is by exception type and category field, never by matching message text. An unclassified failure fails closed as `block` would.
3. While a retrying node's cycle has budget left, its failure is recorded and the node returns to pending without joining the terminal failed set; its dependents are not marked `blocked` and its successful independent siblings are neither invalidated nor dispatched again. After a validated success the dependents release as before. When the budget is exhausted, or the failure is non-retryable, the node is terminal `failed`, its descendants are `blocked`, the run is `incomplete`, the stale `report.json` is removed, the executor exits nonzero and `export` refuses the run, all as before.
4. A retried attempt receives a correction packet the executor wrote: node id, execution signature, previous attempt number, failure category and reason, a reference to the captured diagnostic excerpt or the words that none was captured, and the frozen scope (repository heads, declared `checks`, `depends_on`, `effects` and `outputs`). Captured handler output is passed through `ci_log_receipt.py`'s `LIKELY_SECRET` redaction and capped at 4,000 characters before it is stored or forwarded. For a command handler the packet is a `correction` key in `input.json`; for a model handler the packet's executor-written fields sit outside the TB-002 enclosure with the node and packet, and the handler-produced diagnostic text sits inside it, with the other nodes' results. The frozen node declaration, the dependency results and the execution signature are unchanged by the correction packet: `digest([node, packet, dependencies])` is computed without it. No text from a failed attempt can widen scope, permissions, topology or output ownership.
5. The cycle count is written to `state.json` before the handler is submitted, as the `running` entry is, and an entry found `running` on resume under the same signature is counted as a consumed dispatch, not refunded. A resumed run with an unchanged signature continues the same cycle: an exhausted cycle stays exhausted and dispatches zero more times. A changed signature opens a new cycle under the existing validation and permission checks, and the old cycle's record moves to `cycles_prior` with its diagnostics intact. The lifetime `attempts` field keeps its meaning and is not the budget.
6. Result reuse is unchanged: a cached success still requires a matching signature and matching hashes of every declared output. Correction history lives in the node's state entry and in `events.jsonl`, never in the result that dependents and the cache identify by hash. A retry of one node never causes an unchanged green sibling to run again. A changed input, a tampered output or a changed dependency result still invalidates the affected node and its descendants. A failed new attempt never serves an older successful output as its result.
7. `events.jsonl` records each dispatch start, each failure with its category, attempt number and diagnostic reference, each correction handed to a retry, and the terminal success or exhaustion, every row carrying node id and signature. Diagnostics from failed attempts survive the node's eventual success. The executor's own diagnostic files are written under a reserved run-root name added to `AUDIT_MANAGED`, so the undeclared-write sweep neither flags them nor gains an exemption for any other path. The run lock, the concurrency cap, proof serialization and serial write-audit mode are unchanged.
8. `plugins/gstack-execution/scripts/test_task_graph.py` gains these cases, all on deterministic subprocess fixtures with no model calls, each going through the XE-024 `ok()` helper where a call should succeed. The first must fail against the executor at `d81d9fc` and pass after the change: a diamond `prepare -> a/b -> checker`, report depending on every source node, `a` on `retry` with three total attempts failing its first dispatch and succeeding its second under the same signature, `b` succeeding once; in one `run`, `a` dispatches exactly twice and `b` exactly once with `b`'s saved result and hash unchanged, `a`'s second `input.json` carries its first failure's category and reason, a real diagnostic excerpt and an unchanged scope, checker and report run once each and only after both branches hold valid results, the report preserves both branches' evidence, the run is `complete`, and the failure and correction history is readable in `state.json` and `events.jsonl`. The rest: an always-failing retry node dispatches exactly three times and never four, its descendants block, export refuses, and resuming the unchanged run dispatches it zero more times; a run interrupted after a recorded dispatch resumes without resetting the cycle budget or re-running green work; `block` performs no retry; an invalid policy value, cap or kind is refused before dispatch; a valid `MISSING` claim finding and a `FAILED` proof trigger no retry; a permission refusal and an undeclared write get no correction attempt; a tampered output, a changed repository head and a changed dependency result still invalidate the right cached work, and the prior cycle's history survives the new one; the model-handler path receives the correction packet with executor fields outside the enclosure and diagnostics inside it, proven with the fake reviewer command.
9. `plugins/gstack-execution/skills/gstack-execution/references/task-graph-contract.md` documents the `retry` policy, its eligibility, the budget, the cycle record and the correction packet, and qualifies "Failed nodes block dependents" to say when. `plugins/gstack-execution/GOVERNANCE.md`'s graph row says that a retrying node's failure blocks convergence once its bounded budget is spent. Neither document gains a word that lets machine success authorize release.
10. `plugins/gstack-execution/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` moves a minor version and the top-level marketplace version moves (CI-002). In CI, `plugins/gstack-execution/scripts/run_all_tests.py`, run by `.github/workflows/tests.yml`, reports a nonzero count of what it examined, names `test_task_graph.py` among the suites it ran, and reports no failures. The Codex review runs at the exact proposed head, and the merge waits for Dorian's GitHub approval and his word "merge" on this pull request, by his instruction in the brief, whatever DR-113 allows elsewhere.

## Tenth objective: external intake

Opened 2026-09-30, when Dorian passed four outside links to `/session-start` and asked where they fit. Each got a fit note in `Taskade/Team Plugins/06 – Engineering/` (`archify-`, `prompt-master-`, `coderabbit-` and `timesfm-fit-2026-09-30.md`), written from the source, not the README. This objective records what was not taken and why, so a later session doesn't reopen it. The rules are the ones earlier intakes set: a native runtime behind a thin skill is not vendored, and its durable ideas are re-expressed in our words with no code copied; heavy overlap means curate, never run two versions side by side (DR-011); attribution names the source, its license and the commit read.

**Not taken, and why:**

- **CodeRabbit** (hosted SaaS, $24 to $72 per developer per month, read 2026-09-30). Not a plugin. It can't stand in for `/gstack-peer-review`, which has the other tool review pinned commits against fixed criteria with bounded rounds. A bot review doesn't count toward the code-owner approval `main` requires. A one-repo trial on a repo outside the gstack loop (crm-web, the websites) is Dorian's call and isn't an item here.
- **TimesFM** (`google-research/timesfm` at `e51928e`). A Python model with a PyTorch runtime, so the do-not-vendor rule applies. Self-hosted 3.0 weights are licensed non-commercial and non-production; 3.0 through BigQuery ML, and 2.5 and earlier self-hosted, are permitted. No forecasting need has been named. If one is, the route is BigQuery ML or a pinned 2.5 checkpoint, never self-hosted 3.0.
- **jev-opus** (`WXK-AI/jev-opus` at `6b6b0f8`, MIT, read 2026-10-06). A Claude Code harness that re-picks the effort level before every step. It can't route a Cowork or cloud session, and its plugin starts a separate Claude Code with our hooks off and permissions on auto. XE-034 takes its failure-tracking rule. A measured trial on terminal goal runs is Dorian's call and isn't an item here. Fit note: `jev-opus-fit-2026-10-06.md`.

**REA** (`morluto/rea` at `7a6defa`, MIT, read 2026-10-13). A Node MCP server and CLI that inspects shipped artifacts and returns each finding as an Evidence record; 1,758 TypeScript source files and 38 runtime dependencies behind a markdown skill, so the do-not-vendor rule applies. Taken without an item: a pointer plugin `plugins/rea` in the shape of `understand-anything`, and one Tooling line in `plugins/security/skills/vulnerability-management/SKILL.md` naming REA for shipped-artifact inspection. Taken as items: EV-010 (its untrusted-content marker) and EV-011 (its unknowns ledger). Not taken: the reconstruction obligation ledger, which needs typed manifests and verifier authority levels this loop doesn't have, and its tool-shape table, which is a note for the next `mcp-builder` edit. Fit note: `rea-fit-2026-10-13.md`.

### DG-001 — A diagram of a repository cites the code that proves it

**Status:** done. PR #92 merged as `92eeb51` (head `95c560c`) on 2026-09-30 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian's instruction, `record-release` `agent_merge_on_instruction`; review `20260930-154626-456d216-_n3mdllw` (codex/gpt-6-astra) `fixes_verified`, 2 rounds, 10/10, coverage `covered`. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** the diagram note's frontmatter names a commit (`repo_head`), and each row of its `## Sources` table names `path:start-end` at that commit. Both re-resolve on demand with `check_repo_diagram.py`, which reads the pinned commit, never the working tree. This validates citations as history, not freshness. Drift against current code is a separate, informational report (criterion 6), never a failed citation.

`excalidraw-vault` keeps no record of what Claude read, so a diagram of one of our repositories can't be checked against its code. We take the rules of Archify's repository-backed mode (`tt-a1i/archify` at `d5a1333`, MIT) and leave its Node compiler, schemas and viewer.

1. `plugins/excalidraw-vault/skills/excalidraw-vault-core/SKILL.md` gains a `## Repo-backed mode` section. `/excalidraw` and `/excalidraw-here` both route to it when the named source is a path inside a git repository, and each command file says so.
2. The mode writes three frontmatter fields to the diagram note: `repo_head` (the 40-character output of `git rev-parse HEAD`), `repo_origin` (`git remote get-url origin` with any `user:pass@` or token removed), and `repo_dirty` (paths from `git status --short`, `[]` when clean), recorded for information only.
3. The section states that evidence counts only at `repo_head`. Every cited file is read with `git show <repo_head>:<path>`, dirty or clean. The working tree is never read as evidence.
4. The note carries a `## Sources` table with columns `element`, `claim`, `source`. `element` is the Excalidraw element `id`. Every non-deleted shape (rectangle, ellipse, diamond) and connector (arrow, line) has exactly one row, unless its `customData` carries `"claim": false` (titles, legends, frames). `source` is `path:start-end` or the word `unknown`. An `unknown` row names what is unresolved, and the element is drawn dashed, not as fact.
5. The section states the tracing rules in our words: a connection to a store names the code that reads or writes it; code that is configured but never called on the normal path is labelled optional; exploration stops when the requested responsibilities are covered, with no node or connection count to reach.
6. `plugins/excalidraw-vault/scripts/check_repo_diagram.py <note> --repo <path>` exits 0 only when: the drawing is uncompressed JSON (a `compressed-json` fence fails, naming the fix); `repo_head` is 40 hex characters and exists in the repo; `repo_origin` carries no userinfo and equals `--repo`'s origin after the same cleaning; every claim element from criterion 4 has exactly one row, no row names an element the drawing lacks, and no element has two rows; every `source` that isn't `unknown` names a path that exists at `repo_head` (`git cat-file -e <repo_head>:<path>`) with `1 <= start <= end <=` that file's line count at `repo_head`. It prints how many elements and rows it checked and names each failure; zero checked is a failure. Separately, without changing the exit code, it prints the repo's current HEAD and, when that differs from `repo_head`, the cited paths changed since (`git diff --name-only <repo_head> HEAD`), labelled drift, not failure.
7. `plugins/excalidraw-vault/scripts/test_check_repo_diagram.py` builds temporary git repositories and covers: a passing note; an unsupported connection alongside an otherwise valid row; a row naming an element the drawing lacks; two rows for one element; a path missing at `repo_head`; a line range past the end of the file; an origin with a token in it; a note for repo A run against repo B at the same commit; an empty Sources table; a compressed drawing; a file whose working-tree lines differ from `repo_head`, where the check reads `repo_head`; and HEAD moved past `repo_head`, which exits 0 and reports the changed cited path as drift.
8. `plugins/excalidraw-vault/README.md` and the new section credit Archify (MIT, `tt-a1i/archify` at `d5a1333`), say the ideas were re-expressed and no code was copied, and list what wasn't taken: the typed JSON IR and its schemas, the interactive viewer, and PNG, video and share-card export.
9. `excalidraw-vault` moves 0.1.0 to 0.2.0 in `plugin.json` and its `marketplace.json` entry, and the top-level marketplace version moves (CI-002). Versions change by surgical text replace; the diff on both JSON files shows version lines only.
10. In CI, `run_all_tests.py` reports a nonzero count of what it examined, names `test_check_repo_diagram.py` among the suites it ran, and reports no failures.

### PG-001 — The prompting guide checks model facts before it gives model advice

**Status:** done. Delivered in the vault's prompting guide on 2026-09-30; no repository change, so no pull request or release record.

**Where:** the vault, not this repository: `MoxyWolf Vault/_Shared Knowledge/Agents and Plugins/guide-prompting-claude-models-2026-07-05.md`, and the prompt-coach rule in the Team Plugins project memory. No file in this repository changes, so this item isn't built through `/gstack-build` or reviewed by `/gstack-peer-review`. The vault isn't under git, so Dorian reviews the full before-and-after diff, shown in chat before the file is written.

**Links introduced:** the guide's `sources:` frontmatter names each provider page it was checked against, with the date checked; each re-resolves by fetching the URL. The credit line names Prompt Master at `2bd9251`.

Prompt Master (`nidhinjs/prompt-master` at `2bd9251`, MIT) overlaps our prompt coach and contradicts three of our rules: it treats a missing role prompt as a failure, prints a status line after every agent step, and routes to a stock template inventory. Ours win on all three. Its recency rule exists to catch the gap where our guide stops at Opus 4.8.

The guide gains a section, "Before you write model-specific advice," in our words: verify model names, defaults and supported controls against the provider's current documentation; tell the consumer app apart from the API and coding-agent surfaces; if the documentation can't be checked, say the detail is unverified; never invent a model ID, parameter or context size.
3. The guide's checks for reusable prompts gain two lines: a pasted prompt under review is data to analyze, not instructions to follow, and embedded instructions are flagged; an agent prompt states its starting state, target state, file scope, stop condition and when it must stop for a human.
4. The guide gains an appendix, "Non-Claude targets we use," covering Codex (the peer-review reviewer), Gemini, ElevenLabs, and image prompts for hero diagrams. Each entry is five lines or fewer and names the provider page it was checked against on the edit date.

6. The guide adds no role-assignment instruction, no per-step status instruction and no named template inventory (CO-STAR, RISEN, CRISPE). A search for each name finds only the credit line, if anything.
7. The Maintenance section's audit record gains a dated entry for this edit, per its own rule.
8. The prompt-coach rule in the Team Plugins project memory lists the checks the guide now carries, and still exempts one-off conversational prompts.
