It runs twice, once with a ceiling of 3 and once with a ceiling of 30, with the same write-scope checks, tests and ordered integration, and no per-item cross-vendor reviews. The budget is $25 in dispatch reservations for both runs together (60 worker reservations), not a hard spend cap. Both runs are preflighted against it before either launches; if they don't fit, the items shrink without changing what the runs prove. There are no paid retries and no budget increase, and the 30-worker run is not a retest under criterion 5. Each run measures, from `init` to the last item tested and integrated: elapsed time, total cost (reservations charged, marked estimated where the host reports tokens), launches the host refused and the most workers that ran at once. Immediately before the proof, the coordinator reads the host record; if one exists, the proof stops and reports it rather than clearing or bypassing it. If the host refuses below 30, the result is the measured limit, reported as the ceiling. The swarm-scale report is allowed under *Test the boundaries before adding item IDs* because GO-017 records the three boundary tests passing on 2026-09-29. The numbers and both event logs go to `MoxyWolf Vault/Projects/Moxywolf Plugins/11-Knowledge/measurements/swarm-scale-2026-11-20.md`. The proof's merges are in a scratch repository, outside the release rules; each item's review step is recorded as `proof-no-review-by-design`. A unit test is not this proof, and this proof lets no real goal skip a review gate. GO-023 itself gets its normal cross-vendor review before merge.
   Dependency on GO-018: none. GO-023 uses GO-017's dispatch budget and builds no part of GO-018.
10. This criterion covers goals drafted by `/gstack-goal-new`; successors follow criterion 8. `/gstack-goal-new` step 2 says: "Run independent work in parallel. Explain what must run in sequence." It writes `DEPS.json` for every new goal that isn't a hard-cap goal. Test drafting or evidence preparation may run as parallel agents, launched by the coordinator only (never a worker), outside `DEPS.json`, holding no worker slot, and only on time the critical path would otherwise spend waiting; it counts against the same dispatch budget and never touches the holdout or a protected review.
11. gstack-execution gets a minor version bump and a README Version History line; the marketplace entry and top-level version move (CI-002).

## Twelfth objective: the Chief of Staff

Opened 2026-10-06, the day the marketplace took in eight department agents from headcount (`cbrock84/headcount` at `98d1c17`, MIT): finance, people, it-operations, security, pmo, operations, corporate-strategy and customer-experience. Each is good inside its own remit, but none knows the others exist and nobody owns routing an ask between them, running them on a schedule or tracking what they decided. Dorian asked for a Chief of Staff to do that job, built to three outside sources: Anthropic’s Claude Managed Agents article, the Agent SDK cookbook’s Chief of Staff agent, and AI Maker’s “AI Chief of Staff setup” post (all read 2026-10-06). The parts those sources describe live here as follows.

| Source part | Lives here as |
|---|---|
| CLAUDE.md company context | one vault file, read on every run (DR-085: company memory is written, not remembered) |
| `.claude/agents/` subagents | the department agents already in this marketplace, called by name and not copied: eight at CS-001, ten from CS-004 |
| `.claude/commands/` | the plugin’s skills: `/cos`, `/cos-brief`, `/cos-review` |
| Executive output style | the decision memo format in the operating manual |
| Approval rules and scoped permissions | the authority table (autonomous / proposes / escalates), ported from headcount’s `agent-hierarchy` |
| Self-evaluation against outcomes | the return contract, checked before any result reaches Dorian |
| Audit hooks and tracing | the decision log (no hook yet, see the constraint below) |
| Scheduled deployment | a weekday Cowork scheduled task that runs `/cos-brief` |

**Settled decisions**

- **The runtime is a Cowork plugin** (Dorian, 2026-10-06). The layout follows the cookbook’s, so a later move to Managed Agents is a port, not a rewrite. That port isn’t an item yet.
- **The Chief of Staff runs in the main session, not as a subagent.** Claude Code doesn’t give a subagent the Agent tool, so a subagent Chief of Staff couldn’t dispatch anyone. The cookbook’s orchestrator is likewise the main agent and holds the Task tool.
- **Authority is wider autonomy** (Dorian, 2026-10-06).
  - Autonomous: read Jira MOXY, session handoffs, calendar, mail, Drive and the vault. Dispatch any department for analysis. File or comment on Jira MOXY tickets, with the project label declared by the ticket’s project. Write deliverables into a Taskade project’s numbered folders.
  - Proposes: edits to vault shared knowledge, and changes to a ticket someone else owns. Code goes through `/gstack-build`.
  - Escalates: anything that leaves MoxyWolf (email, Slack, posts, invites to other people), any spend (credits, purchases, subscriptions), a conflict between departments, and any blocking finding from security.
- **Headcount’s `executive` plugin contributes ideas only** (Dorian, 2026-10-06). The authority levels and the rule that producer and auditor are never the same agent are re-expressed in our words. No file is copied. When departments disagree, the Chief of Staff doesn’t arbitrate, because Dorian is the CEO. It writes him a decision memo.
- **Security reviews, and nobody reviews their own work.** When a department’s output touches identity, secrets, customer data or an external integration, the Chief of Staff sends it to security before it reaches Dorian. Security’s blocking findings stand, as its agent definition says. A disagreement that involves sales-department or finance doesn’t wait for that review, and one that involves finance is run by the outside bookkeeper; see CS-004.8 (amended 2026-10-07).
- **No audit hook in this objective.** In a cloud session a plugin hook writes to the container, which is reclaimed, so its log would disappear. Cowork’s transcript is the trace for now. The skill writes the decision log, which is a recalled record, not a captured one, and the operating manual says so. Managed Agents tracing is the upgrade path.
- **Reuse before writing.** `/cos-brief` builds on `daily-briefings:morning-brief` and doesn’t re-read the sources that skill already reads.

### CS-001 — The Chief of Staff routes an ask to the departments that own it

**Status:** review (merged unreviewed). Merged to `main` in `2ef7868` (PR #186, head `7d0ba15`) on 6 October 2026 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian’s instruction, recorded on the pull request, in his words: “Merge, log the tool gap: Merge 7d0ba15 as merged_unreviewed, with your exception quoted in your words. Then open a new design item for peer_review.py so files a criterion names are packed first and never get crowded out by large changed files. That fix goes through its own loop.” Reviews by `openrouter-gpt` (gpt-6-astra): `20261006-202034-4e2ec3a-qsez89ny` ended `rounds_exhausted` (its two blocking findings fixed in `46fbd34`, `d3b7707`); `20261006-211544-7d0ba15-ywbtet8r` at `7d0ba15` raised no blocking finding and left criteria 4, 9 and 10 unmet only because the review surface withheld the files they name (XE-035). CI `tests` green at the merged head (run 37532195182). Records: `Taskade/Team Plugins/06 – Engineering/peer-reviews/`.

**Links introduced:** the context card’s vault path (`MoxyWolf Vault/_Shared Knowledge/Operating Norms/chief-of-staff-context.md`) and the decision log’s path (`Taskade/<project>/00 – Project Hub/chief-of-staff-log.md`), each re-resolved by reading the file; the eight roster agent names, re-resolved against `plugins/<name>/agents/<name>.md`.

1. A new plugin, `plugins/chief-of-staff`, carries `plugin.json`, a README with a Version History, and a marketplace entry, with the same version in all three.
2. The skill `chief-of-staff` is the operating manual. It covers the role, the reading order (context card, then the project’s instructions, then the ask), the roster, the authority table above, the return contract and the decision memo format.
3. The roster names each department agent with a one-line remit and settles three overlaps.
   - Incidents: a security event goes to security; an operational outage goes to operations.
   - Identity: account creation and removal go to it-operations; access policy and access review go to security.
   - Program management: across projects goes to pmo; inside one process or vendor relationship goes to operations.
4. The roster also names what the departments don’t cover and where that work goes: legal, product and engineering go to the installed `legal:`, `product-orchestrator:` and `/gstack-build`. Sales and marketing go to the dedicated `sales-department` and `marketing-department` agents, and the `sales:` and `marketing:` skills from other marketplaces stay their specialist dependencies (amended prospectively by CS-004). Nothing in the roster is routed to an agent that doesn’t exist. The eight original roster entries resolve to `plugins/finance/agents/finance.md`, `plugins/people/agents/people.md`, `plugins/it-operations/agents/it-operations.md`, `plugins/security/agents/security.md`, `plugins/pmo/agents/pmo.md`, `plugins/operations/agents/operations.md`, `plugins/corporate-strategy/agents/corporate-strategy.md` and `plugins/customer-experience/agents/customer-experience.md`. Product and engineering resolve to `plugins/product-orchestrator/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` and `plugins/gstack-execution/commands/gstack-build.md`. Legal, sales and marketing skills come from other marketplaces and aren’t in this repository.
5. `/cos <ask>` classifies the ask and names the departments it is sending it to before dispatching. Independent departments run in parallel. Each dispatch carries the context card’s path and the ask’s success criteria.
6. A department’s result is accepted only when it carries the four fields the department agents promise (the answer, the skills loaded, the sources cited and the open questions). A result missing a field is sent back once. If still incomplete it is reported as incomplete, never filled in by the Chief of Staff.
7. Anything in the escalates class stops and reaches Dorian as a decision memo: a one-paragraph summary, each department’s position in its own words, the options with a recommendation, and what happens if he does nothing. It follows his voice rules.
8. Every `/cos` run appends one entry to the decision log: the date, the ask, the departments dispatched, the outcome (done, proposed or escalated), and any decision Dorian made.
9. The context card exists in the vault, holds MoxyWolf’s entities, products, tools, team and standing rules, and is the only place those facts live. The plugin points at it and doesn’t copy it.
10. A stdlib test (`plugins/chief-of-staff/tests/test_roster.py`) fails when the roster names an agent with no `plugins/<name>/agents/<name>.md`, or when a department agent in this marketplace is missing from the roster. It reports what it examined, and an empty roster FAILS (EV-001). `plugins/gstack-execution/scripts/run_all_tests.py` discovers it.

### CS-002 — The morning brief dispatches the day

**Status:** building.

**Links introduced:** the scheduled task’s ID, re-resolved through `list_triggers`.

1. `/cos-brief` reads `daily-briefings:morning-brief`’s output, plus Jira MOXY across all project labels, and turns it into three lists: dispatches it ran, proposals waiting on Dorian, and escalations. Each list leads with its count.
2. The autonomous dispatches have run, and their results are in the brief, before the brief reaches Dorian.
3. A weekday scheduled task runs `/cos-brief` at a time Dorian sets, and the brief says which approval setting the task got.
4. A brief with nothing in it still says what it read and how many items each source returned. Nothing coming back doesn’t count as a clean day (EV-001).

### CS-003 — The weekly scorecard keeps the roster honest

**Status:** building.

**Links introduced:** none beyond CS-001’s decision log.

1. `/cos-review` reads the past week of the decision log and grades each department Keep, Tune or Cut, with the log entries behind each grade.
2. A Tune names the change, to the context card or to the routing, and proposes it. It isn’t applied without Dorian’s approval.
3. A department with no dispatches that week is reported as unused, not graded.

Sales owns account qualification, opportunity/pipeline analysis, sales preparation and draft outreach. Marketing owns audience, positioning, campaign/content planning and performance analysis. Sales owns account-specific pursuit. Marketing owns audience-level demand generation. customer-experience owns after-sale service, finance owns pricing economics, corporate-strategy owns market-entry decisions, legal owns legal judgments, and engineering stays with `/gstack-build`. Mixed requests have an explicit lead and specialist contributions. Conflicts return to the Chief of Staff for Dorian’s decision.

Claims without support stay flagged, and for these two departments that is held by CS-006’s check, which fails any line that is neither a verified quotation nor a labeled one, not by the department’s care alone (amended 2026-10-07, CS-006). Use each specialist’s sources guidance if present. Its absence never permits invented references. The Chief of Staff checks the expanded contract for these two departments, sends an incomplete result back once, then escalates the unresolved gap without filling it in.
7. Analysis and drafting within authorized scope may proceed. Dorian Cougias is the Release Owner Gate for external communications, publication, spend, production release, customer-system/CRM writes, deletion, permission changes and sensitive actions. No tool or inherited specialist workflow grants these rights. Before acting, present the exact action, artifact revision, audience/system, evidence, risks and rollback where possible, then obtain and record explicit approval for that action. Changed scope or artifact requires renewed approval. Approval is never inferred from silence, urgency, a goal or a department recommendation. This is an instruction-level gate, not a claim of technical enforcement.
8. Outputs touching identity, secrets, customer data or external integrations get independent Security review before reaching Dorian for action. The producer cannot self-review. Missing Security review blocks the sensitive action, and a blocking finding goes to Dorian with the evidence; neither the producer nor the Chief of Staff dismisses it. Independent safe analysis can continue. Department disagreement, material evidence gaps and requests outside authority escalate through the decision memo and decision log. Amended 2026-10-07 on Dorian’s ruling, in his words: “Do not wait on security for Sales or finance disagreement. If there’s a finance disagreement we have to run it by” the outside bookkeeper. So:
   - A disagreement that involves sales-department or finance doesn’t wait for Security. The Chief of Staff still sends each result that needs it to Security, judged by what the result contains and not by what it cites, a contributor’s result as well as the lead’s. A result going back to its department is sent to Security when it returns, so Security reviews what will be used. The Chief of Staff writes the decision memo without waiting, once the results have been through the return contract. The memo names each review still out and what it covers. The sensitive action stays blocked until that review is back. Any other disagreement still waits.
   - A disagreement that involves finance is run by MoxyWolf’s outside bookkeeper before it is settled. The context card names him and is the only home for his name, so no file in this repository carries it. The memo says so and carries the question for him, written so Dorian can forward it. The question leaves MoxyWolf when he does, so it carries the numbers and nothing that identifies the customer. No agent contacts him (the context card’s standing rule). The decision is logged as pending until Dorian reports it.
   - In a decision memo, a department’s open question stays a question. A statement of fact has a source: a result, the context card, Dorian’s ask or the run’s own record. One with none is marked as the Chief of Staff’s inference or left out, and so is one department’s risk applied to an option that department didn’t discuss (review `20261007-155817-a6a9289-sqfgr1r2`, F7). A result keeps its scope: what one department didn’t do isn’t reported as what nobody did (scenario runs 8 and 9). From CS-006 on, a script checks this bullet’s form: what the Chief of Staff gives Dorian is a quotation found in its source or a labeled line.

Before implementation, add tests in `plugins/chief-of-staff/tests/test_sales_marketing.py` for package discovery, manifest/marketplace consistency, no namespace shadowing, routing completeness and resolvable local head/source references. Extend `plugins/chief-of-staff/tests/test_roster.py` without relaxing its nonempty and drift checks. Tests must fail on the baseline for the missing departments and pass afterward. Use scenario-based independent evaluation for outreach sending, paid campaign launch, CRM mutation, sensitive customer segmentation, missing specialists, unsupported claims, conflicting departments and changed-artifact approval. Record each scenario’s input, expected decision, observed response, evaluator and artifact revision. Static text checks alone do not prove behavior or enforcement.
10. Validate with `plugins/gstack-execution/scripts/skill_packaging.py --json`, the Chief of Staff tests and `plugins/gstack-execution/scripts/run_all_tests.py`, using the check ledger. Obtain independent review of the entire diff and the behavioral scenarios. Report examined counts and any skipped or blocked checks. No edit to existing approved `goals/` or `goal-runs/`. The calendar stays a sensor: it tags an owner and dispatches nobody. `docs/evidence/CS-004-ten-departments-2026-10-07.md` records what changed in each, before and after. ### CS-006 — What reaches Dorian is a quotation or a labeled line, and a script checks it

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` in `6d52b3f` (PR #192, head `bbbf978`) on 8 October 2026 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]`, with CS-004 and on the merge instruction quoted in CS-004’s status. Review `20261007-203859-bf84b40-q531yemm` (codex/gpt-6-astra, `fixes_verified`, 3 rounds, 9/9, coverage `covered`). CI `tests` green at the merged head (run 37729084567). Evidence: `docs/evidence/CS-004-CS-006-release-2026-10-08.md`. Built in `67a6134` to `da1c796` on CS-004’s branch (PR #192), because CS-004’s review couldn’t close without it. Scenario runs 16 to 19 are in `docs/evidence/`; run 19 is the run whose every response passes the script as it stands. Evaluator gradings changed on identical text, so read them as a model’s readings and the script’s output as the fact.

Declared 2026-10-07 on Dorian’s instruction, in his words: “F7 - do the real fix.” He was offered two ways to close CS-004’s last blocking finding and chose this one. The criteria below are the builder’s reading of that instruction, and they’re his to edit.

Premise: fifteen scenario runs and two reviews showed that an agent told in prose to keep a statement inside its source does so nearly every time and slips about once in a long answer (“nobody read the CRM” for “I didn’t read it”). Rewording only moved the slip. So the property moves out of prose and into form: a line is a quotation a script can find in its source, or it carries a label saying whose claim it is. A line that is neither doesn’t pass.

**Links introduced:** two. The script’s receipt names the checked document by its sha256; the receipt describes the text in front of the reader only while the text is unchanged, and running the script again re-resolves it. Each `--source Name=file` binds a label to a file; a line labeled `Sales:` quotes the sales-department’s result only as far as the Chief of Staff passed that result’s file under that name. The script can’t see who wrote a file.

1. `plugins/chief-of-staff/scripts/claim_check.py --doc <file> --source <Name>=<file>` (the source option repeats) reads the document and puts every line in one of three classes: structural, labeled, or failed. Structural is a blank line, a heading, a rule, a line that is only a bold title of 80 characters or fewer, or anything inside a fenced block. A heading or bold title that begins with a label is a labeled line and is judged as one, so a quotation or claim can’t go unread by being set as a title. It exits 0 only when no line failed and at least one labeled line was examined, so a document it examined nothing in doesn’t pass (EV-001).
2. A labeled line takes one of these forms, and no other passes. `<Name>: "<quotation>"`, where `<Name>` is a source given on the command line, every quotation on the line is at least three words and appears word for word in that source’s file once quote marks, whitespace and emphasis marks are normalized (an asterisk, underscore or backtick is an emphasis mark at the edge of a word and part of the word inside one), and nothing follows the label but quotations joined by `and` or `then`. `Me: I …`, one sentence in the first person, for what the writer did or didn’t do. `Open: …?`, one question and nothing else: one sentence that starts with a question word and ends with the question mark, so a statement can’t ride in front of it. `My inference: …` or `Inference: …`. `Proposal: …`. `Skill: …`. `Source: …`.
3. These fail: a quotation that is in another source’s file and not in the named one; a quotation with an ellipsis or a changed word; a line with words outside its quotations; any unlabeled sentence. A labeled line that isn’t a quotation also fails when it retells what a named source said: the source’s name followed by a verb of saying, or “according to” the source. A source’s words are quoted under its label and never retold (added after scenario run 16). Any line that isn’t a quotation also fails when it says nobody did something or nothing was done (“nobody has reviewed”, “no one checked”, “nothing’s been verified”). The rule is that shape, a subject of nobody, no one or nothing with a verb of doing in the past, not the bare words, because a replay over run 17 showed the bare words fail honest lines like “doing nothing is option B”. One run can’t know what nobody did, and the writer can say what it did itself in a `Me:` line (added after run 17). Each failure prints its line number, the reason and the text.
4. The last line the script prints is a receipt: lines examined, lines labeled, quotations verified, failures, and the document’s sha256. `--json` gives the same as JSON.
5. `plugins/chief-of-staff/skills/chief-of-staff/SKILL.md` and `plugins/chief-of-staff/skills/cos/SKILL.md`: everything the Chief of Staff gives Dorian from a `/cos` run, the report and any decision memo, is written in those forms. Before it goes, the Chief of Staff runs the script over it with the ask, the context card and each department’s result as sources, fixes what fails, and ends with the receipt. A result from sales-department or marketing-department goes through the script when it comes back, with the dispatch and the context card as sources. A failure makes the result incomplete, and it goes back once with the failing lines.
6. `plugins/sales-department/skills/head-of-sales/SKILL.md` and `plugins/marketing-department/skills/head-of-marketing/SKILL.md`: a result’s lines are written in those forms, with `Dispatch:` and `Card:` as its quotation sources, and the shared text stays the same in both.
7. `plugins/chief-of-staff/tests/test_claim_check.py` covers each form passing and failing, the wrong-source case, normalization, the fenced block, a document with nothing labeled, and sentences the evaluator failed in scenario runs 12 to 17: each fails as written, and labeled as an inference it passes unless it says what nobody did, which fails under every label. The one line that may carry it is an `Open:` line that asks it outright (“Has nobody checked the address?”). `plugins/gstack-execution/scripts/run_all_tests.py` discovers it.
8. Every scenario is rerun at the revision that carries this. The harness runs the script on each response and sends a failure back once, as the Chief of Staff does with a department’s result and with its own. Each run records the script’s output beside the response, and codex grades the result.
9. The manual and `plugins/chief-of-staff/README.md` say what the check is and isn’t. It checks form, and that a quotation exists in its source. It doesn’t check that an inference is sound, that a `Me:` line is true, or that a quotation is fair to its context. Running it is an instruction, and nothing stops an agent that skips it. The receipt makes a skipped run visible, because anyone can run the script again.

### CS-007 — Every Chief of Staff dispatch carries its department, and the sweep opens with an after-action report

**Status:** done. Declared 2026-10-14 at Dorian's instruction beside MR-003, drafted on Sonnet at his request, approved via PR #217 ("approve #217"), amended on his words before any code (PR #219), built on `build/CS-007-department-aar` after MR-003 merged. Codex (gpt-6-astra) reviews: `20261014-082908-2e126c0-5h1aag0y` (`rounds_exhausted`, 11/11), `20261014-085739-6180a1b-b_ym5zob` (`rounds_exhausted`, 10/11), `20261014-091927-dcf7149-z4e2e7lw` (10/11, criterion 7 unmet only because `peer_review.py` was off the surface), `20261014-094221-0037765-216_g8hh` (`fixes_verified`, 11/11). Release handoff `awaiting_human_release`, lane `wide`, links verified; DR-113 autonomous merge of PR #221 pinned to `c81555c`, merged `ff160ec`; `record-release` recorded `agent_merge_autonomous`. The repository unknown (does the MR-001 writer persist `declared_tier`) closed on check `7eaa04a522c4` (exit 0, 4 of 4 real ledger lines carry it). The runtime unknowns (does a live `/cos` run tag its dispatches) stay open until model-router is installed in a session and `/cos` runs. Kept in the code from the reviews: the run id travels as the whole Bash command `route.py cos-run start <id>` seen by the hook and recorded per session, never as an environment variable (a shell export never reaches a later hook) and never as a project file (two sessions would share it); `applied_model` and `floor_rule` are written at launch so no reader infers them; every rate is over its own usage count. chief-of-staff 0.3.0, model-router 0.2.0, marketplace 1.136.0. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** six things get read by something else.

- A ledger class `department-subagent` in `plugins/model-router/references/call-ontology.json`, read by the router hook. It matches Agent calls whose `subagent_type` is one of the ten department agents (finance, people, it-operations, security, pmo, operations, corporate-strategy, customer-experience, sales-department, marketing-department). Default tier is `standard`, `ask` is true so Jev sees the dispatch, and security never drops below its floor (the same rule shape as review-subagent in MR-003).
- Two new fields on each ledger line in `$GSTACK_PEER_REVIEW_DIR/model-routing/<YYYYMMDD>-<session_id>.jsonl`: `department` (the agent name) and `cos_run`. The hook reads `cos_run` from the env var `COS_RUN_ID`, which `/cos` step 1 sets to the date plus a short slug of the ask. A line without `cos_run` came from outside a COS run, because only `/cos` sets the variable and the hook never invents one.
- A new file, `$GSTACK_PEER_REVIEW_DIR/model-routing/claim-checks.jsonl`. `plugins/chief-of-staff/scripts/claim_check.py` appends `{ts, cos_run, department, model, doc, passed, failing_lines}` to it and `route.py aar` reads it. One line means one check, because the script writes exactly one line per run, and only when both `--department` and `--cos-run` are passed.
- Two new flags, `--department` and `--cos-run`, passed by step 5 of `plugins/chief-of-staff/skills/cos/SKILL.md`.
- A new line in each `chief-of-staff-log.md` entry: `- Run: <cos_run>`, joining the prose to the code-captured lines on the same id. The id is the same in both places because step 1 mints it once and every later step reuses it.
- A new subcommand, `route.py aar --dir <ledger dir> --date <YYYY-MM-DD>`, and a new folder, `Taskade/<project>/00 – Project Hub/after-action/`, holding one `<YYYY-MM-DD>.md` per day. `/cos` step 1 reads and writes it.

**Rationale.** MR-003 logs the model and class of every call, but nothing captured by code says which department ran, on which model, and what happened to its result. The decision log is written from memory and can’t answer "do our send-backs cluster on one model?" or "does one department escalate more?" CS-007 adds the join: every dispatch carries its department and run id, every claim check leaves a line, and one report each morning counts the day before, side by side (model against failures, department against escalations). It names correlations, never causes.

**What's not taken.** We won't parse the prose log for correlations, because it's written from memory. The AAR won't change routing on its own: Dorian reads it, and MR-001.6's rule that routing earns its history stands. We won't score departments on judgement. The AAR counts check outcomes and escalations, which are facts, not quality.

1. `plugins/model-router/references/call-ontology.json` has a class `department-subagent` that matches Agent calls whose `subagent_type` is one of the ten department agents. Default tier is `standard` and `ask` is true.
2. In `plugins/model-router/scripts/test_route.py`, a `finance:finance` launch classifies as `department-subagent`. The ledger line has `department` set to `finance` and `cos_run` taken from `COS_RUN_ID`.
3. In `test_route.py`, a `security:security` launch stays on opus when the stub Jev says light.
4. `plugins/chief-of-staff/scripts/claim_check.py` run with `--department` and `--cos-run` appends one line with `ts`, `cos_run`, `department`, `model` (the model the dispatch ran on, passed as `--model`; absent is recorded as absent, never guessed), `doc`, `passed` and `failing_lines` to `claim-checks.jsonl`. A run without them appends nothing and still prints the receipt. Both cases are tested in `plugins/chief-of-staff/tests/test_claim_check.py`.
5. `route.py aar` on an empty directory prints zeros and reports zero lines read. It never reports a clean day. Tested in `test_route.py`.
6. `route.py aar` on a fixture day (two cos runs, three departments, one claim-check failure, one escalation) prints the right counts per class, per model and per department, plus the run list. Every mistake count is printed beside its usage count and as a rate over calls (failures per dispatch, escalations per run, blocking findings per review), never as a bare count. A rate under 10 observations prints with its count and is marked too few to read, and any pattern the report names under that bar is labeled a lead, not a finding. Tested in `test_route.py`.
7. The AAR output states the file count and line count it read. It reports correlations as counts side by side with no causal wording, and carries a fixed note that harder tasks go to stronger models by design (MR-001 and MR-002 send trust-boundary, cross-file and risky work to the floor), so a stronger model's higher failure rate is expected before it is evidence. Beside each model's rate it prints the share of that model's calls Jev or the table sent there by a floor rule. Every mistake line the AAR reads was tagged with its model and department when written: the claim-check line (criterion 4) carries both from the dispatch, and the MR-001 model-selection ledger line carries the declared tier. The AAR never infers a model or department for a mistake after the fact; a line without them is counted as untagged and reported. The fixture test asserts the note, the floor-rule share, the untagged count and the rates.
8. `plugins/chief-of-staff/skills/cos/SKILL.md` step 1 sets `COS_RUN_ID`, runs the AAR for the previous calendar day, writes it to `00 – Project Hub/after-action/<YYYY-MM-DD>.md`, and leads the report with its headline counts. It skips the write when that day's file exists. If the ledger directory is unreachable it says so and goes on. Step 5 passes `--department`, `--cos-run` and `--model`. A docs test, or the existing roster test, asserts that step 1 names the AAR and the after-action folder.
9. The `chief-of-staff-log.md` entry format in `plugins/chief-of-staff/skills/chief-of-staff/SKILL.md` includes the `- Run: <cos_run>` line. The same docs test checks it.
10. Every AAR ends with exactly one decision, under a heading `Decision`: a named gate or spec to change (an ontology class, a bar, a department's return contract, a claim-check form, a routing default), with the counts that argue for it, or the line `Decision: none this day; the counts are below the bar` when every pattern is a lead. `/cos` carries the decision into its report as a proposal for Dorian. The AAR never applies it. The fixture test asserts the heading, exactly one decision, and the none form on an empty day.
11. `plugins/chief-of-staff/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` and `plugins/model-router/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` each move a minor version, and the top-level marketplace version moves (CI-002). In CI, `run_all_tests.py` reports a nonzero count of what it examined, names `test_route.py` and `test_claim_check.py` among the suites it ran, and reports no failures.

## Thirteenth objective: blog publishing

Opened 2026-10-16. The first OpenControls post ran the 4d-blog-engine pipeline end to end, and two steps came back to Dorian that shouldn't have: `/blog-publish` prepared a commit and told him to click Push origin, and the new pillar's hub term was registered only after the post was ready, as three changes in two repositories (hub-links `d9ad2d7`; oc-website PR #88 for the lockfile pin and the renderer's site slug). His words, in the after-action report for 2026-10-15: "Both of those are errors in the way we work and need to be automated."

**Settled decisions**

- **The Release Owner's signature is the approval to publish** (Dorian, 2026-10-16). Once `changelog.md` carries `Verified — <initials>, <date>`, shipping the signed post is the agent's job, not his. Nothing ships without that line.
- **Git work runs as the `moxywolf-agent` app** (GA-005), through `gstack-execution/scripts/agent_token.py exec`. A repository the app isn't installed on is reported as a gap. No person's token.
- **A protected default branch is met with a pull request, never a bypass.** The agent opens it, waits for the target's required checks, and merges pinned to the head it pushed.
- **The scripts decide; the skills call them.** Pushing, merging, pulling back, registering a hub term and verifying the live page are deterministic and tested. The skill prose names the script and its exit codes.

### BP-001 — `/blog-publish` ships the post itself, and the hub term links before the post is done

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` in `915b9f0` (PR #224, pinned to head `6486e94`) on 16 October 2026 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]`, on Dorian's merge instruction on the pull request: "Merge PR #224 (Recommended). You approve merging the reviewed head 6486e94 with those three gaps written down as evidence the review couldn't see." Review `20261016-102755-988525c-prf70253` (codex, 3 rounds, `rounds_exhausted`): round 3 raised no blocking finding and no regression, and left criteria 1, 2, 5 and 8 unmet only on evidence the surface didn't hold (`plugins/gstack-execution/scripts/agent_token.py` withheld by the dependency cap, 25 shown, 40 withheld; the hub-links, oc-website and FrontierFounder files live in other repositories). `record-release` refused (`review has not passed`); the merge instruction comment on PR #224 is the release record. Tests at the merged head: 4d-blog-engine 41 OK, including `test_docs.ShipBlockRuns` (runs the SKILL.md ship block with ship.py faked); `run_all_tests.py` examined 83 checks, 83 passed (CI run 38944650435). Not exercised: a live GitHub merge and a live `verify-live` through the scripts; the first OpenControls post shipped through them is that test. 4d-blog-engine 0.21.0, marketplace 1.138.0. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** five things get read by something else.

- Three new descriptor fields in `plugins/4d-blog-engine/targets/<target>.md` frontmatter: `default_branch_protected` (true or false), `required_checks` (the check names the default branch requires), and `auto_linker_file` (the repo path where the renderer passes its `site` to hub-links). `targets/README.md` documents them, and `ship.py` and `hub_term.py` read them. The descriptor is assumed current; that holds because `ship.py` refuses when a push to a branch the descriptor calls unprotected is refused as protected, and says which field is wrong.
- `plugins/4d-blog-engine/scripts/ship.py` with subcommands `push`, `merge` and `verify-live`, and documented exit codes, read by `blog-publish/SKILL.md`.
- `plugins/4d-blog-engine/scripts/hub_term.py` with subcommands `register` (hub-links) and `pin` (a consuming target repo), called by the orchestrator's STEP 1.5 and `commands/blog-term.md`.
- Three new fields in a piece's `state.md` frontmatter: `published_pr`, `published_merge`, `live_verified_at`, read by `/blog-status`. `live_verified_at` means the live page showed the post and its hub link, because only `ship.py verify-live` writes it, and only on exit 0.
- A hub-links test case per registered term in `hub-links/test/hub-links.test.ts`, written by `hub_term.py register` and read by the hub-links suite.

1. `ship.py push --repo <path> --branch <name>` pushes through `agent_token.py exec`, then checks `git ls-remote` equals local `HEAD`. A mismatch exits non-zero and names both SHAs. Tested in `plugins/4d-blog-engine/tests/test_ship.py` against a local bare repository, with no network.
2. When the descriptor says `default_branch_protected: true`, `/blog-publish` commits on `publish/<slug>`, never on the default branch. `ship.py merge --repo <owner>/<name> --branch <name> --head <sha> --checks <names>` opens the pull request, polls the named checks until each is completed, merges pinned to `<head>` only when every named check concluded `success`, and exits non-zero naming the check otherwise. A check that never appears before the deadline is a failure, never a pass. Tested with an injected API client serving fixture responses; the test can't reach GitHub (XE-019).
3. After a merge, `ship.py` pulls the default branch back into the local clone with `--ff-only` and prints remote and local SHAs, which must match. Tested against the bare-repository fixture.
4. `ship.py verify-live --url <post url> --hub-path <path> --term <hub term> --deadline <seconds>` fetches the page until it returns 200 or the deadline passes, then requires an `<a>` whose `href` ends in `<hub-path>` and whose text matches `<term>` case-insensitively. A 200 without the link exits non-zero with `hub_link_missing`. Tested on fixture HTML: linked, unlinked, 404.
5. `hub_term.py register --hub-links <path> --term <phrase> --owner <site> --site-url <url> --path <hub path>` adds the site to `SITES` if missing, adds one `HUB_LINKS` entry before the `future pillar terms` marker, adds a test asserting the relative link on the owner and the absolute link elsewhere, runs the package's build and test, and commits, pushes and pulls back through `agent_token.py`. A term that already exists exits 0 without a change. A failing test exits non-zero and commits nothing. Tested in `tests/test_hub_term.py` on a copy of a fixture `map.ts`, with build, test and git stubbed.
6. `hub_term.py pin --repo <path> --site <slug> --file <auto_linker_file>` updates the hub-links pin with the repo's own package manager (`pnpm update @moxywolf/hub-links --lockfile-only` for a pnpm lockfile) and checks that `<file>` passes `site: '<slug>'`. A mismatch exits non-zero naming the file and both slugs. It never rewrites the renderer on its own. Tested on fixture files.
7. `skills/blog-publish/SKILL.md` no longer tells the writer to push or click Push origin. It runs `ship.py push`, then `merge` when the target is protected, then `verify-live`, and reports done only on `verify-live` exit 0, writing the three `state.md` fields. A refusal is reported with the script's output and the item stays unpublished. The orchestrator's STEP 1.5 and `commands/blog-term.md` call `hub_term.py register`, and `/blog-publish` calls `hub_term.py pin` on the target before its commit, so the term, the pin and the post land in one pull request. A docs test asserts that "Push origin" appears in none of the 4d-blog-engine skill or command files, and that each names its script.
8. `targets/opencontrols.md` and `targets/frontier-founder.md` carry the three new fields, filled from what each repository actually does. `4d-blog-engine` moves a minor version and the marketplace version moves (CI-002). In CI, `run_all_tests.py` names `test_ship.py` and `test_hub_term.py` among the suites it ran and reports no failures.

**What's not taken.** Social posting stays manual. The skill never posts to LinkedIn, X or Facebook. Waiting on Vercel's production deploy is `verify-live`'s deadline, not a separate integration.

### BP-002 — `prose_lint.py` counts the tells an outside detector flagged, and the ones Wikipedia names that the catalog missed

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` in `d608306` (PR #271, head `355001e`) on 31 October 2026 by Dorian (`dorianatmoxywolf`) on a passing review. Review `20261030-202414-3b4bc33-ccv31f9y` (codex, 2 rounds, `fixes_verified`) found curly apostrophes counted as two words, fixed in `word_count` for every check. Earlier reviews `20261030-194545-bd9d436-2dpvwtnt` (round 3 refused, ledger run one commit behind) and `20261030-201125-ddd80e5-jqcemspk`, their findings fixed. `peer_review.py release` refused the wide lane: the failure ledger held one macOS-only failure outside 4d-blog-engine (`test_goal_spend.py`, socket timeout), coverage was scored after the review opened (8 of 8 criteria verbatim) rather than attached at open, and the review's evidence links were unverifiable. Dorian merged it himself; no `release.json` or `record-release` exists. Tests at the merged head: `test_prose_lint` 21 OK, 4d-blog-engine 62 OK, CI `tests` run 41164392183 success. Criterion 8's receipt is `docs/evidence/BP-002.md` (base A with 0 findings, head B with one each of `parallel_run`, `closing_zinger`, `here_opener`). Not exercised: the long-form budget against a full published post. 4d-blog-engine 0.22.0, marketplace 1.156.0.

On 2026-10-30 an outside AI-text analyzer read one of Dorian's emails as AI-polished, while `prose_lint.py` graded it A with zero findings. The analyzer pointed at three short same-shape sentences in a row ("Models propose and vote. Versioned rule cards decide. Reviewers rule on what the cards can’t settle."), short lines closing a paragraph ("Most pipelines don’t."), and a "Here’s where" opener. Wikipedia's *Signs of AI writing* guide names three more the catalog lacked: inflated significance, trailing "-ing" clauses that claim importance, and ad-copy adjectives. Hidden zero-width characters pasted from other tools get the same treatment. The run and the closing line are also signature moves in Dorian's voice profile and his published posts use them, so his ruling is: score them in short-form (emails, social posts, abstracts), and in long-form let them join the existing rhetorical-density budget rather than scoring each one.

**Links introduced:** three things get read by something else.

- Five new rule ids in the findings JSON and markdown: `parallel_run`, `closing_zinger`, `here_opener`, `hidden_unicode`, and the new `banned_phrase:` and `banned_word:` entries, read by the release-owner gate and the blog skills by grade and rule id. The catalog and the script list the same rules because a test asserts each new rule id appears in `references/ai-anti-patterns.md`.
- Two new device names in the density hit list, `parallel-run` and `closing-zinger`, read by `density_violations` and the punchline count in long-form. A long-form density finding can come from any of the four devices; the catalog's long-form section says so.
- `auto_fix` now removes zero-width characters, and its returned counts gain a `hidden_unicode` key. `--fix` callers print the counts.

1. **Parallel run.** Three or more consecutive sentences in one body paragraph, each at most 10 words, that aren't already a multi-beat reveal, are one `parallel_run` hit. In short-form each hit is a Medium finding. Tested in `tests/test_prose_lint.py` on a synthetic paragraph shaped like the email's run, and on the same three sentences split across paragraphs, which isn't a hit.
2. **Closing zinger.** A sentence of at most 5 words that closes a body paragraph of three or more sentences, outside any run, is one `closing_zinger` hit. In short-form each is a Minor finding. Tested both ways: a paragraph ending in a 3-word sentence hits, one ending in a long sentence doesn't.
3. **Long-form budget.** Past the long-form threshold, `parallel-run` and `closing-zinger` hits are not findings on their own. They join the rhetorical hits that `density_violations` spaces against `density_gap` and `density_budget`. Tested with `longform=True`: the same paragraph produces no `parallel_run` finding and raises `density_violations`.
4. **"Here" opener.** A sentence that starts "Here’s / Here is / Here are" followed by where, what, how or why is a Minor `here_opener` finding, curly or straight apostrophe. Tested both ways.
5. **Wikipedia tells.** `BANNED_PHRASES` gains inflated significance ("a broader movement", "a defining moment", "marks a significant shift", "plays a key role", "serves as a reminder") and a trailing participle after a comma ("…, highlighting the…", underscoring, emphasizing, reflecting, showcasing, signaling, illustrating). `BANNED_WORDS` gains breathtaking, nestled, vibrant, stunning, bustling, renowned. Each is tested with one sentence that hits; "The results highlight the gap" (no comma, not a participle) doesn't.
6. **Hidden Unicode.** U+200B, U+200C, U+200D, U+2060, U+FEFF and U+00AD are a Medium `hidden_unicode` finding with their line numbers, and `--fix` removes them. A non-breaking space isn't flagged. Tested both ways.
7. `references/ai-anti-patterns.md` lists every new rule and word, and its long-form section names the two new devices. A test asserts each new rule id and word appears in it.
8. The email that prompted this, run locally and not committed, goes from zero findings to at least one `parallel_run`, one `closing_zinger` and one `here_opener`. The before and after are quoted in the review packet. `4d-blog-engine` moves a minor version and the marketplace version moves (CI-002). `run_all_tests.py` names `test_prose_lint.py` among the suites it ran and reports no failures.

**What's not taken.** Sharing this lint with the academic pipeline and `/blog-social` is a separate item. Contrast framing in short-form stays advisory. Calibrating the long-form budget against a full published post isn't possible here, because the published STIGViewer posts aren't in any local clone, so the existing calibration stands and the risk is named in the review packet.

## Fourteenth objective: the decider council

Opened 2026-10-18. Four scripts already ask a decision model typed questions and act on the answers: `packet_coverage.mjs` (is this criterion covered), `model_select.mjs` (which tier builds this item), `council/scripts/jev_route.py` (which deliberation shape) and `model-router/scripts/route.py` (which tier runs this subagent). Each has its own wire (`/v1/evaluate`, `/v4/ai`, the AI SDK), key loading, test stub and one judge, Jev. Dorian (2026-10-18): "We need to be able to ask the deciders more than just which model to use. We are going to be employing a decider call when building out many, many tasks." So the decider is a primitive every plugin calls, not a feature of one script. His name for it: "Think of it as a decider council." It lives in the council plugin as the sibling of the deliberation council: that one takes open questions, this one typed questions.

**Settled decisions**

- **One wire.** Every decider call is `POST /v1/decisions` on the AI Gateway (OpenAI-compatible; `input`, `questions` typed `predicate`, `choice`, `score`; probabilities back). It accepts any decision model slug, so a voice is a string, never a code path.
- **Three voices from three vendors** by default: `typesafe-ai/jev`, `perplexity/pplx-decider-v1.2`, `openai/gpt-6-luna-decisions`. The list is a constant with an environment override. No vendor checks itself, as in cross-vendor review.
- **Majority wins** (Dorian, 2026-10-18: "Majority wins."). For a choice, the option most voices picked. For a predicate or a score, the median of the voices' numbers. No majority (a three-way split on a choice, or two voices that disagree) is returned as a tie, never as a pick.
- **Code decides what an answer means.** The primitive returns probabilities and the majority. The caller's own rule table turns them into an action, as MR-001 and MR-002 do. The primitive never carries a bar, a tier or a policy.
- **Deciders answer typed questions only.** `/deliberate` stays the gate for open questions. Nothing here touches the council's deliberation pipeline.
- **A decider that hasn't been scored watches before it steers** (MR-001's rule). Every call writes a ledger line with the caller's name. The caller's own outcome signal is scored against each voice before the quorum may decide anything for that caller.

### DC-001 — The decider council: three voices on one wire, a majority, and a ledger every caller writes to

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` in `60a3e4c` (PR #228, head `77110c2`) on 18 October 2026 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]`, recorded by `record-release` as `agent_merge_autonomous` under DR-113, lane `wide`; declared and approved in PR #227 (`195f18f`); probe `6a0f97c` (`docs/evidence/decision-quorum-probe-2026-10-18.md`, all three voices answered); reviews `20261018-100213-7c23b25-xdpcz0y9` (codex gpt-6-astra; design findings closed by amending criteria 1, 2, 9 and 10 on Dorian's approval in `2e78652`) and `20261018-104726-2e78652-u2t4ltw8` (codex; round 1 F1, choice distributions must cover the offered values and rank the pick first, as `jev_route.well_formed` does, fixed in `415b166`; round 3 `fixes_verified`, 11/11 met, no unknowns; commit order in `docs/evidence/dc-001-build-history-2026-10-18.md`); CI `tests` green at every reviewed head (last run 39152996266); MR-001 ledger line and council outcome on council line `7bef4cddb3b2`; council 0.12.0, gstack-execution 0.89.0, marketplace 1.139.0. Not exercised live: `quorum` mode (the gate needs 20 scored lines; there is one) and `/decide` by hand. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

**Links introduced:** `plugins/council/scripts/decider_council.py`, called by path from any plugin (precedent: `ship.py` naming `gstack-execution/scripts/agent_token.py`), and `/decide` in the council plugin, which calls it for a person or a skill. Its output, per question: each voice's answer verbatim (or `unavailable` with the reason), `majority`, `agreed` (every available voice gave the same choice, or every number is within `AGREE_BAND` of the median), `tie` (no majority exists), and per-voice `cost` and latency. The shared ledger `$GSTACK_PEER_REVIEW_DIR/decisions/<YYYYMM>.jsonl` (fallback `~/.gstack/decisions/`), one line per call: `ts`, `caller`, `ref` (what was decided about: a review id, an item id, a session id), question names and types, the output object, and an empty `outcome` filled later by `decider_council.py outcome --line <id> --result <json>`. A reader assumes every voice answered the same questions about the same input; criterion 9 pins that byte for byte.

1. `decider_council.py ask --caller <name> --ref <id> --input <file or -> --questions <json file or inline>` serializes one `/v1/decisions` body once and posts it concurrently to each slug in `DECIDERS` (`GSTACK_DECIDERS` overrides, comma-separated), only the leading `model` member differing (the wire names the voice in the body; nothing else varies), each bounded by `DECIDER_TIMEOUT_MS` (8000). Stdlib Python only: `urllib`, `threading`, `json`. The key comes from `GSTACK_AIGATEWAY_ENV` or `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY`, as `plugins/council/scripts/jev_route.py` loads it today. `JEV_ENDPOINT` replaces the base URL for the test stub. A test stub delays each voice one second and asserts a three-voice call completes in under two. (amended 2026-10-18, `2e78652`)
2. A voice that times out, returns a non-200, returns a `refusal` for a question, or returns a malformed answer (a probability outside [0, 1], probabilities not summing to 1 within 0.01, a choice not offered, a missing question) is `unavailable` for that question with the reason and does not count toward the majority. The well-formedness checks are the ones `plugins/council/scripts/jev_route.py` already applies, moved here. (amended 2026-10-18, `2e78652`)
3. Majority: for a `choice`, the value picked by more than half the available voices; for a `predicate`, the median probability; for a `score`, the median score. `tie` is true when a choice has no such value, or fewer than two voices answered. With one voice the output carries that voice's answer and `quorum: degraded`; with none, `unavailable`. The primitive never maps an answer to an action.
4. Every `ask` appends one ledger line before returning and prints the line id with the output. `decider_council.py outcome --line <id> --result <json>` fills the line's `outcome`. Each caller owns its outcome vocabulary (model selection: the MR-001 outcome fields; coverage: whether the criterion was later found met at release; the smart-router: its existing value-added score). Writing an outcome never loses a line's answers.
5. `decider_council.py score [--caller <name>] [--since <YYYY-MM>]` prints, per caller and voice: calls, unavailable count, agreement rate, tie rate, mean cost, median latency, and, over lines with an outcome, how often each voice's answer and the majority answer matched the outcome by the caller's rule. Each caller registers that rule as a small Python function in a table in `decider_council.py`, one per caller; without one the row prints `no outcome rule`. Zeros print as zero; a caller with no lines prints a row of zeros.
6. `decider_council.py gate --caller <name>` exits 0 when that caller has at least 20 ledger lines with outcomes and at least two voices each, and the majority's match rate is at least Jev's alone; otherwise it exits nonzero with `insufficient_history` and prints both rates. A caller that wants the majority to decide checks this gate first and otherwise acts on Jev's answer alone, as today. The 20 and the comparison are named constants.
7. **First caller: `model_select.mjs`.** It calls `decider_council.py ask --caller model_select` by `subprocess` for its five MR-002 questions, applies its rule table to the majority probabilities when `gate` passes and to Jev's otherwise, and records both tiers and `rule: quorum_majority` or `quorum_tie`. A tie falls to the dearer tier, Dorian's one exception to majority: "a tie is not a decision". It writes the MR-001 outcome on `record-release` through `decider_council.py outcome`. The AI SDK `evaluationModel` path leaves `model_select.mjs`. `packet_coverage.mjs`, `jev_route.py` and `route.py` are unchanged here and move under DC-002.
8. **Probe first.** The build branch's first commit sends the five-question body to each of the three slugs on the real gateway and records each voice's HTTP status, answer shape, `cost` and latency verbatim in `docs/evidence/decision-quorum-probe-<date>.md`. A voice that doesn't answer `/v1/decisions` as documented is dropped from `DECIDERS` in that commit, with the evidence named. No later step assumes a voice the probe didn't see answer.
9. `plugins/council/scripts/test_decider_council.py` runs `decider_council.py` as a subprocess against a stub serving `/v1/decisions` per slug: three voices agree; a two-to-one choice split returns the majority in both directions; a three-way split returns `tie`; predicate and score return the median; one voice unavailable is excluded and named; a refusal is unavailable; all unavailable is `unavailable`; the raw request bytes each stub slug received are byte-identical after the leading `model` member; the ledger line is written before the output prints; `outcome` fills a line and keeps its answers; `score` prints zeros on an empty ledger and the right counts on a fixture; `gate` refuses at 19 lines and at 20 with the majority below Jev, and passes at 20 with the majority at or above. `test_model_select.py` gains: the script calls `decider_council.py`; with `gate` failing it acts on Jev's tier; with `gate` passing, on the majority; a tie selects the dearer tier. (amended 2026-10-18, `2e78652`)
10. `vocabulary.json` gains `decider_council`, `quorum`, `tie`, `degraded`, `quorum_majority` and `quorum_tie`, and `plugins/gstack-execution/scripts/vocab_check.py` resolves them. The peer-review contract's Model selection section, the `gstack-build` command text, `council/README.md`, `council/commands/decide.md` and `gstack-execution/README.md` describe the decider council, the three voices, the majority and tie rules, the ledger, `outcome`, `score` and `gate`, with a worked example of calling it from a new plugin in under ten lines, and `/decide <question> --choices a,b,c` as the hand-run form, printing each voice, the majority and the ledger line id. (amended 2026-10-18, `2e78652`)
11. `plugins/council/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` and `plugins/gstack-execution/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` each move a minor version, and the top-level marketplace version moves (CI-002). In CI, `run_all_tests.py` reports a nonzero count of what it examined, names `test_decider_council.py` and `test_model_select.py` among the suites it ran, and reports no failures.

### DC-002 — The other three decider calls join the decider council

**Status:** planned, declared with DC-001; built only after DC-001's probe and first ledger lines exist.

Callers: `packet_coverage.mjs` (caller `coverage`, outcome: criterion met or unmet at release); `jev_route.py` (caller `council_router`, outcome: the smart-router's logged value-added score); `route.py` PreToolUse (caller `model_router_hook`, outcome: none yet; shadow only; it keeps its 8-second budget by calling the primitive with `DECIDER_TIMEOUT_MS` 6000 and acting on the table default when the call is late). Each keeps its own rule table and drops its private wire, key loading and stub. Criteria are written when DC-001's score has its first rows.

**Callers this objective expects next, not declared:** the Chief of Staff's routing of an ask to departments (CS-001 does it in skill prose today); GA-010's blast-radius lane; GO-005's "who makes this judgment call"; synergy-engine's fit-to-fingerprint score; `/session-review`'s proposal classification; BP-001's rubric checks before publish. Each becomes an item when its outcome signal is named, because a caller with no outcome can't be scored and a decider that can't be scored only watches.

## Fifteenth objective: the demand harness

Opened 2026-10-26. `plugins/mcp-demand-harness` turns what a product's customers will ask its MCP server and REST gateway into tests. The deterministic half is the probe: a direct `tools/call` or REST request with a check on the reply. The 0.1.0 probe graded any non-empty reply as a pass. On 2026-10-25 a 36-case baseline against STIGViewer production scored 36/36 under 0.1.0 grading; the same case IDs under 0.2.0 grading, against the same advertised server surface, scored 30/36 (the original responses were not regraded; 0.1.0 kept no bodies), and nine of the thirty-six (25%) never tested what their question text says: three never reached their endpoint, four hit a slug that does not exist, two used the wrong search mode, one graded a correct refusal as content, and empty role searches passed as answers. The premise: **a green probe must mean its declared checks passed, and a failure the server reports inside a 2xx reply is a failure.** A liveness or negative-only check cannot establish that the server answered the question. Structured and content checks establish only the properties they explicitly test, and every layer says which kind it carries.

**Settled decisions**

- **Deterministic probes are graded by code only.** No model reads a probe reply. The model-driven suites (MCPJam, mcp-eval) stay separate and are not covered by this item.
- **Structured contracts, not substrings, are the strong form.** A `json_any_of` contract is a list of alternative branches; a branch is a conjunction of JSON Pointer predicates (`path`, `type`, `nonempty`, `equals`). A substring proves an envelope arrived; a contract proves the shape of the answer.
- **Only one failure is excusable, and only explicitly.** A 2xx reply carrying `ok:false` passes only as a `not_found` denial with HTTP status 404 whose body matches a branch naming `/absence/reason` and a nonempty `/absence/message`. Authentication, entitlement, rate-limit and server-error envelopes never pass, whatever the contract says.
- **`error` and `isError` are reserved keys** at the top of any payload. A product whose success payload needs those names renames or nests the field; the grader does not relax for everyone.
- **Evidence is kept, bounded and redacted.** Each layer records a receipt: sha256 of the canonical original body, up to 16,384 characters of sanitized text, and explicit `truncated` and `redacted` flags. Redaction is best-effort and says so. A truncated or redacted receipt cannot support exact offline regrading, and the report does not pretend otherwise.
- **A run never overwrites a run.** Output paths are opened exclusively; an existing file refuses the run before any request is sent.
- **Staging by default; production needs a named approver** (unchanged from 0.1.0, `GOVERNANCE.md`).

### DH-001 — A green probe means its declared checks passed

**Status:** review. Built on `build/mcp-probe-evidence` (harness 0.2.0, frozen at `b55a9e0`, fixes through `85d5f4a`); cross-vendor reviews `20261026-080924-da4beb3-htsmx86c` (codex, 2 rounds, fixed at `c32cd54`) and `20261026-082123-182cc9e-sas80u0x` (codex, 3 rounds, fixed at `613f858` and `85d5f4a`, final round zero findings, `rounds_exhausted` on execution evidence at the reviewed head); not released; coverage against this item not yet scored (the item postdates the reviews); 82 tests at `85d5f4a`, failure-ledger run `331c2d9bb407`, exit 0.

**Links introduced:** a probe result layer links to its response receipt (sha256 of the original body plus bounded sanitized text), to the plan item that produced it (plan sha256 and the plan's declared bank hash), and to the grading harness (source hashes of `probe.py`, `probe_checks.py`, `demandlib.py`), with the server's self-reported name and version beside them. These are hashes over content in the run record; nothing links to a deployed commit, and the record says so.

1. A `tools/call` reply fails the `mcp_payload` layer when it carries a JSON-RPC `error`, an `id` not matching the request, `isError: true`, or empty data. Empty is decided by the selected payload: `structuredContent.data` when `structuredContent` is present, otherwise the first JSON text block's `data`. Prose never makes an explicitly empty payload non-empty, and prose alone counts only when no block is JSON. Tests: `test_tool_and_http_errors_fail_before_content`, `test_rpc_errors_malformed_and_wrong_id_cannot_be_results`, `test_empty_text_and_text_failure_envelopes_fail`, `test_f1_text_cannot_make_empty_structured_data_nonempty`, `test_r2_f1_prose_cannot_make_empty_json_text_data_nonempty`, and `test_error_results_cannot_pass_a_nonempty_check` through a real subprocess run against a local HTTP fixture.
2. Every JSON envelope in a reply (`structuredContent` and each parseable text block) is inspected for an application failure. An envelope with `ok:false` fails unless it is a `not_found`/404 denial matched by an explicit typed-absence branch of the case's `json_any_of`. `rate_limited`, `unauthorized`, `forbidden`, `error` and any non-404 status cannot borrow a success branch or an absence branch. A reserved `error` or `isError` key in any envelope's payload fails. Tests: `test_error_denial_cannot_use_a_success_branch`, `test_application_exceptions_in_text_are_not_success`, `test_f2_failure_envelope_in_any_block_is_seen`, `test_r2_f3_reserved_error_keys_apply_to_every_envelope`.
3. A REST layer passes its transport check in exactly two cases, consistent with criterion 2: a 2xx response whose body is a non-empty JSON object or array without an application failure; or a 2xx body with `ok:false` whose denial is `not_found` with status 404 and which matches an explicit typed-absence branch evaluated against the whole body (REST contracts are body-relative; nothing is unwrapped). Every other `ok:false` body, and any reserved `error` or `isError` key, fails. Tests: `test_rest_json_error_is_not_success`, `test_r2_f2_rest_body_accepts_only_an_explicit_typed_absence`, `test_r2_f2b_rest_absence_is_body_relative_even_with_a_data_field`.
4. A `json_any_of` contract is validated before any request: a non-empty list of non-empty branches, each predicate a JSON Pointer `path` with at least one of `type` (`object`, `array`, `string`, `boolean`, `number`, `null`), `nonempty: true`, or `equals`. An invalid contract in the bank or the plan refuses the run with zero requests. Grading evaluates every predicate of a branch and passes when any branch holds. A missing path fails. `equals` keeps JSON types apart at every depth, so `true` is not `1` inside objects or arrays. A substring elsewhere in the reply never excuses a structural check. Tests: `test_bad_contract_is_refused`, `test_structured_success_or_explicit_absence`, `test_nested_payload_and_pointer_escaping`, `test_f3_equals_keeps_json_booleans_and_numbers_apart_recursively`, `test_invalid_contract_is_refused_before_traffic`, `test_payload_json_contract_is_validated_and_exportable`.
5. Each layer records `assertion_strength`: `structured` (a `json_any_of` contract), `content` (`must_contain`), `negative_only` (`must_not_match` only) or `liveness` (nothing but non-emptiness). `results.py ingest` renders a coverage table by strength and marks reports written before the label existed as `unknown_legacy`, never inferring strength from a green verdict. Tests: `test_probe_retains_assertion_strength_and_flags_legacy`, `test_receipts_and_structured_contract_survive_real_http_run`.
6. Each layer keeps a response receipt: sha256 of the canonical original, up to 16,384 characters of sanitized text, `truncated` and `redacted` flags, and the original byte count. Redaction covers the run's own credential, keys named like secrets, bearer tokens, GitHub tokens and JWT-shaped strings, inside JSON text blocks and SSE `data:` lines as well as top-level fields. Safe JSON reformatting is not reported as redaction. Tests: `test_receipt_redacts_then_bounds_and_records_original_hash`, `test_json_text_secrets_are_redacted`, `test_sse_secrets_are_redacted_without_flagging_safe_json`, and the subprocess run asserting the fixture token never appears in the report.
7. A run record carries the plan file's sha256, the profile file's sha256, the bank hash the plan declares (labelled as the plan's declaration, not an independent bank check), sha256 of the three harness source files, the local git revisions of the harness and the working repository, and the `serverInfo` name and version observed at `initialize`. It states that a version or surface digest is not proof of a deployed commit. Test: `test_receipts_and_structured_contract_survive_real_http_run`.
8. Before any request: an output path that already exists, an `--only` selection that matches nothing or names only items with no executable call, or an invalid contract refuses the run with exit 3, leaving existing output byte-identical. Production still requires `--approver "First Last"` and refuses draft questions. Tests: `test_existing_output_and_empty_selection_refuse_before_traffic`, `test_invalid_contract_is_refused_before_traffic`, `test_production_needs_named_person`.
9. `demandlib.validate_bank` validates `json_any_of` in both `payload` and `rest` sections through `probe_checks.validate_contract`, importing it at the point of validation, not at module scope: `demandlib` depends on `probe_checks` whenever a bank carries a contract, but loading `demandlib` for export, seeding or reporting does not import the probe module. Test: `test_payload_json_contract_is_validated_and_exportable`.
10. The whole suite runs against local HTTP fixtures with no external network and no production credentials. A packet claiming this item includes a passing suite ledger receipt bound to the reviewed head.

## Sixteenth objective: specification consistency

Opened 2026-11-02. A plan can pass every critic round and still hold two requirements that can't both be met, because each reads fine alone and the conflict only shows in a scenario neither names. The premise: **before anything is built from an item, its requirements are checked against each other and against the constraints, and the check is evidence a script reads, not a verdict a session remembers.**

### SR-001 — An item's requirements are checked against each other before it's built

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` in `5d855a9` (PR #280, head `837fa1a`) on 2 November 2026 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]` on Dorian's posted instruction ("merge #280"), recorded as `agent_merge_on_instruction`; review `20261102-133808-41378e8-qmgtczf8` (codex, 2 rounds, `fixes_verified`); own live spec review 0 contradictions, 6 ambiguities (`reviews/spec-review/SR-001.json`); criterion 8's run `docs/evidence/SR-001.md`; `run_all_tests.py` 104 of 104 through ledger run `4611b01025e5` on Dorian's Mac; CI `tests` run 41531439004 success; wide lane, gates passed; branch protection unreadable to the agent token (403); not exercised from a cloud or phone session; gstack-execution 0.99.0, marketplace 1.160.0. Removed history (review narrative and evidence) is kept in [`DESIGN.md` at `ef74ce8`](https://github.com/MoxyWolfLLC/moxywolf-plugins/blob/ef74ce8/DESIGN.md).

`spec_review.py` makes one Claude API call to cross-check the requirements in scope pairwise: a Sonnet 5.5 executor with adaptive thinking that consults a Fable 5.1 advisor before committing to a reading. The advisor tool exists only on the Claude API, not on OpenRouter, so this is the plugin's one script that calls Anthropic directly. SR-001 is exempt from *Test the boundaries before adding item IDs*, by Dorian's ruling on 2026-11-02. Dorian approved the scope the same day: the items under build plus Goal and Constraints, not the whole `DESIGN.md`, so a status line doesn't invalidate the record and a run stays affordable on a 570 KB doc (the same scoping XE-031 gives `target=design`).

**Links introduced:**

- The record `reviews/spec-review/<id>.json` beside the doc, one per item, or `all.json` for a whole file, read by `check`, `/gstack-build` Step 2 and `peer_review.py open`. A reader assumes it describes the doc as it is now; that holds because `check` recomputes `scope_sha256` from the doc every time and never trusts a stored verdict.
- `GSTACK_ANTHROPIC_ENV`, naming the vault's `anthropic.env` (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=...`), the same convention as `GSTACK_OPENROUTER_ENV`.
- The reason codes `spec_review_missing`, `spec_review_stale` and `spec_review_open` become `peer_review.py open` error codes.

1. **Scope.** `spec_review.py run <doc> --items <id>[,<id>]` reviews each named item on its own: the doc's `## Goal` and `## Constraints and settled decisions` sections plus that item. An item is a `### <id>` section (heading to the next `###` or `##`) or, in the design-doc template's format, a table row whose first cell is the id, sent with the table's header row. With no `--items` it sends the whole file, which is how a goal's `PLAN.md` is reviewed. In every mode every `**Status:**` line is removed and every table's Status and Review ID cells are blanked, so moving an item's status never changes what was reviewed. An item id the doc doesn't contain refuses (exit 1) before any request.
2. **The call.** One Messages API request to `claude-sonnet-5-5` with `thinking: adaptive` and the `advisor_20260301` tool backed by `claude-fable-5-1` (`max_uses` 3). A `pause_turn` reply is resumed by resending the assistant turn unchanged, at most six times; still paused after that refuses, exit 1. Sonnet 5.5 rejects a forced `tool_choice`, so a turn ending without an advisor call gets one reminder in the same conversation; still none refuses, exit 1, and writes no record. At least one advisor call is required, up to `max_uses`. `GSTACK_SPEC_EXECUTOR` and `GSTACK_SPEC_ADVISOR` override the pair.
3. **The record.** `run` writes `reviews/spec-review/<id>.json` and `<id>.md` per item beside the doc (`all` for a whole file): schema, doc name, items, `scope_sha256` of exactly the text sent, both models, advisor call count, usage, `contradictions` (requirements, triggering scenario, smallest change) and `ambiguities` (requirements, question). Exit 0 with no contradiction, 2 with any, 1 refused or failed.
4. **The gate.** `check <doc> [--items ...]` re-extracts each item's scope and passes only when every item's record exists, records at least one advisor call, matches `scope_sha256` and lists no contradiction, so a batch passes when each item does, in any order. With no `--items` it checks `all.json`. A record with no advisor call is refused as `spec_review_missing`. Ambiguities are reported and don't block. The three refusals are distinct: `spec_review_missing`, `spec_review_stale`, `spec_review_open`. A contradiction clears only by revising the doc and re-running: the record has no `resolved` flag and `check` reads none. Editing a status line alone leaves the record current; editing a criterion or a constraint makes it stale.
5. **The credential.** Read only from the file `GSTACK_ANTHROPIC_ENV` names, with an optional `ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE_ID` from the same file sent as `anthropic-workspace-id` for a key not scoped to a workspace. Unset, a missing file, or no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` value refuses with that reason; no other credential is tried. An API error is reported as `refused:` with the API's own message, exit 1.
6. **Wiring.** `/gstack-plan-review` runs it as Round 0 on the plan, and its contradictions go into the Round 1 critic prompt as settled findings; a refusal is reported as `review_unavailable` and the plan isn't presented as consistency-checked. `/gstack-build` Step 2 runs `check` for the item before Step 3, and `spec_review_open` takes the amendment path with each smallest change as the proposed diff and the ambiguities as questions. `peer_review.py open` refuses an item packet unless `check` passes for its items against that repository's `DESIGN.md`. A goal item `<id>/<n>` is checked against `goals/<id>/PLAN.md`'s single whole-file record and needs no section of its own.
7. **Tests.** `spec_review.py --selftest`, stubbed transport and no network, covers scope extraction from a heading and from a table row, status-line and status-cell insensitivity, a record with no advisor call refused, a paused turn resumed, each of the three refusals, an unknown item id and a missing credential. A `peer_review.py` test shows `open` refused with `spec_review_missing` and accepted once the record matches. `run_all_tests.py` names both among what it ran and reports no failures.
8. **One live run.** A plan with one deliberately planted conflict, run through `run`, lists that conflict in `contradictions`; the record is quoted in the review packet. It needs `anthropic.env` in the vault.
9. gstack-execution moves a minor version, and its marketplace entry, the top-level marketplace version and the README Version History move with it (CI-002).

**What's not taken.** Every item review in a repo with a `DESIGN.md` needs an Anthropic credential and one API call per item before `open` accepts the packet; that is the point of the gate, and why the scope is the item. Running it from the Cowork cloud proxy isn't covered: it runs where `model_select.mjs recommend` runs.

### SR-002 – The constraints are reviewed on their own, and an item's review is about the item

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` in `e9c166c` (PR #297, head `1954f7b`) on 12 November 2026 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]`, recorded by `record-release` as `agent_merge_autonomous` under DR-113 on the wide lane; review `20261111-195124-292079b-gzo_cfs5` (codex, 3 rounds, `fixes_verified`, coverage covered); `run_all_tests.py` 111 of 111 through ledger run `7223ce03ed47` on Dorian's Mac; SR-002.3 met on Dorian's recorded acceptance (Amendments log, 2026-11-12).

On 2026-11-11, five spec-review runs over ME-002 to ME-004 found five clashes, each between two existing constraints and none in the item under review; each constraint edit made every item's record stale, and the constraints had never been reviewed against each other as a set. SR-002 is exempt from *Test the boundaries before adding item IDs*, by Dorian's ruling on 2026-11-11 ("Approve, new item + waiver"), covering SR-002 only.

**Links introduced:** the record `reviews/spec-review/constraints.json`, read by `check`; a reader assumes it describes the constraints as they are now, which holds because `check` recomputes its `scope_sha256` from the doc every time. Each contradiction gains a `scope` field (`item` or `constraints`), read by `check`; a reader assumes `constraints` means no requirement of the item is involved, which holds only as far as criterion 4's code test goes (limit under *What's not taken*).

1. **Constraints mode.** `spec_review.py run <doc> --constraints` sends `## Goal` and `## Constraints and settled decisions` alone and writes `reviews/spec-review/constraints.json` and `constraints.md`, with an item record's schema and SR-001.2's advisor rule (at least one advisor call, or no record). `check <doc> --constraints` reads `constraints.json`, which each run overwrites, and passes when it's current and lists no contradiction; in a constraints record every contradiction blocks, whatever its `scope`. `--constraints` with `--items` refuses, exit 1.
2. **The sweep, bounded.** Constraints mode is run on this `DESIGN.md`. Each contradiction goes to Dorian as its smallest change, and each, except any still open at the six-run cap, ends in an edit to the doc: the change as proposed, his own edit, or, when he declines, a sentence in the constraint stating his ruling on why both hold. Nothing is carried between runs but the doc, so a later run listing a clash again opens the record again. The mode is re-run after the edits.
