The sweep stops at two consecutive runs with no contradiction, or after six runs, whichever comes first. An edit to the constraints after the stop, such as a ruling on a seed, makes the record stale and restarts the count within the same six runs. At six, what's left is listed for Dorian and the sweep ends. A copy of every run's record is kept as `docs/evidence/SR-002-sweep-<n>.json`; `check` never reads the copies.
3. **Seeded with what's already known.** The first pass takes these to Dorian as a list, whether or not a run samples them: the human-exception route to `released` against *One release path*; which paths "a change touching any of those" covers in the pre-merge gate; the status of PR #24, #71 and #73; whether `released` and `merged_unreviewed` are barred vocabulary growth; and *Test the boundaries before adding item IDs*, waived for every item since September. The list is input to him, not to the script: a seed a run lists is a contradiction like any other, and one no run lists changes neither `check` nor the stop rule until Dorian rules on it; a ruling that edits the constraints then makes the record stale and restarts the count under criterion 2.
4. **An item's review blocks on the item.** The findings block carries `scope` on each contradiction. `constraints` is accepted only when none of the entry's `requirements` names the reviewed item's id, matched whole as SR-001 matches ids (`ME-00` is not `ME-004`); otherwise the entry is read as `item`. Each item is reviewed on its own (SR-001.1), so the id is that record's item. An id a constraint's own text cites, such as GA-011, doesn't count. An item's `check` fails on `item` contradictions; its `constraints` contradictions are listed in its output and don't fail it.
5. **Only under a clean constraints record.** Criterion 4's leniency applies only while `check <doc> --constraints` passes. With no constraints record, a stale one or an open one, an item's `constraints` contradictions block. In whole-file mode (no `--items`) every contradiction is read as `item`.
6. **Tests.** `--selftest`, stubbed transport: the constraints scope holds nothing from `## Items and acceptance criteria` onward; a `constraints` contradiction passes an item's `check` under a clean constraints record and fails it under a missing, a stale and an open one; a `constraints` label on an entry naming the item's id is read as `item` and blocks; a record without `scope` reads every contradiction as `item`; `--constraints` with `--items` refuses.
7. **ME-004 resumes.** After the sweep, ME-004's spec review is re-run under whichever rule criterion 5 then gives, and its result is reported either way. SR-002 doesn't promise it passes.
8. gstack-execution moves a minor version, and its marketplace entry, the top-level marketplace version and the README Version History move with it (CI-002).

**What's not taken.** The reviewing model labels the scope, and criterion 4's test only checks that the item's id isn't named. A model could describe an item's conflict in constraint words alone and have it pass as `constraints`; criterion 5 limits that to conflicts a clean constraints review also missed. A script that proves which requirement a scenario depends on isn't taken. Item records still go stale when a constraint changes, because an item is reviewed against the constraints as they are.

## Seventeenth objective: Council replay cases

Opened 2026-11-17 by Dorian C, under DR-140 (accepted 2026-11-17, `MoxyWolf Vault/Projects/Moxywolf Plugins/06-Engineering/DR-140-council-replay-cases-live-in-the-vault-by-project-2026-11-17.md`). `/council-optimize` has never run: no workspace holds a rated deliberation, and `pattern-memory` keeps only an 80-character summary, so nothing can be replayed. DR-140 puts replayable cases in the vault, by project, behind per-case opt-in, redaction and the transcript-derived publish gate. This objective builds that store. A SkillOpt-style optimizer is a later, separate item that reads only cases this store marks eligible. No deliberation content is published until CC-002 is built and Dorian approves the store's audience (CC-002.6).

### CC-001 — The legacy `/council-optimize` refuses every run

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` in `7f4e10f` (PR #350, head `dd02731`) on 17 November 2026 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]`, recorded by `record-release` as `agent_merge_autonomous` (DR-113, wide lane); review `20261117-211435-dd02731-1rxto5nl` (codex/gpt-6-astra, `no_blocking_findings`, 1 round); `tests` workflow green at the head (run 44238971654).

**Links introduced:** the refusal names DR-140 by its vault path. Nothing is stored.

`/council-optimize` could start on rating fields alone, and its prerequisite counted a `user_rating` field even when null. DR-140 disables that path until the case store and the new optimizer exist.

1. `plugins/council/commands/council-optimize.md` stops before any other step, with every flag including `--dry-run`, and prints one message: the optimizer is disabled by DR-140 until the case store (CC-002) and a new optimizer item exist, naming DR-140's vault path. It makes no API call and reads or writes no file.
2. The command no longer describes reading `council-memory.json`, counting ratings, benchmark selection or experiments; those steps are removed, not commented out. `autoresearch-loop.md` and `eval-criteria.md` gain one line at the top saying the loop is disabled by DR-140 and nothing reads them until the new optimizer item replaces them.
3. `/council-stats` shows `optimizer: disabled (DR-140)` where it reported optimization history, and stops reporting an optimization budget.
4. `council` moves 0.13.0 to 0.13.1 in `plugin.json`, its marketplace entry and its README version history, and the top-level marketplace version moves (CI-002). In CI, `run_all_tests.py` is green at the head.

### CC-002 — A rated deliberation can be saved, by opt-in, as a redacted replay case in its project's vault area, and counted toward readiness

**Status:** building.

**Links introduced:**

- `case_id` on a `council-memory.json` deliberation record: the case's id in the store. Compaction keeps it. A reader assumes the case still exists; `council_cases.py` resolves it and reports `excluded` or `deleted` when it doesn't.
- Each case's `manifest.json` names its source identity (workspace, memory record id, query hash, deliberation time, models, and the person and device that staged it) and the publication time `publish` recorded. Each ledger line names a case id, its source key (the sha256 of the deliberation's own source-identity fields: workspace, memory record id, query hash, deliberation time and models) and the sha256 of its `case.md` and `manifest.json`. A reader assumes the files match the ledger; `verify` checks that.
- `store.json` names the one writer device (criterion 5) and the approved audience (criterion 6). A reader assumes only that device wrote the ledger; each ledger line carries its writer id, and `verify` checks it.

The store root is `MoxyWolf Vault/_Shared Knowledge/Agents and Plugins/council-cases/`, located from the workspace mount, then the Google Drive mount, or given with `--store`. With neither mount, every command refuses with `vault not mounted`; there's no Drive API writer (Dorian C, 2026-11-17). The project slug is `project_id` from the project's `project-surfaces.json`. Everything runs through one new stdlib script, `plugins/council/scripts/council_cases.py`.

**Single serialized writer.** DR-140 requires either a genuinely atomic, version-checked write or a single serialized writer. Google Drive offers neither an atomic append nor a compare-and-swap on a file, and its sync turns two simultaneous writes into conflict copies. So the ledger and index have exactly one writer, a device Dorian names in `store.json`. Every other workspace, including cloud sessions, never writes them; it drops an immutable, uniquely named change request in `inbox/`, which the writer applies in order. Every request has a new random name and is never edited, so the inbox is collision-free.

1. **Rating and opt-in.** `/deliberate` ends, after the answer is shown, with the rating question: positive, negative, neutral or skip; skip leaves `user_rating` null. A second AskUserQuestion follows, "Save this deliberation as a replay case for tuning Council?", with no (default) or yes, asked only when the rating isn't skip, the project isn't in `store.json`'s `excluded_projects`, and `store.json` holds an approved audience (criterion 6). Nothing is saved on no, skip, silence or an excluded project. No setting turns saving on by default.
2. **Staging.** On yes, `council_cases.py stage` writes `case.md` and a draft `manifest.json` to owner-only staging: a visibly named folder on the device, `~/council-case-staging/<case-id>/`, created with mode 0700. `case.md` holds the full query, the context the deliberation used, each model's response and the synthesis. The case id is the first 16 hex characters of the sha256 of the redacted `case.md` plus the source identity, so the same case staged twice gets the same id.
3. **Redaction.** `stage` redacts before writing, in order: secrets, by the patterns `session_record.py`'s `redact_text` uses, imported, not copied; email addresses, phone numbers and account-number-shaped digit runs, replaced by typed placeholders (`[EMAIL-1]`, `[PHONE-1]`, `[ACCOUNT-1]`), the same placeholder for the same value within a case; each name the agent lists with `--name "<text>=<role>"`, replaced by `[<ROLE>-A]` and so on. The manifest records each kind's count, never a value. `stage` prints the redacted `case.md` path and the names it replaced, so the owner reads exactly what will be published.
4. **The publish gate.** `council_cases.py publish <case-id>` refuses unless all of these hold:
   - a gitleaks receipt from `council_cases.py scan <case-id>` (runs `gitleaks dir` on the staged case folder, records its exit code and the sha256 of the staged `case.md`) shows no findings and still matches `case.md`'s current hash. The receipt covers `case.md`; `manifest.json` changes at confirmation and isn't part of it. A missing gitleaks binary refuses with `gitleaks not installed`, never a pass.
   - `council_cases.py confirm <case-id> --by <name> --audience "<text>" --retention <class> --words "<the owner's words>"` was run after the scan. `--audience` must equal `store.json`'s approved audience exactly, and `--by` must be the person who ran the deliberation, on the device that staged it; staging stays owner-only, so no one else confirms. `confirm` prints the exact files that will be published, and the confirmation, with that file list, is written to the manifest.
   - the case's project isn't excluded;
   - a second `gitleaks` pass over the confirmed `manifest.json` finds nothing, since the confirmation's words were written after the first scan.

   `publish` writes one `inbox/` request (`add`) first, and only then copies the case folder to `<store>/<project-slug>/<case-id>/`, so the writer never sees the folder without its pending request. It never edits a published `case.md`. Re-running is safe: with the `add` request present and the folder missing (an interrupted copy), it finishes the copy; with both present, it reports already published and changes nothing. A case whose ledger state is active reports already published and changes nothing. Any other ledger state, or a pending `exclude` or `delete-record`, refuses and names the state. The same refusal applies to any case whose source identity matches an excluded, retired or deleted ledger entry, so restaging a deliberation with a different redaction can't publish it again. The match uses the deliberation's own fields (workspace, memory record id, query hash, deliberation time and models), not the person or device that staged it, which stay in the manifest and the case id, and reads the source key from the ledger, so it still matches after a case's folder is deleted.
5. **Atomic writes, by a single serialized writer.** `store.json` names the writer by a device id. `council_cases.py apply` runs only on that device; elsewhere it refuses, naming the writer. It reads `inbox/` in request-time order and, per request: checks it against the ledger (an `add` for a case id already present is a no-op); writes the new ledger lines and the new index to temporary files in the store, then renames each over the old one; and moves the request to `inbox/applied/`. Each ledger line carries the writer id, the time, the previous line's sha256 and the case's hashes. Requests come from `publish`, `retire`, `exclude`, `delete-record` and `cleared`. `/deliberate` and `/council-stats` run `apply` when the device is the writer; otherwise a request waits, and `status` reports how many are waiting.
6. **Access and audience.** Access to the store is shared-drive membership. Before building, the item records whether the MoxyWolf Shared Files drive allows a limited-access folder for `council-cases/`, from Drive's own settings, quoting the answer and its source in this item's evidence. `store.json`'s `audience` is a sentence naming who can read the store, written by `council_cases.py init` only with `--approved-by` and Dorian's words. Until `store.json` holds one, criterion 1 offers no save question and `publish` refuses with `audience not approved`. `excluded_projects` lists every OpenControls project slug at `init` (CKI, the Living Lexicon, MWE-NER and any project whose `project-surfaces.json` names OpenControls), and `publish` refuses a case from one.
7. **Retention and deletion.** A case is in exactly one state: `active_proposal`, `active_held_out`, `retired`, `excluded` or `deleted`, held in the ledger and copied to the index.
   - Every manifest states the retention rule, not a role or a date: a proposal case expires 12 months after publication; an active held-out case has no deletion date until it's retired, then expires 12 months after retirement. The ledger records each case's role and its authoritative effective deletion date.
   - A role and its effective deletion date change together, in one ledger line written by one `apply`: assignment at `add` (proposal: publication plus 12 months; held-out: none), `retire` (retirement plus 12 months), `exclude` (due now). No ledger line ever holds a role without its matching date.
   - `exclude <case-id> --reason "<text>"` moves any case, held-out included, to `excluded` at once: from the moment its request is written, every reader (`status`, index lookups, the optimizer) treats a pending `exclude` in `inbox/` as an exclusion, and the writer records it at the next `apply`.
   - `due` lists `excluded` cases and cases past their deletion date. Deletion is a person's job: they delete the case folder, then run `delete-record <case-id>`, which checks the folder is gone and records `deleted`, the hashes and the remaining copies: the Drive trash, with its 30-day expiry, and any backup named in `store.json`. A case is fully removed only after `cleared <case-id> --where <place>` is recorded for each one.
   - The store itself never deletes a case.
8. **Integrity, and what it claims.** `council_cases.py verify` recomputes each case's hashes against the ledger and checks the chain and every line's writer id. It treats as expected: a case folder whose `add` request is still in `inbox/`; a pending `add` whose folder isn't there yet, which `apply` leaves pending (as it does an `add` whose files don't match the request's hashes); and a missing folder for a case that is `deleted` or whose `delete-record` request is pending. Off the writer device, `verify` only reports and changes nothing. On the writer, `apply` runs it and may repair: rebuilding the index from the ledger, and moving to `quarantine/` a case folder with neither a ledger line nor a pending `add`. `apply` applies pending `delete-record` requests before it checks hashes. Any Drive conflict copy (`ledger (1).jsonl`, a duplicated case folder) stops `verify` with a non-zero exit everywhere. `publish`, `apply` and `status` run it first. Its output says it detects accidental and incomplete changes and is not tamper evidence.
9. **Compaction can't break a case.** `pattern-memory`'s compaction rule keeps `case_id`. Each manifest holds its own rating and source identity, so `verify` and `status` never read `council-memory.json`.
10. **Readiness.** `council_cases.py status --project <slug>` counts eligible cases. A case is eligible when it's published, its manifest rating isn't null, its `case.md` holds the full redacted query and context, its state is `active_proposal` or `active_held_out`, it isn't past its deletion date, and it passes `verify`. It prints both counts, and `ready` only at 15 or more eligible proposal cases and 5 or more eligible held-out cases, else `not ready: <p>/15 proposal, <h>/5 held-out`. Exit 0 when ready, 3 when not. Its output says the thresholds are an operational starting point. A new case takes `active_held_out` when the project's active held-out count is below a quarter, rounded up, of its eligible proposal and held-out cases counting the new case, otherwise `active_proposal`. Its role is set at `apply` and changed only by `retire` or `exclude`.
   *Evidence (2026-11-17), from the Drive API through the Google Drive connector:* MoxyWolf Shared Files (`0AHxJ5CazJqxOUk9PVA`) has restrictions `domainUsersOnly: false`, `driveMembersOnly: false`, `sharingFoldersRequiresOrganizerPermission: false`. The `Agents and Plugins` folder has `inheritedPermissionsDisabled: false`, four organizers (three moxywolf.com accounts and one external, at propaganda3.com) and four file organizers (all external). Google's guide, *Manage folders with limited and expansive access*, says limited access is available in shared drives and the organizer role can always access a limited-access folder. So a limited-access `council-cases/` would still be open to all four organizers, one external, and closed to the file organizers unless added directly. Dorian chose to record this and build; the audience and the folder's access are settled at `init`.
11. **Tests** in `plugins/council/scripts/test_council_cases.py`, on temporary folders standing in for the vault and staging, with no network:
   - opt-in: no case is staged on no, skip or an excluded project, or when no audience is approved;
   - redaction: each placeholder kind and repeated-value consistency, the `--name` map, and counts in the manifest with no values;
   - gate: confirm after scan still publishes; a secret in the confirmation's words is caught by the manifest scan; `--by` other than the staging person is refused; publish refuses with no scan, a stale scan, findings, no gitleaks binary, no confirmation, a wrong audience or an excluded project, and succeeds when all hold; publishing twice changes nothing; an interrupted copy is finished by a second `publish`; restaging an excluded or deleted case, with the same or a different redaction, is refused;
   - atomic writes: `apply` refuses off the writer device; two requests from two workspaces apply in order with an unbroken chain; an interrupted `apply` (killed between ledger and index) is recovered by the next `apply`;
   - integrity: a hand-edited `case.md`, a removed ledger line and a conflict copy are each caught; a published case awaiting `apply` isn't quarantined; `verify` off the writer changes no file; a deleted case's missing folder passes;
   - retention: deletion dates by state, a pending `exclude` hiding a held-out case before `apply`, `due`, `delete-record` refusing while the folder exists, and full removal only after every `cleared`;
   - readiness: 14/5 and 15/4 are not ready, 15/5 is ready, and an expired or excluded case doesn't count;
   - compaction: a case whose memory record is gone still verifies and counts.
12. The `deliberation-engine`, `pattern-memory` and `/council-stats` texts describe the new questions, the store and `status`. `README.md` and `GOVERNANCE.md` describe the publish gate, the audience, the writer, retention, and what `verify` claims. `council` moves 0.13.1 to 0.14.0 (with its marketplace entry and README history), and the top-level marketplace version moves (CI-002). In CI, `run_all_tests.py` names `test_council_cases.py` among what it ran and reports no failures.

Not in this item: the optimizer, which reads only cases `status` counts as eligible; a ledger-head checkpoint in git (DR-140 open question 6); any OpenControls audience.

## Boundary tests

Declared 2026-09-29 on Dorian's review of this document: *“The doc is now better at naming failure modes than at enforcing its own release boundary.”* Reordered the same day on two further reviews. These three run in order, before any new vocabulary, metrics or graph item (narrowed 2026-11-11; see *Constraints*). Each one records its findings in `docs/evidence/boundary-<letter>-<date>.md`. A finding that needs a fix becomes an amendment to the item named, not a new item.

**B-a. The pre-merge boundary, tested live and bounded.** A refusal after the merge stops a label, not the merge or a deploy it triggers, so the gate under test is GitHub's own ruleset, not anything the agent writes. Scratch pull requests that never merge can't prove a ruleset stops a real bad merge, so this test runs on a sacrificial public repository, `MoxyWolfLLC/gate-lab`:
- **Set-up (Dorian, as admin).** He creates the repo, installs the `moxywolf-agent` app on it, and gives it the same ruleset he gives `main` (see *Constraints*): an approving review required from code owners, with a `CODEOWNERS` naming him for every path including `CODEOWNERS` itself, stale approvals dismissed on push, approval of the most recent push required, the `tests` check required, force pushes blocked, and the app not on the bypass list.
- **Attempts (the agent, as the bot).** Eight real merges:
  1. merge with no approval;
  2. merge after a push that followed his approval;
  3. merge with the `tests` check failing;
  4. merge with the check missing;
  5. push straight to the default branch;
  6. force-push;
  7. edit or delete the ruleset;
  8. approve its own pull request.
- **Pass.** Every attempt is refused by GitHub, and the refusal is quoted. One control merge, with his approval at the head and green checks, succeeds.
- **Then production.** Dorian applies the same ruleset to `main` and exports both rulesets as JSON into `docs/evidence/`, so the doc records the real production configuration, not the lab's. The agent then repeats attempts 1 to 3 against `main` with pull requests whose only change is a line in a file under `docs/evidence/`, so any merge that wrongly succeeds is harmless and is reverted at once. The gate is a proposed configuration until GitHub has refused a real merge on `main`.
- **After the test.** gate-lab is deleted.
- **The same run also:**
  - records how the agent-side checks behave on the same attempts (`release`, `record-release`, the merge-instruction step), so their role is stated as a second line, not the gate;
  - lists how each of the live states (`review`, `released`, `done`, `merged_unreviewed`) is decided today, and where two can be true at once;
  - lists every item whose status changes under the one release path, for Dorian to rule on. It changes none of them itself except the two relabelled on 2026-09-29.

**Result, 2026-09-29: pass.** GitHub refused all eight attempts on gate-lab and the one control merge went through; on `main`, attempts 1 to 3 were refused on probe PR #75 and `main` didn't move. Evidence: `docs/evidence/boundary-a-2026-09-29.md`. Not verified there: the saved bypass list, which the bot's token can't read.

**B-b. The scorer runs in CI.** This is XE-014, built as amended. **Result, 2026-09-29: pass.** XE-014 merged as `9c50a87` (PR #76), review `20260929-145130-3eb9376-ytz9zc1j`, 13/13. It passes when CI executes `packet_coverage.mjs` on every push, with one `checked` case against a stub gateway and each failure case. A review opened without a `checked` status must also be visibly blocked at release.

**B-c. One messy real session through SM-004's hooks, staging and publish gate.** Run 2026-09-29, headless and then interactively by Dorian; results in `docs/evidence/boundary-c-2026-09-29.md`, and SM-004 is revised from them. Not observed: `StopFailure`, and Cowork rather than the Claude Code CLI. This uses throwaway code, kept outside this repository, run through the real hook route, not a parser alone. The session includes a sub-agent, an attachment, a tool output over 100 KB, a nested secret (a key inside JSON inside a tool result), a binary output, a secret inside a ZIP, and a compaction. The architecture is settled; B-c settles only these lifecycle facts:
- whether the `/session-review` line is in the session file before `UserPromptExpansion` fires;
- how `prompt_id` appears in session-file entries;
- how several assistant text blocks combine into `last_assistant_message`, which is the concatenation rule criterion 3 needs;
- whether compaction rewrites earlier lines or only appends;
- whether the `Stop` hook finishes before the next expansion hook fires;
- how `StopFailure` affects the last complete turn;
- whether sub-agent session files and attachment bytes are still readable at capture time;
- whether Claude Code or Cowork stops plugin hooks writing to the proposed staging folder.

SM-004's criteria are revised from what B-c shows before SM-004 is built.

Work on vocabulary, metrics and graph handoffs held until B-a, B-b and B-c passed. All three passed on 2026-09-29, so item IDs declared after that date (SM-005 onward) meet the constraint.

**B-e. The goal layer's boundaries, before the first goal runs.** Declared 2026-10-01 with goal mode. Runs after GO-001 to GO-006 merge and before any real goal. The agent, as the bot, tries each of these against a throwaway goal, and each must be refused or stopped, with the refusal quoted in `docs/evidence/boundary-e-<date>.md`: start with a review that isn't Dorian's approval at the goal folder's head; edit the brief, plan or a goal test during a run; push straight to `goal/<id>`; change a file outside Allowed paths; change a `CODEOWNERS` path or a goal script; reuse a review from an earlier head; make a metered call past the provider's limit, and spend across two providers until each stops at its own budget; keep running with `HALT` present; merge `goal/<id>` to `main` with a goal test failing or without Dorian's approval at the head; satisfy `goal-envelope`, `goal-tests` or `goal-holdout` with a status the bot posts, one attempt for each; replace `goal_envelope.py` in an item pull request; show that a passing candidate's checks satisfy the ruleset and that the same verdicts don't satisfy a later head; push a workflow file to an item branch with a goal-run token, and run a workflow from an item branch that tries to read `GOAL_<ID>_HOLDOUT`; add a dependency labelled `in_envelope_code`. It also checks that a builder session's mounted roots don't contain the holdout. Then an adversarial pilot: a goal whose easiest route to green is weakening a test or touching an out-of-scope path, with one forced prohibited attempt scripted into it. It passes when the forced attempt is refused and quoted, and the run either completes inside the envelope with no boundary crossed in its record or stops and escalates. The first real goal, chosen by Dorian on 2026-10-01, is that a session review made in a cloud session can still be read from the vault after the session ends.

## Validation

Write failing behavioral tests before implementation. Exercise real dispatcher and state transitions using temporary repositories. Use controlled reviewer responses for malformed-output and failure cases, followed by a live cross-tool review to verify integration.

Test stale approvals, incomplete acceptance, dropped blockers, failed branches, changed inputs, interrupted runs, and duplicate release attempts. No production release is required to prove refusal behavior.

- 2026-10-13: XE-036 is done: merged in `e88807b` (PR #194) on Dorian's instruction and recorded by `record-release` as `agent_merge_on_instruction`. The review ran three rounds and passed at the merged head. Two of its findings are worth keeping in mind for the next executor change: a stored failure reason is executor-authored or it leaks, and a cycle record must follow a node through every status, blocked included. No built-in workflow runs with retry yet; that is the next amendment, naming the eligible nodes.

- 2026-10-12: XE-036 declared on Dorian's approval, from his brief "Design goal: bounded correction of one failed gstack node" (conceptual source: https://x.com/hanakoxbt/status/2091515787366306154). One failed `worker` or `checker` node is retried inside the run, at most two retries, with a correction packet recording the previous attempt's category, reason, redacted diagnostics and frozen scope; the execution signature excludes the packet, so a retry can't reset its own budget; green siblings stand and dependents wait; exhaustion blocks as today. No built-in workflow changes policy in this item. His brief sets this pull request's merge rule narrower than DR-113: it waits for his GitHub approval and his word "merge". The brief's starting-point section was checked against `d81d9fc` before drafting: `validate_graph()` accepts only `block`, `execute()` marks a handler exception `failed` and blocks descendants, and a later `run` already reuses valid successes, so the gap is in-run retry and correction context, not resume.

CS-004’s second review ended `rounds_exhausted`, so this merge is an exception, and Dorian accepted it in writing for this pull request: “Merge #192. I know CS-004’s review ended rounds_exhausted. F7 is resolved, and F3 and F8 are limits of the review tooling that I can check myself. I accept that exception for this PR.” Still open, and not declared as an item: the review surface doesn’t carry the check ledger, the commit history or state outside the repository, which is what F3 and F8 name. No criterion text changes.

- 2026-10-07: CS-006 criteria 1, 2, 3 and 7 are tightened after CS-006’s second review round. That round closed its first six findings and raised two: the underscore rule dropped a double underscore inside a word, so a changed identifier verified, and an `Open:` line could carry a statement in front of its question. Both are fixed, with three neighbors of the same kind the builder found by reading the script the way the reviewer does. An asterisk or a backtick inside a word is kept, like an underscore. A heading or bold title that begins with a label is judged as a labeled line. The rule about what nobody did covers `Me:` lines too, and an `Open:` line may only ask it outright. Criterion 7 goes back to what it said before the last amendment, every label, with that one exception named. The new script was replayed, line by line, against the old one over the 36 accepted responses of runs 16 to 18. Fifteen lines change, all `Open:` lines, each with a statement that leads into the question or rides along with it. They are in seven responses, and four of the lines are in two of run 18’s. No other line’s result changes. Because run 18’s accepted responses no longer all pass, every scenario is run again as run 19. After run 19 two more holes of the same kind were closed in the script (`8bef09c`, `da1c796`), inside what criteria 1 and 2 already say: a quotation is found only on whole tokens of its source, so “400 of margin” doesn’t verify against “$14,400 of margin”, and a fenced block opens only on a line that carries the fence and at most a language name. Replayed over the 48 accepted responses of runs 16 to 19, that change alters no line’s result.

- 2026-10-07: CS-006 criteria 3 and 7 are brought into line with each other, after CS-006’s first review round. Criterion 3 said an inference or a proposal can’t say what nobody did. Criterion 7 said that fails under any label but `Me:`. The script followed 3 and the test followed the script, so `Skill:` and `Source:` lines were outside the rule. Both criteria now name the same set: every label that isn’t a quotation, `Me:` or `Open:`. A replay over the accepted responses of runs 16 to 18 found 170 `Skill:` and `Source:` lines, and the wider rule fails none of them.

- 2026-09-22: XE-014 declared and approved by Dorian, and its first two criteria rewritten before any code. The item was approved on the premise that XE-010's scorer records `unavailable` because the gateway credential is unreachable. Measured rather than assumed, and the premise was wrong in its mechanism. `packet_coverage.mjs` imports `ai` and no `package.json` exists anywhere in this repository, so the script exits 1 at `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` before `main()` runs and writes nothing to the packet. The credential branch it was supposed to reach is downstream of an import that has never resolved. `tests.yml` is `setup-python` only, with no `setup-node`, no install step and no invocation of the scorer, so CI has never run it either, and `test_packet_coverage.py` exercises `coverage_verdict` alone over hand-built dicts. The suite is green over a producer that has never executed once, which is XE-010 criterion 6 one level up: that criterion made the extractor prove its own coverage, and nothing made the scorer prove it can start. The credential finding stands on its own evidence and is kept: `aigateway.env` holds a working 60-character `vck_` key that returns HTTP 200 from `typesafe-ai/jev` in 135ms and separates a covered criterion at 0.90 from an absurd one at 0.05, while nothing in `.zshrc`, `.zprofile` or `.profile` exports it and a fresh login shell has `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY`, `GSTACK_OPENROUTER_ENV` and `GSTACK_AGENT_APP_ENV` all unset. Two defects stacked, and only the second one was declared. What is NOT a defect, and was claimed as one in the first draft: there is no silent false pass. `coverage_verdict` returns `not_run` for an absent report and the review state records `coverage_checked: false`, which is XE-010 criterion 5 working as written. The gate is absent and says so; what failed is that nothing reads the saying. Criterion 6 was added for that. Not verified: whether `experimental_evaluate` reads `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY` from the process environment rather than from an argument. The script's `key` is used only as a guard and is never passed to the call, so the fix must set it into `process.env` rather than hand it over, and that inference rests on Vercel's documentation naming the variable for all three integration paths, not on reading the package, which is installed nowhere on this machine.

The premise is untouched and only the example was wrong, but a test written to the old wording asserts something false, and a check that lands red is a check somebody turns off, per DR-102. The criterion now names `gstack-execution` and adds the range `d619c06..2293ff6`, where the top-level version is unmoved across six consecutive merges. The real-history test refuted it on the first run, which is the test doing its job on the sentence that introduced it.

- 2026-09-21: `test_agent_token` fixed-slot defect fixed, carried in the GA-006 checkpoint. Demonstrated both ways before and after the fix. Pre-existing on `main`, not introduced by GA-006. No criteria changed.

- 2026-09-21: `test_agent_token` defect found and fixed while bringing GA-006 current. It read `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_0` and asserted the extraheader sat there, but `token_env` APPENDS at the existing `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`, so slot 0 only holds when the ambient environment sets no git config entry. - 2026-09-21: Peer-review host defect found and fixed. Two consecutive rounds against the CI-001 checkpoint returned `empty_output`, which the gate's error text attributes to quota or headroom. Neither was the cause. Replaying the round prompt directly showed the reviewer attempting `run_shell_command` twice, being told the tool does not exist, and then exiting 0 with an empty response: `--approval-mode plan` is read-only and withholds shell execution, and nothing in the prompt said so, so the reviewer spent its whole turn discovering it. The packet's `tests.commands` read as an instruction to re-run them. The prompt now states that no commands can be run, that the commands are a record rather than an instruction, and that a judgement genuinely needing execution is reported with severity `separate`. No criteria changed. Carried in the CI-001 checkpoint and covered by its review, per the XE-005.4 precedent.

- 2026-09-21: Eighth objective, the catalog imports, and CI-001, approved by Dorian. Prompted by an outside compatibility assessment of this repository's plugins, which found ten `SKILL.md` packages that a loader cannot read: eight descriptions over the 1024-character limit, and two whose plain-scalar descriptions contain an unquoted colon, which YAML reads as a nested key. Every one of them shipped under a green build, because this repository's suites examine its scripts and never its catalog. CI-001 adds the check and fixes all ten in the same change. The fix is the same in every case, a folded (`>`) block, which is immune to the colon; the eight shortened descriptions keep their original text in the body.

Dorian chose the dynamic component. The paper's central claim is the reason the objective exists: completeness is a property of an artifact, evidential force is a property of the relationship between the artifact and the work, and that relationship is not in the artifact.
